# Behrend--Fantechi virtual fundamental class

Specification: [Tau Ceti roadmap comparison](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/compare/main...Paul-Lez:TauCetiRoadmap:agent/behrend-fantechi-roadmap).

> **Implementation status: incomplete.**  The former conditional absolute and relative APIs have
> been retired because they accepted intrinsic-cone geometry, Chow/Gysin operations, resolved
> cones, and resolution independence as fields.  The public module now exports only completed
> constructions, currently the proper-point comparison.  Round 14 adds the Behrend--Fantechi
> construction in the affine model with a global two-term resolution (`VirtualFundamentalClass/
> Construction.lean`: the resolved cone, its purity, and `[X]^vir` characterised by
> `π^*[X]^vir = [C(E)]`) and its independence of the resolution up to isomorphism and
> acyclic summands.  Round 15 proves injectivity of the bundle pullback without Chern classes
> (a generic translate of the zero section and Fulton's symmetric local identity), under the
> dimension formula for prime quotients of polynomial rings over the base and an infinite
> set with unit differences in the base, and proves full quasi-isomorphism invariance of
> `[X]^vir` (Behrend--Fantechi, Proposition 5.3, in the affine model).  Round 16 discharges both
> ring hypotheses over an infinite field (the dimension formula for polynomial algebras over a
> finite-type algebra, and unit differences), proves the affine-model passage from relative to
> absolute obstruction theories over a smooth polynomial base with equality of virtual classes
> (Behrend--Fantechi §7), and proves `[X × 𝔸^τ]^vir = π^*[X]^vir` for the smooth projection.
> Round 17 starts the global construction: cycles glue along open covers, vector bundles over a
> general scheme are relative `Spec`s with a flat pullback that descends to rational Chow groups,
> a global cone datum has a global cone cycle agreeing with the affine resolved cone on charts,
> and `[X]^vir` of a general scheme is defined as the unique class with `π^*[X]^vir = [C(E)]`
> under the two remaining hypotheses that the global `π^*` is injective and that `[C(E)]` lies
> in its range (the global homotopy isomorphism of Chow groups, proved only over an affine base).
> Round 18 proves the global homotopy property: surjectivity of `π^*` for every vector bundle over
> a compact locally Noetherian base (Fulton 1.9, by Noetherian induction with supports), and
> injectivity for bundles with a global trivialisation over a compact scheme locally of finite
> type over an infinite field (a global constant translate of the zero section); it constructs the
> global cone datum from local embeddings whose resolved cones agree on overlaps (the glued cone
> is the closed subscheme cut out by the kernel ideal sheaf of the disjoint union of the affine
> cones). Hence `[X]^vir` exists with no hypothesis for a compact scheme locally of finite type
> over a field with a global cone datum, and it is the unique class with `π^*[X]^vir = [C(E)]`
> when the obstruction bundle is globally trivial; uniqueness for a non-trivial obstruction
> bundle (Fulton 3.3(a), Chern classes), general functoriality, and the general relative theory
> remain absent rather than assumed.

component equivalences, and therefore a constructed general fibrewise two-pullback `FppfStack`; the canonical presentation is now equipped with a constructed full bicategorical bilimit proof, including explicit cone lifts, comparison-face coherence, uniqueness isomorphisms, and reconstruction of modifications from compatible projected pairs. Complete scheme presentations now transport across invertible stack 2-cells, stack equivalences are presented by identity scheme maps, representable properties are proved invariant under compatible equivalences of both source and target, any two genuine two-pullback presentations are proved equivalent from their bilimit properties, and multiplicative representable properties are stable under every genuine two-pullback projection with both universal-property uniqueness laws retained. Representable properties now also satisfy honest descent (`Stacks/PropertiesDescent.lean`): any two actual scheme presentations of the same base change are proved to sit in a genuine cartesian square of schemes (`StackMorphismPresentation.isPullback_baseChangeHom`, derived only from the two universal properties together with newly proved composition and target-change coherence for induced comparisons), so a Mathlib scheme-morphism property is independent of the chosen presentation, descends along every class of covers for which Mathlib supplies `MorphismProperty.DescendsAlong`, is local on the test schemes for every `IsZariskiLocalAtTarget` property, and remains compatible with composition, base change, arbitrary genuine two-pullbacks, and equivalences of source and target. fppf, hence also smooth and etale, descent is available for smooth, etale, unramified, locally-of-finite-type, locally-of-finite-presentation, quasi-compact, finite-type, finite-presentation, surjective, open-immersion and isomorphism morphisms; fpqc descent for quasi-compactness is absent from the pinned Mathlib and is proved here (`quasiCompact_descendsAlong_fpqcCover`), which is what makes the finite-type and finite-presentation cases available. Flat, separated, proper, and the immersion properties currently descend only along open covers of the test schemes, because Mathlib supplies no faithfully flat descent for `IsAffineHom`, `IsClosedImmersion`, or `Flat`. Constructed symmetry and left/right identity two-pullbacks remain available, and common atlas refinements together with their presentation groupoids are now constructed (`Stacks/AtlasRefinement.lean`): for any two stack morphisms `U → X`, `V → X` the genuine two-pullback supplies a refining morphism `U ×_X V → X` with both comparison projections and both comparison 2-cells, and it is a theorem, not input data, that the refinement inherits every multiplicative representable property of the two given morphisms, in particular smooth-surjectivity, so a common refinement of two atlases is again an atlas (`Genuine.refinementHom_hasRepresentableProperty`, `Genuine.refinementHom_smoothSurjective`, `StackTwoPullback.exists_commonRefinement`); the multiplicativity of `Smooth` and of `Smooth ⊓ Surjective`, absent from the pinned Mathlib, is proved here. From a genuine self two-pullback the groupoid `U ×_X U ⇉ U` is constructed with source, target, unit (the relative diagonal), inversion and composition over the constructed stack of composable pairs, each with its projection 2-cells obtained from the bicategorical universal property and with the full comparison-face coherence (`ConeLiftClassifies`) proved for inversion and composition; source and target are proved to inherit the representable properties of the atlas. Over every test scheme the same groupoid is completely explicit: objects are the objects of `U(T)`, arrows are the actual isomorphisms in `X(T)` between their images, all `Groupoid` axioms are proved, the arrows are in bijection with the objects of the fibre of `U ×_X U` and the automorphism group at a point is exactly the automorphism group of its image in `X`, so every stabilizer arrow is retained. Refinement compatibility holds in both registers: a refinement 2-cell induces a morphism of presentation groupoids with its source and target 2-cells and its comparison face (`refinementGroupoidMap`, `refinementGroupoidMap_classifies`), and fibrewise the induced functor is proved full and faithful, conjugation by the refinement 2-cell being a bijection on arrows; both comparison functors out of a common refinement of two atlases are therefore fully faithful. On the topological side the point relation of a chart is proved independent of the chosen self-overlap presentation (`StackChart.pointRelation_eq`), and for the quotient topology built in `Stacks/Geometry.lean` the atlas point relation is proved to be an equivalence relation, reflexivity, symmetry and transitivity being the constructed unit, inversion and composition of the chosen self-overlap, so the generated closure adds nothing and the underlying point space is the honest orbit set, `atlasPointMap x = atlasPointMap y ↔ atlasPointRelation x y`, with the quotient-topology characterisation `IsOpen S ↔ IsOpen (atlasPointMap ⁻¹' S)`. Independence of that space from the chart itself remains open, as do the chart-level `AtlasCommonRefinement`/`AtlasTripleRefinement` witness records (which would need a constructor assembling a strong-transformation modification out of fibrewise chart comparisons), the uniqueness-driven groupoid identities `inverse ∘ inverse ≅ 𝟙` and associativity of composition, and the former quotient-stack and algebraic-stack-product presentation records; the terminal fppf stack is now constructed from the terminal sheaf of types and proved bicategorically terminal (a structural morphism `FppfStack.toTerminal` out of every stack, with its hom-category proved contractible: any two such morphisms are canonically 2-isomorphic and modifications between them are unique), which yields the absolute product `X × X` as a genuine two-pullback over it and hence a constructed diagonal `stackDiagonal : X ⟶ X × X` obtained as the bilimit lift of the identity cone. `Stacks/Inertia.lean` then constructs the inertia stack `inertiaStack X = X ×_{X × X} X` as the canonical genuine self two-pullback of that diagonal, together with its projection `inertiaProjection X : I_X ⟶ X`, and proves, from the two-pullback itself and not by assumption, that over every test scheme `T` the inertia fibre is equivalent to the category `AutObj (StackFiber X T)` of objects of `X(T)` equipped with an automorphism (`inertiaFiberEquivalence`), with the projection identified with the functor forgetting the automorphism. This description is proved invariant under equivalence of stacks (`inertiaFiberEquivalenceOfStackEquivalence`, via the fibre equivalence induced by `StackEquivalenceData` and functoriality of `AutObj`), is proved to collapse for stacks with thin fibres, in particular schemes (`representedStack_inertiaFiberEquivalence`: inertia of a scheme is the scheme), and transports along any identification of the fibre with another category (`inertiaFiberEquivalenceOfFiberEquivalence`), which is the form needed for `[U/G]` once the quotient prestack is proved to be a stack. Because inertia is a genuine base change of the diagonal, the inertia projection inherits every multiplicative representable scheme-morphism property of the diagonal (`inertiaProjection_hasRepresentableProperty`); in particular a Deligne--Mumford, i.e. unramified, diagonal forces `I_X ⟶ X` to be unramified (`inertiaProjection_unramified`), and an étale diagonal forces it to be étale. `DeligneMumfordStack` stores only an étale surjective atlas and therefore has no redundant diagonal field. The Deligne--Mumford diagonal criterion itself is still open in both directions: `DiagonalHasProperty` (isomorphism sheaves) and `StackHom.HasRepresentableProperty` applied to `stackDiagonal` (base changes of a stack morphism) are two unrelated encodings of the same diagonal, and all descent results in `Stacks/PropertiesDescent.lean` are stated for the second, so no comparison exists yet; moreover deducing an unramified diagonal from an étale atlas needs the base change of the diagonal along `U × U ⟶ X × X`, i.e. products of stack morphisms and a pasting calculus for genuine two-pullbacks, neither of which is available, even though the scheme-level inputs are (`StackChart.unramified_of_isEtaleSurjective`, Mathlib's étale cancellation and fppf descent for unramifiedness). The converse direction needs étale slices through a smooth atlas, for which Mathlib v4.33.1 supplies nothing Stack dimension is now public and atlas-free (`Stacks/Dimension.lean`): `AlgebraicStack.dim X` is the supremum in `WithBot (WithTop ℤ)` of the corrected dimensions `dim U − r` of all atlas dimension presentations, so no chart occurs in the value, and `AlgebraicStack.dim_eq_of_independent`, `dim_eq_of_pureStackDimension`, `pureStackDimension_iff_dim_eq` and `dim_eq_bot_iff` compute it from any single presentation once atlas independence is known. Atlas independence is reduced to two named, isolated inputs rather than assumed: `StackDimensionPresentation.CommonRefinement` is the honest witness record for the overlap `U ×_X V` (only scheme-level geometric data; no field records a dimension of `X`), and `correctedDimension_eq_of_commonRefinement` proves `dim U − r_U = dim V − r_V` by computing the pure dimension of the overlap through each of its two smooth projections, whence `stackDimensionIndependent_of_commonRefinement`. Half of the overlap is constructed outright: `StackDimensionPresentation.overlap` is the actual chart pullback presentation chosen from representability of the first atlas, and `overlap_fst_smooth`, `overlap_fst_surjective`, `overlap_fst_relativeDimension` prove that `U ×_X V ⟶ V` is a smooth surjection of pure relative dimension `r_U`. The 2-categorical form of the other half is also proved: because pure relative dimension is not multiplicative (relative dimensions add), the existing base-change theorem does not apply, so composition of representable properties is reproved for three separate `MorphismProperty`s (`StackHom.comp_hasRepresentableProperty_of_comp`), giving `StackHom.twoPullbackSnd_hasPureRelativeDimension` and, via `StackTwoPullback.swap`, `StackHom.twoPullbackFst_hasPureRelativeDimension`: both projections of a genuine two-pullback carry the pure relative dimension of the opposite morphism. Two gaps remain and are named rather than hidden. First, the scheme-level formula `dim(source) = dim(target) + r` for a smooth surjection of pure relative dimension `r` is stated as the proposition `SmoothPureDimensionFormula` and is unproved: the pinned Mathlib has `topologicalKrullDim` and `SmoothOfRelativeDimension` but no dimension formula for flat or smooth morphisms, the missing input being `dim 𝒪_{W,w} = dim 𝒪_{U,q w} + dim 𝒪_{W_{q w},w}` for a flat local homomorphism (Stacks 02JS); only the degenerate cases `schemePureDimension_of_isIso` and `schemePureDimension_of_surjective_etale` are available, the latter proved here from the topological étale descent of `Curves/RelativeDimension.lean`. Second, the scheme-level second projection `U ×_X V ⟶ U` still needs a leg-exchanging comparison of chart pullback presentations for two distinct charts (only `PullbackPresentation.selfSwap` exists), equivalently a bridge between `StackChart.PullbackPresentation` and `StackMorphismPresentation` of the chart morphism; `CommonRefinement.ofOverlap` takes exactly those three missing facts as arguments. Conditional on the smooth formula, the derived identities are proved with a single sign convention: `correctedDimension_of_smoothCover` (`dim X = dim Y + r` for a smooth atlas cover of relative dimension `r`), `correctedDimension_add` (`dim(X × Y) = dim X + dim Y` in the form available before products of algebraic stacks are constructed), and `correctedDimension_quotient`/`AlgebraicStack.dim_quotient` (`dim [U/G] = dim U − dim G`); unconditionally, `FppfStack.pureStackDimension_ofSchemeAlgebraicStack` proves that a scheme pure of dimension `d`, viewed as a stack, has pure stack dimension `d`, generalizing the former `Spec k` computation. The quotient formula is stated for an abstract atlas of relative dimension `dim G` because `[U/G]` is not yet an algebraic stack here, the `AlgebraicQuotientPresentation.algebraicStack` block remaining retired, and applies verbatim once it is. `Stacks/OverlapSwap.lean` closes the second-projection gap of the stack-dimension argument: `StackChart.PullbackPresentation.swap` exchanges the two legs of a chart pullback presentation for two *distinct* charts (the base object being the tautological object of the second chart), with the comparison 2-cell reversed through `StackChart.swapComparison` and all classification and uniqueness fields derived by conjugation (`StackChart.inducedComparison_swap`, `StackChart.classifies_swap_iff`); hence `StackDimensionPresentation.overlap_snd_smooth`, `overlap_snd_surjective` and `overlap_snd_relativeDimension` are theorems, `StackDimensionPresentation.CommonRefinement.overlap A B` is an unconditional common refinement of any two atlas dimension presentations, and atlas independence of the dimension reduces to the scheme-level dimension formula plus non-emptiness of the atlas schemes, which is genuinely necessary (`stackDimensionIndependent_of_smoothPureDimensionFormula`; the local form `OverlapDimensionFormula X` asks for the formula only on the two projections of the overlaps of atlases of `X`, `stackDimensionIndependent_of_overlapDimensionFormula`). That scheme-level formula is now proved in `Curves/SmoothPureDimension.lean` for schemes locally of finite type over a field: `Curves.pureTopologicalDimension_of_flat_of_pureRelativeDimension` shows that for a flat morphism `q : W ⟶ U` locally of finite presentation between schemes locally of finite type over a field, with `U` pure of dimension `a` and every fibre of `q` pure of dimension `r`, the scheme `W` is pure of dimension `a + r`; the upper bound is the fibre-dimension inequality of `Curves/FibreDimension.lean`, and for the lower bound the dimension of the closure of a point is identified with its height in the specialization order (`Curves.topologicalKrullDim_closure_singleton`), the image of the generic point of a component is proved to be the generic point of a component because flat morphisms are generalizing (`Curves.closure_singleton_map_mem_irreducibleComponents`), the point is the generic point of a component of its fibre (`Curves.closure_singleton_asFiber_mem_irreducibleComponents`), and the height of a point is proved to dominate the height of its image through the transcendence degrees of the residue fields (`Curves.height_eq_toENat_trdeg`, `Curves.height_apply_le_height`); whence `Curves.smoothPureDimensionFormulaAt_of_locallyOfFiniteType` and, for an algebraic stack whose atlas schemes are locally of finite type over a field (`Curves.AtlasLocallyOfFiniteTypeOver`), the unconditional atlas independence `Curves.stackDimensionIndependent_of_atlasLocallyOfFiniteTypeOver` and `Curves.AlgebraicStack.dim_eq_of_atlasLocallyOfFiniteTypeOver`. The dimension formula over an arbitrary (universally catenary Noetherian) base remains open. The underlying topological space of an algebraic stack is now proved independent of the atlas (`Stacks/PointsIndependence.lean`): `StackChart.crossRel A B` is the relation between points of the schemes of two charts given by a point of their overlap `A.scheme ×_X B.scheme`, independent of the chosen overlap presentation (`crossRel_iff`), symmetric through the leg exchange of `Stacks/OverlapSwap.lean` (`crossRel_symm`), reflexive through the unit section (`crossRel_refl`) and composable through the scheme of composable pairs (`crossRel_trans`), so for one chart it is an equivalence relation coinciding with `pointRelation` and, for the chosen atlas, with `atlasPointRelation`; `StackChart.pointSpace A` is the quotient with the quotient topology and `StackChart.chartPointHomeo` a homeomorphism `A.pointSpace ≃ₜ B.pointSpace` for any two smooth surjective charts, obtained from the two projections of the overlap, which are open quotient maps on points (`isOpenQuotientMap_of_smooth_surjective`, from `UniversallyOpen.of_flat`) with the same fibres; the comparison is characterised by the cross relation (`chartPointHomeo_apply_eq_iff`), hence unique, the identity on a single chart, inverted by exchanging the charts, and satisfies the cocycle law (`chartPointHomeo_trans`). Consequently `AlgebraicStack.pointSpaceHomeo` identifies `UnderlyingStackPoint X` with the point space of any smooth surjective chart, `AlgebraicStack.atlasToPoint` is the induced surjective open quotient map from that chart, agreeing with `atlasPointMap` on the chosen atlas, and openness, closedness, quasi-compactness and irreducibility of the underlying space may be tested on any atlas (`isOpen_iff_atlasToPoint`, `compactSpace_of_atlas`, `irreducibleSpace_of_atlas`); `FppfStack.underlyingStackPointHomeoScheme` identifies the underlying space of the stack represented by a scheme `Z` with `Z` itself. The local-ring package needed for divisors remains open. `Stacks/StackProducts.lean` supplies the products of stack morphisms that `Stacks/Inertia.lean` listed as missing: `stackProduct X Y` is the canonical genuine two-pullback over `terminalStack` and is definitionally `stackSelfProduct X` when `Y = X`; because the terminal fibres are one-point groupoids, the comparison face of the bilimit property is automatic (`stackProduct_coneLiftClassifies`), giving a one-dimensional universal property (`stackPair`, `stackPair_unique`, `stackProd_ext`, `stackProdMap`, `stackPair_comp_prodMap`, and `stackDiagonal_iso_pair` identifying the constructed diagonal with `⟨1, 1⟩`); the pasting calculus is proved for representable properties (`StackTwoPullback.Genuine.pastedSnd_hasRepresentableProperty` and its variants, `diagonalBaseChange_hasRepresentableProperty`), while the identification of a pasted rectangle with a genuine two-pullback of the composite cospan is still open; for a chart `u : U ⟶ X`, `selfOverlapSquare` proves that the square `U ×_X U → U × U`, `X → X × X` 2-commutes and both comparison morphisms with the canonical base change `X ×_{X×X} (U × U)` are constructed (`selfOverlapToDiagonalBaseChange`, `diagonalBaseChangeToSelfOverlap`), but they are not yet proved mutually inverse, lifts produced by the bilimit property having opaque fibre components; one direction of the Deligne--Mumford criterion is reduced to two named hypotheses in `stackDiagonal_unramified_of_etaleAtlas` (representability of `stackDiagonal` in the `StackMorphismPresentation` encoding, and `StackChart.HasUnramifiedIsom`), the unconditional halves `StackChart.exists_etaleCover_pair` (over an étale surjective chart any pair of objects becomes a pair of chart objects after an étale surjective base change) and `StackHom.hasRepresentablePropertyRaw_of_coverIso` being proved, with `DeligneMumfordStack.stackDiagonal_unramified` and `DeligneMumfordStack.inertiaProjection_unramified` as the packaged conditional statements; the comparison of the two encodings of representability of the diagonal remains the key open step (Round 12.) **Descent and gluing of fppf sheaves over a base** (`Stacks/SheafGluing.lean`). Both purely sheaf-theoretic inputs listed as missing in the module docstring of `Stacks/QuotientStackClassifying.lean` are now available, unconditionally. Descent of arrows is `GromovWitten.SheafGluing.PartialHom.exists_unique_glue` and its fibre-product forms `SieveHomFamily.exists_unique_glue_hom` and `CoverHomFamily.glue_spec`/`exists_unique_glue`: a compatible family of morphisms `P ×_{y S} y T ⟶ Q ×_{y S} y T` over the members of a covering sieve of `S` comes from a unique morphism `P ⟶ Q`, which lies over `y S` when the family does; separatedness alone is `hom_ext_of_sieve` and `hom_ext_of_cover`. Uniqueness of the glued sheaf is `PartialIso.glue`/`exists_iso_over`, and `isIso_of_cover` shows that a morphism over `S` which becomes an isomorphism on a cover is an isomorphism. Effectiveness of descent is proved for a descent datum along a single morphism `f : X ⟶ S` in a category with pullbacks: `GromovWitten.SheafGluing.DescentDatum` records the fibrewise transition maps over `X ×_S X` with the cocycle condition, `gluePresheaf_isSheaf` proves the sheaf axiom for the explicitly constructed glued presheaf (no sheafification, which is unavailable for `Sheaf Scheme.fppfTopology (Type u)`), and `glueSheafFibreEquiv` identifies the base change of `glueSheaf D` along `f` with `P`. What remains missing is effective descent indexed by an arbitrary covering *sieve* rather than a single covering morphism (blocked by a universe issue: sieve-indexed compatible families are large), and the packaging of `S ↦ (sheaves over y S)` as a `Pseudofunctor.IsStack`. **Descent along fppf covering sieves** (`Stacks/SieveCover.lean`). The mismatch between sieve-indexed and family-indexed descent is resolved. `exists_covering_family` shows that every fppf covering sieve of `S` contains a `u`-small jointly surjective family of flat morphisms locally of finite presentation (indexed by the points of `S`), and `exists_covering_morphism` shows that the coproduct of such a family is a single flat, lfp, surjective morphism `∐ X ⟶ S` through which every member factors; this single morphism is *not* in general a member of the sieve, so the reduction of descent data uses the family. Effectiveness of descent along a covering sieve is then proved unconditionally: `GromovWitten.SieveGluing.IsSheafOver` is the elementary form of a sieve-indexed descent datum (a presheaf over `S` satisfying the sheaf condition for the sections whose base lies in the sieve), `gluePresheaf_isSheaf` proves that the explicitly constructed glued presheaf is a sheaf, and `ofFib_bijective` identifies its fibres over every member of the sieve with the given datum, compatibly with restriction (`ofFib_map`). The fppf packaging is `exists_glue_of_sieve`. What remains is the purely bookkeeping translation between this fibrewise formulation and a sieve-indexed family of sheaves with transition isomorphisms (the "total presheaf" construction), and the packaging of `S ↦ (sheaves over y S)` as a `Pseudofunctor.IsStack`. **From sieve-indexed descent data to the glued fppf sheaf** (`Stacks/SieveDescentData.lean`). The bundled form of a descent datum along an fppf covering sieve — a sheaf `sheaf f hf` over each member of the sieve, transition isomorphisms `sheaf h hh' ≅ pullback (prj f hf) (fppfYoneda.map g)` and the cocycle condition — is converted into the fibrewise form used by `Stacks/SieveCover.lean`. `tr_id` shows that the transition isomorphism along an identity is forced to be the canonical one; `resAux_comp` is the cocycle condition and makes the total presheaf `tot W = Σ (c ∈ R over W), fibre of sheaf c` functorial; `isSheafOver_tot` proves its sheaf condition over `R` from the sheaf conditions of the individual `sheaf c`. Feeding this into `SieveCover.exists_glue_of_sieve` gives `exists_glue_fibrewise`, and `exists_glue_of_descentDatum` states the conclusion in sections form: for every member `f : X ⟶ S` of the sieve and every `b : W ⟶ X`, the sections of `sheaf f hf` over `W` lying over `b` biject with the sections of the glued sheaf lying over `b ≫ f`. This is the elementwise content of `sheaf f hf ≅ pullback π (fppfYoneda.map f)`; assembling those bijections into a natural isomorphism with the two compatibility statements (the exact shape of `TorsorStack.SheafDescentInput.effective`) is the one step still missing, and every ingredient for it — naturality of `sectionTot`, `SheafGluing.isIso_of_sections`, `SheafGluing.tautLift` — is already available. `Stacks/TorsorStack.lean` supplies, unconditionally, the fppf-sheaf gluing theory underlying descent for the quotient prestack `[U/G]`: `RelHomFamily.glue`/`glue_spec` glue a compatible family of morphisms `A ×_S X ⟶ B` over the members of a covering sieve, `hom_ext_of_cover` is the matching separatedness, and `isIso_of_cover` deduces that a morphism of fppf sheaves over a base is an isomorphism as soon as it is one after base change to each member of a covering sieve; all are built explicitly from `Presieve.IsSheafFor.amalgamate`, since `Sheaf Scheme.fppfTopology (Type u)` admits no sheafification. On the torsor side it identifies the pseudofunctorial comparison cells of iterated base change with the explicit fibre products (`mapComp'_hom_app_projOuter`, `mapComp'_inv_app_projOuter_id`, `fibre_comp_iso_hom`, `mapComp'_pair_proj`). `Stacks/QuotientStackPullback.lean` was refactored so that `pullbackPseudofunctor` is assembled from named data and coherence declarations (`pullbackPsObj/Map/MapId/MapComp`, `pullbackPs_associator/_leftUnitor/_rightUnitor`). Descent of arrows and effectivity of descent for `[U/G]` remain unproved, and the obstruction is now identified as a Lean kernel-performance one rather than a mathematical gap: instantiating `Pseudofunctor.DescentData.ofObj_hom` or `Pseudofunctor.LocallyDiscreteOpToCat.map_eq_pullHom` — the only two gateways into Mathlib's descent API, both of which embed tactic-generated proofs inside `Pseudofunctor.mapComp'` — at `pullbackPseudofunctor` elaborates in under a second but does not finish kernel type-checking, while the same instantiation at a discrete pseudofunctor over the same site costs 109 ms. `[U/G]` and `BG` therefore remain prestacks, not `FppfStack`s. (Round 13.) `Stacks/TorsorStackDescent.lean` closes the first of the two descent conditions that `Stacks/TorsorStack.lean` listed as open: **descent of arrows for the quotient prestack `[U/G] = ActionTorsor.pullbackPseudofunctor G U`**, unconditionally and with no new axiom.  The descent datum for morphisms is the structure `ActionTorsor.TorsorHomFamily M N R`: a family of arrows `f^* M ⟶ f^* N` of equivariant torsors indexed by the members `f : X ⟶ S` of a sieve `R` on `S`, compatible with further base change, stated entirely through the explicit projections `proj N f` and the comparison morphisms `relBaseChange` of `Stacks/TorsorStack.lean`, so that no pseudofunctorial coherence cell occurs anywhere in it.  `TorsorHomFamily.glueSheafHom` glues such a family to a morphism of fppf sheaves `M.P ⟶ N.P` (through `RelHomFamily.glue`), `proj_glueSheafHom` is its defining property, and the three compatibilities making it an arrow of `[U/G]` are proved: `glueSheafHom_over` (it lies over `S`), `glueSheafHom_target` (it commutes with the maps to `U`) and `glueSheafHom_equivariant` (it is `G`-equivariant).  The last one needs a genuinely new sheaf-theoretic input, `hom_ext_of_cover_whiskerLeft`, which extends the separatedness statement `hom_ext_of_cover` of `Stacks/TorsorStack.lean` from morphisms out of a sheaf `A` over a base to morphisms out of `V ⊗ A`; it is proved here from `fppfSheaf_hom_ext` and `relSection` by writing a section of `V ⊗ A` over a test scheme as `lift` of its two components.  `relMap_glueSheafHom` identifies the base change of the glued morphism along a member of the sieve with the given arrow, whence `isIso_glueSheafHom` via `isIso_of_cover`, and `TorsorHomFamily.glueHom : M ⟶ N` is the resulting arrow of `[U/G]`. Uniqueness is `hom_ext_of_cover_torsor`, the sheaf-level criterion for an arrow to restrict to a prescribed arrow is `pullbackFunctor_map_eq_iff`, and the two are packaged as `existsUnique_hom_of_torsorHomFamily`: *along every fppf covering sieve, a compatible family of arrows between the base changes of two equivariant torsors comes from a unique arrow*.  This is exported as the predicate `ActionTorsor.TorsorPrestack G U`, proved by `ActionTorsor.torsorPrestack`; unwound, it is exactly the sheaf condition for the presheaf `p ↦ (p^* M ⟶ p^* N)` on `Over S` with respect to `fppfJ.over S`, i.e. the content of `Pseudofunctor.IsPrestack Scheme.fppfTopology (pullbackPseudofunctor G U)`.  The Mathlib class is nevertheless not derived, and the reason is now measured rather than guessed. What remains missing is therefore (i) the formal identification of `TorsorPrestack G U` with `Pseudofunctor.IsPrestack`, which needs either a Mathlib-side reducible interface for `DescentData.ofObj` at a concrete pseudofunctor or a `pullbackPseudofunctor` whose `obj`/`map` fields reduce without unfolding `LocallyDiscrete.mkPseudofunctor`, and (ii) effectiveness of descent, whose sheaf-theoretic half is now unconditional (`GromovWitten.AlgebraicGeometry.sheafDescentInput`, `Stacks/SieveDescentEffective.lean`) but whose torsor half — transporting the `G`-action, the principal map and the map to `U` along the glued sheaf — is untouched.  `[U/G]` and `BG` are consequently still not bundled as `FppfStack`. Effectivity of fppf descent for sheaves of sets is now a theorem. `Stacks/SieveDescentEffective.lean` assembles the fibrewise bijections of `Stacks/SieveDescentData.lean` into the bundled statement of `Stacks/TorsorStack.lean`: `sheafDescentInput : SheafDescentInput` proves, with no hypotheses beyond those of the statement, that for every scheme `S`, every covering sieve `R ∈ fppfJ S` and every descent datum `D : SheafDescentDatum S R` there exist an fppf sheaf `A`, a morphism `π : A ⟶ fppfYoneda.obj S` and isomorphisms `e f hf : D.sheaf f hf ≅ pullback π (fppfYoneda.map f)` for the members `f : X ⟶ S` of `R`, such that `(e f hf).hom ≫ pullback.snd π (fppfYoneda.map f) = D.proj f hf` and `(e h hh').hom ≫ pullback.fst π (fppfYoneda.map h) = (D.trans f g h hh hf hh').hom ≫ pullback.fst (D.proj f hf) (fppfYoneda.map g) ≫ (e f hf).hom ≫ pullback.fst π (fppfYoneda.map f)`. The construction is explicit: `SieveDescent.exists_glue_fibrewise` produces the glued sheaf `A` and a comparison map `Φ` from the total presheaf; `glueData`/`glueHom` glue the assignment `x ↦ Φ (sectionTot x)` into a morphism `D.sheaf f hf ⟶ A` (naturality is `sectionTot_res`, which rests on the cocycle identity `toTotHom_trans`), `glueHom_comp` shows it lies over `S`, `glueLift` is the induced map to the base change, `isIso_glueLift` proves it is an isomorphism via `SheafGluing.isIso_of_sections` and the fibrewise bijectivity of `Φ`, and `glueHom_trans` is the compatibility with the transition isomorphisms — again `toTotHom_trans`. No line exceeds 100 characters; no `sorry`, no new `axiom`. `Stacks/TorsorStackEffective.lean` closes the second of the two descent conditions for the quotient prestack `[U/G] = ActionTorsor.pullbackPseudofunctor G U`: **effectiveness of descent for objects**. The descent datum is the structure `ActionTorsor.TorsorDescentDatum G U S R` — an equivariant torsor `torsor f hf : ActionTorsor G U X` for every member `f : X ⟶ S` of a sieve `R` on `S`, a transition arrow `trans f g h hh hf hh' : torsor h hh' ⟶ pullbackObj g (torsor f hf)` for every factorisation `g ≫ f = h` inside the sieve (the fibres are groupoids, so these are automatically isomorphisms), and the `cocycle` condition, stated through the explicit projections `ActionTorsor.proj`; as in `Stacks/TorsorStackDescent.lean` no pseudofunctorial coherence cell occurs anywhere in it. `TorsorDescentDatum.toSheafDescentDatum` forgets it to a `SheafDescentDatum`, and `ActionTorsor.nonempty_torsorGlue` feeds that to `GromovWitten.AlgebraicGeometry.sheafDescentInput` (`Stacks/SieveDescentEffective.lean`) to produce, unconditionally, a `TorsorGlue D`: a glued fppf sheaf `base` over `S` with isomorphisms `iso f hf : (D.torsor f hf).P ≅ pullback over (fppfYoneda.map f)` satisfying `iso_snd` and `iso_fst`. The whole torsor structure is then transported onto `base` by gluing morphisms of sheaves with `RelHomFamily.glue`, `hom_ext_of_cover` and `hom_ext_of_cover_whiskerLeft`: `TorsorGlue.smulBase` is the glued `G`-action, characterised by `TorsorGlue.str_smulBase` (`(G ◁ str f hf) ≫ smulBase = smul ≫ str f hf`, where `str f hf = (iso f hf).hom ≫ pullback.fst over (fppfYoneda.map f)`), and the module axioms `one_smulBase`, `mul_smulBase` and `smulBase_over` are proved from it by separatedness for maps out of `𝟙_ ⊗ base`, `(G ⊗ G) ⊗ base` and `G ⊗ base` respectively; `TorsorGlue.targetBase` is the glued map to `U` with `str_targetBase` and `targetBase_equivariant`; `TorsorGlue.principalMapBase` is the principal map and `TorsorGlue.isIso_principalMapBase` proves it invertible, via `isIso_of_cover` and the two comparison isomorphisms `sigmaIso f hf : G ⊗ (D.torsor f hf).P ≅ (G ⊗ base) ×_S X` and `rhoHom f hf : (D.torsor f hf).P ×_X (D.torsor f hf).P ≅ (base ×_S base) ×_S X`, which identify the base change of `principalMapBase` with the principal map of `D.torsor f hf` (`sigmaHom_relMap`). `TorsorGlue.torsor hR loc` assembles all of this into an `ActionTorsor G U S`, where `loc` is an fppf-local section of the glued sheaf, and `TorsorGlue.compIso hR loc f hf : D.torsor f hf ≅ pullbackObj f (T.torsor hR loc)` are the comparison isomorphisms of equivariant torsors, whose compatibility with the transition arrows is `TorsorGlue.compIso_trans`. Everything up to this point is unconditional. The one genuinely missing ingredient is the local section: `FppfTorsor` asks for a trivialisation over a *single* flat, locally finitely presented, surjective morphism `W ⟶ S`, whereas an fppf covering sieve only contains a jointly surjective *family*; replacing the family by the coproduct `∐ Wᵢ` requires knowing that an fppf sheaf carries coproducts of schemes to products, which the repository does not have. This is handled by keeping `loc` an explicit argument of `TorsorGlue.torsor` and by `TorsorGlue.localSectionOfCover`, which produces it unconditionally when the sieve contains one surjective flat morphism `w` locally of finite presentation (compose the trivialising cover of `D.torsor w hw` with `w`). Hence the main theorem `ActionTorsor.exists_torsor_of_torsorDescentDatum`: *for every fppf covering sieve `R` on `S` containing such a `w`, every `TorsorDescentDatum G U S R` is isomorphic to the restriction of a single equivariant torsor over `S`, compatibly with the transition arrows.* Together with `ActionTorsor.torsorPrestack` this gives `ActionTorsor.TorsorStack G U`, proved by `ActionTorsor.torsorStack`, unconditionally and with no new axiom (`#print axioms` returns `propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound`). The single mathematical restriction is a *hypothesis of the theorems*, clearly visible in their statements: the covering sieve `R` must contain one morphism `w : W ⟶ S` which is flat, locally of finite presentation and surjective (`exists_torsor_of_torsorDescentDatum`, and the second conjunct of `TorsorStack`). It is needed only to build the `FppfLocalSection` field of `FppfTorsor`, and the core construction `TorsorGlue.torsor hR loc` takes that section as an argument, so every other ingredient — the glued action, its module axioms, the map to `U`, principality, and the comparison isomorphisms — is unconditional. Removing the hypothesis is exactly the statement that an fppf sheaf of sets turns a coproduct of schemes into a product (so that the local sections over a covering *family* assemble into one over `∐ Wᵢ`); this is documented in the module docstring under "The local-triviality caveat".  ## Mathlib bridge (task 2): not achieved, but the blocker is now identified exactly  Scratch files in `<scratchpad>/scratch_TE/`. No line exceeds 100 characters; no `sorry`, no new `axiom`, and no `set_option maxHeartbeats` anywhere in the file (every declaration fits inside the default 200000 heartbeats). `GromovWitten/AlgebraicGeometry/Stacks/TorsorPushout.lean` removes the obstruction recorded at layer 7 in round 13 (`Cones/QuotientTorsorStack.lean` could only contract *trivialised* torsors, the general case being conditional on `AllTorsorsTrivial`): for every homomorphism `r : G ⟶ G'` of the group objects underlying two group algebraic spaces (`[IsMonHom r]`) and every fppf `G`-torsor `P` over a scheme `T`, `TorsorPushout.pushoutFppfTorsor P r` is an fppf `G'`-torsor over `T`, the pushout `r_* P`.  The construction is unconditional and uses no sheafification: the naive "sheaf of `G`-equivariant maps `P ⟶ G'`" cannot even be written down, since hom-sets of `Sheaf Scheme.fppfTopology (Type u)` live in `Type (u+1)`, so `r_* P` is instead glued (`SheafGluing.glueSheaf`) from the descent datum `TorsorPushout.descentDatum P r` of the trivial `G'`-torsor over the chosen trivialising cover of `P`, with transition maps the right translations by the image under `r` of the transition cocycle `TorsorPushout.coverDiv` of `P`; that cocycle comes from the new point calculus of torsors (`TorsorPushout.actPt`, `divPt`, `actPt_divPt`, `eq_divPt`, `exists_unique_actPt`, `divPt_mul`), which makes the principality isomorphism usable as "the difference of two points".  The defining property of the pushout is packaged as `TorsorPushout.PushoutTorsor r P`: a sheaf with a `G'`-action, a projection, and the evaluation pairing `ev α p` of a point of the pushout against a point of `P` over the same base, subject to `ev (g' · α) p = g' * ev α p`, `ev α (g · p) = ev α p * (r g)⁻¹` and bijectivity of `α ↦ ev α p` on fibres; from that datum alone the torsor axioms are *theorems* (`PushoutTorsor.isIso_principalMap` through `SheafGluing.isIso_of_sections`, `PushoutTorsor.localSection`, `PushoutTorsor.toFppfTorsor`), and `TorsorPushout.pushoutTorsor` verifies the datum for the glued sheaf: the `G'`-action (`pushoutSmul`, `pushoutAction`, through the new pointwise criterion `modObjOfPoints` for module objects) is fibrewise (`pushoutSmul_proj`), the local trivialisations `coordAt`/`ptOfCoord` identify the fibres over the cover with the points of `G'`, the local evaluation `evLocal α p = coord α * r (p / L c)⁻¹` is independent of the chosen lift `c` of the base (`evLocal_lift_congr`) and glues to the global pairing `evMap`/`evPt` (`SheafGluing.PartialHom.glue`), and `ptOfEv` produces a point of the pushout with a prescribed value at any given point of `P`.  For an action torsor `P : ActionTorsor G U T` and an `r`-equivariant morphism `pt : U ⟶ U'` of action spaces (`TorsorPushout.IsEquivariantOver`), the file also constructs the induced map `TorsorPushout.targetMap : r_* P ⟶ U'`, glued from `α ↦ α (L c) · pt (L c)`, proves it `G'`-equivariant (`targetMap_equivariant`) and assembles the contraction `TorsorPushout.pushoutActionTorsor : ActionTorsor G' U' T`.  What is still missing is the functoriality of `r_*` — its action on arrows of torsors, `(𝟙 G)_* P ≅ P`, `(r ∘ s)_* ≅ r_* ∘ s_*`, `r_* G ≅ G'` — and the uniqueness of a pushout datum; all of these are further instances of the same "glue over `coverSieve P`, then `isIso_of_sections`" pattern. Note finally that `ConeQuotient.ActionTwist` twists only the `T`-points of `G`, so it is not a homomorphism of group objects; using this file for the `𝔸¹`-contraction of `[C/E]` needs group objects relative to `T`. `GromovWitten/AlgebraicGeometry/Stacks/TorsorPushoutRelative.lean` removes the limitation recorded at the end of `Stacks/TorsorPushout.lean`: the pushout `r_* P` was constructed there only for a homomorphism `r : G ⟶ G'` of *absolute* group objects, whereas the scalar contraction of the affine cone quotient `[C/E]` by `r : Γ(T, 𝒪)` is a homomorphism of group objects only over `T`.  A `TorsorPushoutRel.RelMonHom G G' T` is a single morphism of fppf sheaves `G ⊗ y(T) ⟶ G'` whose induced map on the `W`-points lying over a base point `b : y(W) ⟶ y(T)` is a group homomorphism (`map_one'`, `map_mul'`); naturality in `W` is automatic, so this is exactly a homomorphism of group objects over `y(T)`, and `RelMonHom.pt b x` together with `comp_pt`, `pt_one`, `pt_mul`, `pt_inv`, `pt_congr` and the comparison `RelMonHom.ofMonHom` with absolute homomorphisms is its point calculus; `RelEquivMap ρ U U'` is the corresponding notion of a `ρ`-equivariant map `U ⊗ y(T) ⟶ U'` of action spaces, with `RelEquivMap.pt_actPt`.  The whole descent construction of `Stacks/TorsorPushout.lean` is carried out again with `g ≫ r` replaced by `ρ.pt b g`, where `b` is the base point over which `g` lives: `TorsorPushoutRel.PushoutTorsorRel ρ P` is the defining property (a sheaf with a `G'`-action and the evaluation pairing `ev`, subject to `ev (g' · α) p = g' * ev α p`, `ev α (g · p) = ev α p * (ρ_b g)⁻¹` and bijectivity on fibres), from which the torsor axioms are *theorems* (`PushoutTorsorRel.isIso_principalMap`, `localSection`, `toFppfTorsor`); `TorsorPushoutRel.descentDatum P ρ` glues the trivial `G'`-torsor over the trivialising cover of `P` by right translation by `TorsorPushoutRel.relDiv ρ`, and `pushoutSheaf`, `pushoutProj`, `pushoutAction`, `coordAt`, `ptOfCoord`, `evLocal`, `evMap`, `evPt`, `ptOfEv` assemble into `TorsorPushoutRel.pushoutTorsor` and the relative pushout `TorsorPushoutRel.pushoutFppfTorsor P ρ : FppfTorsor G' T`, with the equivariant map `targetMap` and the contraction `TorsorPushoutRel.pushoutActionTorsor P ρ τ : ActionTorsor G' U' T` for a `RelEquivMap τ`.  Beyond the absolute case the file proves `TorsorPushoutRel.targetMap_eq_actPt`, the global formula `targetMap α = ev(α, p) · τ (p)` valid at any point `p` of `P` over the same base with no lift to the cover, from which `contractionIsoTrivial` (the contraction of a torsor with a global section is the trivial torsor with the contracted point) and `pushoutTrivialIso` (`ρ_*` of the trivial torsor with point `t` is the trivial torsor with point `τ (t)`) follow, and `idPushoutTorsorRel`, the statement that `P` itself is a pushout datum of `P` along the identity, with `ev` the difference of two points. Finally the file instantiates all of this for the cone quotient, with no `AllTorsorsTrivial` hypothesis anywhere: `TorsorPushoutRel.scaleRelMonHom σ T r` is multiplication by a scalar `r : Γ(T, 𝒪)` on `vectorBundleGroup σ = 𝔾ₐ^σ` (built from the scheme morphism `scaleBundleScheme`, with `map_one'`/`map_mul'` checked on `coords`), `TorsorPushoutRel.coneScaleRelEquivMap A bas r` is the contraction `ConeQuotient.scaleRing` of the cone (built from `scaleConeScheme`, its equivariance being exactly `ConeQuotient.scaleRing_actOn`), and `TorsorPushoutRel.coneContraction A bas r` contracts *every* object of `ActionTorsor (vectorBundleGroup σ) (coneActionSpace A bas) T` by `r`; `coneScaleRelEquivMap_pt_eq_coneTwist` identifies its effect on `T`-points with `ConeQuotient.coneTwist A bas r₀` over an affine base, and `coneContractionEmbeddingIso` gives the natural-in-objects isomorphism `embedding.obj ((coneTwist A bas r₀).functor.obj x) ≅ coneContraction A bas (gammaMap B r₀) (embedding.obj x)` on trivialised torsors.  What remains missing is the functoriality of `ρ_*` on arrows of torsors and the uniqueness of a pushout datum — hence the unit law `(𝟙)_* P ≅ P` as an isomorphism of torsors, and the composition law `(ρ ∘ ρ')_* ≅ ρ_* ∘ ρ'_*` — and consequently the transport of the multiplicativity, zero, vertex and base-change coherence isomorphisms of `Cones/QuotientTorsorStack.lean` from `TrivialPoint` to `ActionTorsor`. Issue #40: `Stacks/SchemeAtlasRefinement*.lean` (from the owner's Codex branch) present maps of represented schemes by scheme pullbacks, convert chart pullback presentations into stack-morphism presentations and back, construct a common scheme atlas of two smooth surjective charts with smooth surjective projections and its comparison 2-cell, and give fully faithful functors from its presentation groupoid to both input groupoids; `Stacks/PresentationGroupoidObject.lean` bundles the self-overlap `U ×_X U ⇉ U` of a chart with its source, target, unit, composition and inverse as `PresentationGroupoidObject` and proves `Genuine.inverse_inverse`; `Stacks/PresentationGroupoidLaws.lean` proves the five remaining groupoid-object laws as `StackIso2`s — both unit laws (`Genuine.unit_compose_right/_left`), both inverse laws (`Genuine.inverse_compose_right/_left`) and associativity (`Genuine.compose_assoc`) on the triple-composable stack `Genuine.composable3` — each through pairing maps lifted along `R.composable.bilimit` and two applications of `R.bilimit.lift_unique`, and identifies them with the fibrewise groupoid structure (`PresentationGroupoid.unit_agrees/compose_agrees/inverse_agrees`). Atlas independence of the point set and topology was already in `Stacks/PointsIndependence.lean`. Remaining for #40: the chart-level `ChartRefinement` record and the compatibility of `refinementGroupoidMap` with the groupoid structure. |
| 1: modules and cones | `Sites/*`, `Modules/Stack.lean`, `Modules/Derived.lean`, `Cones/Affine.lean`, `Cones/Graded.lean`, `Cones/Quotient.lean`, `Cones/Stack.lean`, `Cones/DeformationSpaceGeometry.lean`, `RelativeSpec.lean`, `Cones/NormalConeGlobal.lean`, `Cones/ConeTranslation.lean`, `Cones/NormalConeRegular.lean`, `Cones/Products.lean`, `RelativeSpecRestrict.lean`, `VectorBundleTotalSpace.lean`, `RelativeSpecAffineHom.lean`, `VectorBundleTrivial.lean` |   distinct stack sites, canonical regular-function sheaves, constructed ringed-site identity/composition and module pullback adjunctions, actual module sheaves, direct sums and canonical finite-free sheaves, symmetric affine cones, Rees normal cones, the proved canonical closed immersion `Spec(gr_I R) → Spec Sym(I/I²)`, the proof that this canonical map is an isomorphism when the affine ideal comes with a chosen finite regular generating sequence (`Cones/RegularSequence.lean`, on top of the neutral commutative-algebra file `Algebra/QuasiRegular.lean`), concrete quotient groupoids, and a constructed rank-zero vector-bundle stack; the former relative-Spec/Proj and derived-Picard theorem packages are inactive; `Cones/DeformationSpace.lean` now constructs the affine deformation to the normal cone: the extended Rees algebra `R[It, t⁻¹]` is built as the subalgebra of Laurent polynomials whose degree-`n` coefficient lies in `Iⁿ` for `n ≥ 0`, with the deformation parameter `u = t⁻¹` proved a nonzerodivisor, the special fibre `R[It, t⁻¹]/(u)` proved isomorphic to the associated graded ring `gr_I(R)` (the coordinate ring of the affine normal cone), so that the normal cone is a closed subscheme of the deformation space `M° = Spec R[It, t⁻¹]` lying over the origin of the affine line, the localization at `u` proved to be the full Laurent polynomial ring, so that the generic fibre over `𝔾_m` is the trivial family `Spec R × 𝔾_m` embedded as the open subscheme `D(u)`, the fibre over `u = 1` proved isomorphic to `Spec R` itself (evaluation at `t = 1` is surjective with kernel `(u − 1)`, by an explicit geometric-series division inside the extended Rees algebra), and, over a base field `k`, the deformation space proved torsion-free hence flat over `k[u]`, so the structure morphism `M° → 𝔸¹_k` is a flat morphism of schemes; `Cones/DeformationSpaceGeometry.lean` now packages these fibre identifications as genuine cartesian squares of schemes and supplies the remaining geometry: a general pushout criterion for squares of commutative rings whose two right-hand maps are surjective (`isPushout_of_surjective`, proved from the colimit universal property under the kernel condition `ker i ≤ (ker g)·B`) gives `specialFibre_isPullback` and `normalCone_isPullback` (the fibre of `M° → 𝔸¹` over the origin is `Spec gr_I R`, the affine normal cone) and `fibreOne_isPullback`/`fibreOne_isPullback_spec` (the fibre over `u = 1` is `Spec R`), while `genericFibre_isPullback` exhibits the open piece over `𝔾_m ⊆ 𝔸¹` as the honest base change of `𝔾_m → 𝔸¹`, from Mathlib's localization pushout together with `isLocalization_parameter` and `Submonoid.map_powers`; the deformation of the closed subscheme is an actual ring map `subschemeHom : R[It,t⁻¹] → (R/I)[u]` (reduce coefficients modulo `I` and keep the non-positive powers of `t`, built from the coefficientwise base change `mapLaurent` of Laurent polynomials and the additive retraction of the embedding `(R/I)[u] ↪ (R/I)[t,t⁻¹]`, `u ↦ t⁻¹`), proved surjective and hence a closed immersion `subschemeι : Spec(R/I) × 𝔸¹ → M°` (`subschemeι_isClosedImmersion`) compatible with the projections to `𝔸¹` (`subschemeι_comp_toLine`) and meeting the special fibre exactly in the vertex of the normal cone (`origin_comp_subschemeι`, through the transported augmentation `specialFibreAugmentation` and `vertex_comp_normalConeIso`); base change is constructed for an arbitrary `R`-algebra `B` as a comparison map `reesTensorMap : R[It,t⁻¹] ⊗_R B → B[(IB)t,t⁻¹]`, proved surjective unconditionally (`reesTensorMap_surjective`, by span induction on the extended ideal) and bijective whenever `B` is flat (`reesTensorEquiv`), injectivity coming from the proved linear isomorphism `R[t,t⁻¹] ⊗_R B ≅ B[t,t⁻¹]` (`laurentTensorEquiv`, via `TensorProduct.finsuppScalarLeft`) together with flatness of the inclusion `R[It,t⁻¹] ⊆ R[t,t⁻¹]`, whence the ring pushout `isPushout_reesBaseChange`, the cartesian square `reesBaseChange_isPullback` and its localization case `reesBaseChange_isPullback_isLocalization`; the tangent action is constructed in the `ConeAction` language of `Cones/Quotient.lean` in the general form `ConeQuotient.bundleTranslationAction d`, the action of `Spec Sym(F)` on `Spec Sym(N)` attached to any linear map `d : N →ₗ F`, with the unit, associativity and conic laws all proved and the translation action of a bundle on itself recovered as the case `d = id` (`bundleTranslationCoaction_id`), and is specialised through the constructed conormal map `AffineNormalCone.conormalMap : I/I² → (R/I) ⊗_R Ω[R⁄k]` to `AffineNormalCone.normalSheafTangentAction`, the action of the restricted tangent bundle `Spec Sym_{R/I}((R/I) ⊗_R Ω[R⁄k])` on the affine normal *sheaf* `Spec Sym_{R/I}(I/I²)`, with the affine action morphism `normalSheafActionMap` and its proved unit law; what remains is the descent of this action along the closed immersion `Spec gr_I R → Spec Sym(I/I²)` to the normal *cone* itself, which requires the translation coaction to preserve the kernel of `Sym_{R/I}(I/I²) ↠ gr_I R` — true when the ambient scheme is smooth over `k` (Vistoli's lemma, the input to the intrinsic normal cone) and unavailable from the pinned Mathlib — together with the globalization of all of this over a non-affine base; `Cones/Graded.lean` now constructs affine graded cones over an affine base as actual data rather than fields: a graded `R`-algebra is encoded by its `𝔸¹`-contraction coaction `ψ : S →ₐ[R] S[t]` (`ψ s = s·tⁿ` in degree `n`), whose counit and coassociativity laws are proved for every cone built in the file, and from which the monoid-action axioms on `B`-points are derived (`scale_one`, `scale_scale`) together with `contraction_one`, `contraction_mul` and, for a vertex `ε`, `contraction 0 = ofId ∘ ε` and the proof that the vertex is a section of the projection and a fixed point of every contraction, also as morphisms of schemes (`vertexSection_comp_projection`, `contractionMap_one/_mul/_zero`, `vertexSection_comp_contractionMap`); the internal grading is recovered from the coaction (`homogeneous ψ n`, degree projections `proj ψ n` with `s = Σ proj n s` from the counit and `proj n s ∈ homogeneous ψ n` from coassociativity), and Mathlib's `GradedAlgebra 𝒮` is bridged to it with `homogeneous (gradedCoaction 𝒮) n = 𝒮 n`, giving polynomial rings graded by total degree as an example; the affine cone `Spec Sym(M)` and the Rees normal cone `Spec gr_I(R)` are proved to be instances: for `Sym(M)` the augmentation is proved to be the vertex and the new contraction is proved equal to the existing `AffineCone.contraction`, and for `gr_I(R)` the coaction is constructed by restricting the comultiplication of `R[t]` to the Rees algebra (proved to land there coefficientwise) and descending along the ideal `I·Rees_I(R)`, proved homogeneous, with the resulting contraction proved equal to the existing `AffineNormalCone.reesScale` and `associatedGradedScale`; morphisms of cones are graded algebra maps intertwining the coactions, proved to commute with all contractions and to carry vertices to vertices (not assumed), closed subcones are constructed as graded quotients by homogeneous ideals (with the closed immersion on spectra), products of cones are constructed as tensor products with the total grading together with the two projections as cone morphisms and the product vertex, and the abelian hull `Spec Sym(S₁) ← Spec S` is constructed and proved to be a closed immersion whenever the cone is generated in degree one; on vector bundles `C(F) = Spec Sym(F)` the functor of points is proved to be `Hom_R(F, B)` with the contraction action corresponding to scalar multiplication of linear maps, the degree-one part of `Sym(F)` is proved to be exactly `F` for an arbitrary module (`ι` is injective via the square-zero extension, and every degree-one element comes from `F`), hence the bundle rank equals the module rank (`rank_homogeneous_one`, `finrank_homogeneous_one`), `C(F)` is proved smooth over the base for `F` finite free, and base change is proved as an algebra isomorphism `Sym_B(B ⊗_R F) ≃ₐ[B] B ⊗_R Sym_R(F)`; the comparison with the rank-zero vector-bundle stack of `Cones/Stack.lean`, the `R/I`-linear vertex of the normal cone, and smoothness for merely finite projective modules remain to be constructed; `Modules/Derived.lean` additionally constructs the big-étale and big-fppf ringed sites of schemes (both ringed by the representable regular-function sheaf) and an actual `RingedSiteMorphism` between them, whose site functor is the identity and whose continuity is the proved inclusion `etaleTopology ≤ fppfTopology`; the induced pullback of module sheaves `etaleToFppfModules` is exported together with the identity and composition comparisons obtained from the constructed ringed-site machinery; a ringed-site morphism from the small étale site of a scheme (or of a Deligne–Mumford stack) into the big fppf site remains absent, since it needs continuity of `Scheme.Etale.forget X ⋙ Over.forget X` from `Scheme.smallEtaleTopology X` to `Scheme.fppfTopology`, which Mathlib does not supply; `Cones/Quotient.lean` now constructs, at the affine level, actions of a vector bundle on a cone: an action of `E = Spec Sym(F)` on the affine cone `C = Spec S` over `Spec R` is packaged as `ConeQuotient.ConeAction`, whose data are the `𝔸¹`-contraction coaction of `Cones/Graded.lean` and a coaction `α : S →ₐ[R] S ⊗_R Sym(F)`, and whose `Prop` fields are hypotheses about that data (the unit and associativity laws of the induced action `ConeQuotient.translate` on `B`-points `(S →ₐ[R] B) × (F →ₗ[R] B) → (S →ₐ[R] B)`, and the requirement `conic` that the action map `C ×_{Spec R} E → C` be a morphism of cones) which are proved, not assumed, for every action built in the file; the Hopf comultiplication `symComul : Sym(F) → Sym(F) ⊗ Sym(F)` is constructed from the diagonal and proved to compute the addition of `B`-points (`lift_comp_symComul`), `symMap` gives the coordinate algebra map of a morphism of bundles, and the action on points is proved natural in the test algebra (`comp_translate`); the two examples are constructed with all axioms proved: the translation action of `E` on itself (`translationAction`, from `symComul`) and the free action of `E` on a product `C ×_{Spec R} E` (`freeAction`, built from `freeLeft`/`freeRight`/`freeCoaction` and the new `isConeHom_tensor_ext` criterion for a map out of a product of cones to be conic); from `conic` it is proved that contractions commute with the action, `scale (translate φ l) b = translate (scale φ b) (b • l)` (`ConeAction.scale_translate`), so the action is `𝔾ₘ`-equivariant, and that contracting any point by `0` is the vertex and the vertex is fixed by every contraction over every test algebra (`scale_zero_eq`, `scale_vertex`); on spectra the action and the zero section are constructed as morphisms of affine schemes (`actionMap`, `zeroSectionMap`) with the unit law `zeroSectionMap_comp_actionMap` and the equivariance `actionMap_comp_contractionMap` proved; equivariant morphisms of cones-with-actions are `IsEquivariant A A' φ u` (a `Prop` about `φ : S →ₐ[R] S'` and `u : F →ₗ[R] F'`, not stored data), proved closed under identity and composition and proved to intertwine the point actions (`translate_comp`); still missing at this level are products and fibre products of cone actions, which need the canonical isomorphism `Sym(F ⊕ F') ≃ Sym(F) ⊗ Sym(F')` that Mathlib's `SymmetricAlgebra` API does not provide (only `equivMvPolynomial` for a module with a basis), and any non-affine base; `RelativeSpec.lean` now constructs relative `Spec` over an arbitrary scheme: a quasi-coherent algebra is given affine-locally (`AlgebraData`: an algebra over the section ring of every affine open, functorial transition maps, and the requirement that each transition square be an actual pushout of commutative rings, which `isPushout_of_algEquiv` derives from a bijective base-change map `Γ(U) ⊗ 𝒜(V) → 𝒜(U)`), and `relativeSpec X 𝒜` is glued from the affine spectra by Mathlib's relative gluing along the locally directed cover by affine opens, with the affine structure morphism `toBase` (proved affine, `toBase_isAffineHom`, and an isomorphism when all structure maps are bijective), cartesian affine pieces (`isPullback_affine`), and the affine pieces covering the total space; a morphism of algebra data (`RelativeSpec.Hom`, a compatible family of algebra maps) induces a morphism of relative spectra over `X` (`Hom.map`, `map_toBase`) whose restriction to every affine piece is the affine `Spec.map` (`isPullback_map`), so that any property local on the target holds for it as soon as it holds affine-locally (`property_map_of_forall`); in particular a surjective family gives a closed immersion and a bijective family an isomorphism. `Cones/NormalConeGlobal.lean` applies this to a quasi-coherent ideal sheaf `I` on any scheme `X`: the normal cone `C_{Z/X} = Spec_X (gr_I 𝒪_X)` (`NormalCone.normalCone`) and the normal sheaf `N_{Z/X} = Spec_X Sym(I/I²)` (`NormalSheaf.normalSheaf`) are relative spectra of the affine `gr_{I(U)}` and `Sym(I(U))/I(U)·Sym(I(U))`, with both structure morphisms affine and both proved to restrict to the affine constructions of `Cones/Affine.lean` over every affine open; the required pushout squares along the restriction maps of affine opens are proved, not assumed, from flatness of those restriction maps: the Rees algebra commutes with flat base change (`reesTensorEquiv`, from `ReesBlowup.reesBaseChangeMap`), an ideal commutes with flat base change (`idealTensorEquiv`, `B ⊗ I ≅ IB`), the symmetric algebra commutes with arbitrary base change (`symTensorEquiv`, via `GradedCone.baseChangeEquiv`), and quotients by extended ideals commute with arbitrary base change (`quotBaseChangeEquiv`, from Mathlib's `Algebra.TensorProduct.tensorQuotientEquiv`), giving `grTensorEquiv` and `nsTensorEquiv`; the canonical surjection `Sym(I) → Rees(I)` onto the degree-one generators (`symToRees`, surjective by `adjoin_monomial_eq_reesAlgebra`) descends to the compatible family of surjections `nsToGr : Sym(I/I²) → gr_I` and hence to a closed immersion `NormalCone.toNormalSheaf : C_{Z/X} → N_{Z/X}` over `X` (`toNormalSheaf_isClosedImmersion`, `toNormalSheaf_toBase`), which over every affine open is proved to be the affine `AffineNormalCone.coneToNormalSheaf` of `Cones/Affine.lean` composed with the identification `normalSheafRingEquiv : Sym(I)/I·Sym(I) ≃ Sym_{A/I}(I/I²)` (`isPullback_coneToNormalSheaf`); still missing are flat base change and product formulas for the glued cones and relative `Spec` on stacks. `Cones/NormalConeRegular.lean` supplies three of the items previously listed as missing for the glued normal cone: the affine regular-immersion theorem is generalised to quasi-regular generating sequences (`AffineNormalCone.QuasiregularGenerators`, the hypothesis that `Cones/RegularSequence.lean` actually uses), the resulting bijectivity of the gluing comparison `nsToGr : Sym(I)/I·Sym(I) → gr_I` is transported through `normalSheafRingEquiv`, and `RelativeSpec.Hom.isIso_map` gives `NormalCone.isIso_toNormalSheaf`: for a quasi-coherent ideal sheaf which on every affine open is generated by a quasi-regular (in particular a regular) sequence, the canonical closed immersion `C_{Z/X} → N_{Z/X}` is an isomorphism over `X` (`NormalCone.isoNormalSheaf`); the comparison is proved to be local on the base: bijectivity of `nsToGr` is inherited by smaller affine opens through flat base change (`NormalCone.bijective_nsToGr_of_le`) and descends along an affine open cover (`NormalCone.bijective_nsToGr_of_cover`, by the localisation argument `exists_pow_smul_eq_zero`, `eq_zero_of_forall_exists_pow_smul_eq_zero` with the sections whose basic opens lie in members of the cover spanning the unit ideal), so `NormalCone.isIso_toNormalSheaf_of_cover` and `NormalCone.isIso_toNormalSheaf_of_forall_mem_cover`/`_of_regular_cover` need the quasi-regular (in particular regular) generators only on the members of one affine open cover, the usual definition of a regular immersion. The abelian-hull statement is proved: the degree-one part of the cone `Spec gr_I(R)` over its own base `Spec(R/I)` is exactly the conormal module (`homogeneous_one_eq_range`, `conormalToAssociatedGraded` injective), under this identification `Sym_{R/I}(I/I²) → gr_I(R)` is the abelian hull map of `Cones/Graded.lean` (`abelianHull_symCongr`, `isGeneratedInDegreeOne_grQuotCoaction`), and over every affine open the global `toNormalSheaf` is the abelian hull (`NormalCone.isPullback_abelianHull`). The structure morphisms are factored through the closed subscheme: `RelativeSpec.toSubscheme` maps a relative `Spec` to `I.subscheme` whenever the chart structure maps are killed by `I`, giving `NormalCone.toSubscheme` and `NormalSheaf.toSubscheme` with `toSubscheme ≫ I.subschemeι = toBase` and `toNormalSheaf ≫ NormalSheaf.toSubscheme = NormalCone.toSubscheme`. Products and fibre products of affine cones and of cone actions are now available over an affine base (`Cones/Products.lean`): the universal property of the product cone (`GradedCone.isConeHom_tensorLift`, `GradedCone.tensorLift_unique`, `GradedCone.isConeHom_tensorMap`) and the fibre-product square `GradedCone.isPullback_projection` for `Spec (S ⊗[R] S')`; using `NormalSheafPicard.symProdEquiv`, `GradedCone.isConeHom_symProdEquiv` and its inverse show that `Sym(M × N) ≃ Sym M ⊗ Sym N` is an isomorphism of cones and `GradedCone.isPullback_symProd` identifies `C(M × N)` with `C(M) ×_{Spec R} C(N)`, the projections being induced by `LinearMap.inl`/`LinearMap.inr`; for cone actions, `ConeQuotient.ConeAction.prod` constructs the action of `E × E'` on `C ×_{Spec R} C'`, `ConeQuotient.ConeAction.pullback` restricts an action along a morphism of vector bundles, and `ConeQuotient.ConeAction.diagonal` gives the diagonal action of a single `E` on a fibre product, with the unit, associativity and conic laws proved and all projections equivariant. The vector-bundle criterion is unconditional for finite projective modules: `GradedCone.smooth_symmetricAlgebra_of_projective` proves `Algebra.Smooth R (Sym F)` and `GradedCone.smooth_projection_of_projective` the smoothness of `C(F) → Spec R`, via the new general lemmas that a retract of a smooth (resp. finitely presented, formally smooth) algebra is again one (`AlgebraRetract.smooth`, `AlgebraRetract.finitePresentation`, `AlgebraRetract.formallySmooth`, with the kernel computation `AlgebraRetract.ker_eq_span`); the converse `GradedCone.projective_of_flat_symmetricAlgebra` deduces projectivity of a finitely presented `F` from flatness of `Sym F`. The `R/I`-linear vertex of the normal cone is constructed (`GradedCone.normalConeCoactionQuot`, `GradedCone.normalConeVertexHom`, `GradedCone.isConeVertex_normalConeCoactionQuot`). Still missing at this level: any non-affine base for products, the comparison of `Spec Sym F` with the `VectorBundleStack`/`stackRank` of `Cones/Stack.lean` (Round 17.) `GromovWitten/AlgebraicGeometry/RelativeSpecRestrict.lean` restricts the relative `Spec` of a quasi-coherent algebra to an open subscheme. `AlgebraData.restrict 𝒜 V` is the restriction of an `AlgebraData X` to `V : X.Opens`; because the section ring of an affine open `W` of `V` is definitionally the section ring of `V.ι ''ᵁ W` in `X`, all its fields — including the base-change pushout squares — are inherited from `𝒜` verbatim. `RelativeSpec.restrictι 𝒜 V` is the induced morphism `relativeSpec V (𝒜|_V) ⟶ relativeSpec X 𝒜`; it is proved to lie over `V.ι` (`restrictι_toBase`), to be injective (`injective_restrictι`) and an open immersion (`isOpenImmersion_restrictι`, via `IsOpenImmersion.of_openCover_source` applied to the affine pieces), and to have image exactly the preimage of `V` (`opensRange_restrictι`), the two last results resting on `toBase_preimage_affineOpen`, the `RelativeSpec`-level form of Mathlib's `RelativeGluingData.toBase_preimage_eq_opensRange_ι`. The main theorem `RelativeSpec.isPullback_restrict` states that `IsPullback (toBase V (𝒜|_V)) (restrictι 𝒜 V) V.ι (toBase X 𝒜)`, i.e. `Spec_V (𝒜|_V) = Spec_X 𝒜 ×_X V`; it is deduced from `isPullback_morphismRestrict` through the isomorphism `restrictIso` with the open subscheme `(toBase X 𝒜) ⁻¹ᵁ V`. As an application the file defines the restriction of a quasi-coherent ideal sheaf `Scheme.IdealSheafData.restrict` (absent from Mathlib), proves that the associated graded algebra commutes with it on the nose (`NormalCone.algebraData_restrict`, a `rfl`), and obtains `NormalCone.isPullback_restrict : C_{Z ∩ V / V} = C_{Z / X} ×_X V`, filling gap (d) of the round-17 cone survey. Not covered: restriction along a general (non-open) morphism, functoriality of `restrictι` in `V` (transitivity for `V' ≤ V`), and compatibility with `RelativeSpec.Hom.map`. The total space of a vector bundle over a general scheme is now available as a relative `Spec`. `VectorBundleTotalSpace.structureData X` presents `𝒪_X` itself as a `RelativeSpec.AlgebraData`, and `isIso_toBase_structureData`/`baseIso` identify its relative `Spec` with `X`. A vector bundle is packaged as `BundleData X ι`: a quasi-coherent algebra, an augmentation to `structureData X`, and trivialisations `triv j : algebra.ring (chart j) ≃ₐ MvPolynomial ι Γ(X, chart j)` over affine opens `chart j` covering `X` under which the augmentation is evaluation at the origin. Its total space `BundleData.totalSpace` carries an affine projection `BundleData.proj` (`isAffineHom_proj`) and a zero section `BundleData.zeroSection` which is proved to be a section (`zeroSection_proj`) and a closed immersion (`isClosedImmersion_zeroSection`), the latter from surjectivity of the augmentation on every affine open (`surjective_augmentation_app`). The local structure is proved: `isPullback_chart` exhibits the chart `𝔸^ι_U = Spec (MvPolynomial ι Γ(X,U))` as the fibre product of the total space with `U` over `X`, `opensRange_chartι` and `iSup_opensRange_chartι` show the charts are the preimages of the base charts and cover the total space, and `zeroSection_chart` identifies the zero section over a chart with the vertex section `GradedCone.vertexSection (MvPolynomial.aeval 0)`. Missing: no construction of a `BundleData` from a locally free sheaf or from `Sym` of a module (the structure is deliberately abstract), no rank or dimension statements, and the fibre point `chartBundlePoint` is only known to lie over the right base point (`proj_chartBundlePoint`) — its independence of the chart is not proved. (Round 18.) `RelativeSpecAffineHom.lean` shows that every affine morphism `g : C ⟶ X` is a relative `Spec`: `AlgebraData.ofAffineHom g` (rings `Γ(C, g⁻¹ᵁ W)`, pushout squares from `isPushout_appTop_of_isPullback`), `ringEquivGamma : 𝒜.ring U ≃+* Γ(relativeSpec X 𝒜, toBase⁻¹ᵁ U)`, morphisms of relative `Spec`s from morphisms over `X` (`Hom.ofOver`, `Hom.ofOverRelativeSpec`, surjective on affine opens for closed immersions), and `relativeSpecOfAffineHomIso : relativeSpec X (ofAffineHom g) ≅ C` over `X`. `VectorBundleTrivial.lean` packages the trivial bundle `X × 𝔸^ι` as `trivialData X ι : BundleData X ι`, defines `GlobalTrivialisation 𝓔` (compatible trivialisations on all affine opens) with the induced isomorphism of total spaces over `X` compatible with fibre generic points, flat pullbacks and principal-divisor spans, the constant sections `sectionMap f ι c : X ⟶ X × 𝔸^ι` for `c : ι → k` (closed immersions, sections of the projection), and the rename tower `forget : X × 𝔸^{Option ι'} ⟶ X × 𝔸^{ι'}` with `forget_bundlePoint`. |
| 2: rational intersection theory | `IntersectionTheory/*`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyRankOne.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopy.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyKey.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/LocalOrdSymmetry.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleSectionGysin.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleSectionGysinIdentity.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyInjective.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/CycleGluing.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/HomogeneityLocal.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/FiniteTypeDimension.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackGlobal.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackFundamental.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleOverSubscheme.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackGlobalChow.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/SupportedCycles.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyClosed.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyGlobal.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/GeneratorInvariance.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/GeneratorInvarianceAffine.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleSectionGysinExplicit.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/LineBundleInjective.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/LineBundleInjectiveGlobal.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/LineBundleInjectiveGysin.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/LineBundleInjectiveRelation.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyInjectiveGlobal.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/LineBundleData.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/FirstChernClass.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/DivisorSymmetryAffine.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/FirstChernClassDescent.lean`, `Algebra/SemilocalMoving.lean` |     scheme cycles graded by actual closure dimension, functorial proper pushforward on those cycle groups, constructed orders of vanishing, canonical rational equivalence, closed-immersion Chow pushforward, the proper-point Chow computation, and a constructed flat pullback along arbitrary open immersions which is proved rational-linear and contravariantly functorial, proved to descend to the rational Chow quotient, and assembled into the right-exact part of the localization sequence: restriction to an open subscheme of a Noetherian scheme is proved surjective through an actual extension-by-zero section, and it is proved to annihilate every class pushed forward from a closed subscheme of the complement; the principal divisor of a rational function is proved to have zero Chow class. `IntersectionTheory/StackChowVistoli.lean` now adds the Vistoli descent construction on top of this: for a scheme-level presentation groupoid `R ⇉ U` whose source and target are open immersions of relative dimension zero (`OpenPresentationGroupoid`, a data-only record with no conclusion stored in a field), the Vistoli cycles are constructed as the equalizer `{α ∈ Z_i(U) : s^*α = t^*α}` of the two genuine flat pullbacks, proved equal to the pointwise descent condition `α(s(r)) = α(t(r))` (`mem_cycles_iff`), and the Vistoli rational Chow group `chow` is their quotient by rational equivalence on the atlas. A refinement of presentations (an open immersion of atlases intertwined with a map of arrow schemes, the scheme shadow of `refinementGroupoidMap`) is proved, not assumed, to carry Vistoli cycles to Vistoli cycles and to preserve rational equivalence, hence to induce a map of Vistoli Chow groups which is proved functorial (`chowMap_id`, `chowMap_trans`); two presentations with a common refinement are therefore canonically comparable (`commonRefinementComparison`), and an invertible refinement induces an isomorphism (`Refinement.chowEquiv`, `commonRefinementEquiv`). For a presentation whose two legs agree, in particular the identity atlas of a scheme, every cycle is proved to descend and the Vistoli Chow group is proved canonically isomorphic to the scheme rational Chow group, compatibly with both quotient maps (`chowEquivSchemeChow`, `identityPresentationChowEquiv`), so the construction genuinely extends the scheme theory. On the stabilizer side the rational weight `1/n` is constructed with its normalization `n · (1/n) = 1` and the weight-one criterion for trivial stabilizers; for an object of a presentation groupoid the weight is proved to be `1/|Aut|` of its image in the base stack (`autWeight_presentationGroupoid`, via `PresentationGroupoid.autEquiv`) and is proved invariant under refinement of atlases (`autWeight_refinementFunctor`, via the fully faithful refinement functor). The stabilizer-weighted degree of a zero-cycle is constructed and proved independent of the finite set used to compute it, and on the one-point presentation over `Spec k` it is assembled into an actual homomorphism `chow 0 →ₗ[ℚ] ℚ` on the Vistoli Chow group, for which the fundamental class of a point with stabilizer group `Γ` is proved to have degree `1/|Γ|`, with `|Γ| · deg = 1` (`pointDegree_pointFundamentalClass`, `card_mul_pointDegree_pointFundamentalClass`, and `weightedDegree_fundamentalCycle_of_autEquiv` with the stabilizer read off from a genuine presentation-groupoid object). What is still missing is precisely one geometric input: flat pullback of algebraic cycles along a flat morphism of positive relative dimension. Mathlib v4.33.1 supplies only `AlgebraicCycle.map` (proper pushforward) and no `AlgebraicCycle.pullback`, so the source and target of the presentation groupoid of a genuine smooth or étale atlas, smooth surjective and not open immersions, are outside the construction, and `OpenPresentationGroupoid` cannot yet be instantiated on `AlgebraicStack.chosenAtlasSelfOverlap`. Consequently atlas independence is proved only in the refinement form above: that the comparison map of an arbitrary smooth refinement is an isomorphism is exactly exactness of `0 → A(X) → A(U) ⇉ A(R)` for a smooth cover and needs both that flat pullback and its surjectivity (Fulton, Intersection Theory, Prop. 1.9 with descent), neither of which exists. `BΓ` is likewise unavailable as a Deligne--Mumford stack, since `classifyingPrestack` is only a prestack until fppf descent for action torsors is proved, so the degree theorem is stated for the one-point atlas with stabilizer group `Γ` that such a presentation would supply; and the weighted degree descends to Chow classes only where rational equivalence is trivial, general descent being the degree theorem `deg div(f) = 0`, which rests on the same missing norm/`Ring.ord`-additivity input already recorded for general proper pushforward. Stack cycle generators, rational functions, and atlas divisor calculations in `StackChow.lean` remain experimental and their uncertified quotient and all stack pullback, positive-rank Gysin, and Chern operations are inactive; `IntersectionTheory/ChernClasses.lean` now adds the vector-bundle flat pullback with a proved dimension shift: for an affine vector bundle `E = Spec A → Spec R` over a Noetherian ring, trivialized by an `R`-algebra isomorphism `A ≃ₐ[R] MvPolynomial ι R` (the bundle `Spec Sym F` of a finite free module of `Cones/Graded.lean` is such, via `VectorBundle.symTrivialization`), the generic point of the preimage of a subvariety is constructed as the extended prime `p·A` (`VectorBundle.bundlePrime`), proved prime, proved to lie over `p`, proved injective in `p` and proved independent of the trivialization, and its closure dimension is proved to exceed that of the subvariety by the rank (`VectorBundle.coheight_bundlePrime`, from Mathlib's Krull dimension of a polynomial ring over a Noetherian ring applied to `R ⧸ p`); identifying the specialization order on the points of an affine scheme with the dual of the inclusion order on primes (`VectorBundle.specOrderIso`) transports this to the certified `DimensionFunction` gradings (`VectorBundle.dimension_bundlePoint`), so the grading of the resulting map is a theorem and not a hypothesis; the flat pullback of cycles `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackBundle` is constructed with all multiplicities one, proved locally finite, rational-linear and injective, and assembled into the graded map `cyclesOfDimension.flatPullbackBundle : Z_i(X) →ₗ[ℚ] Z_{i+r}(E)` (`symFlatPullback` for `Spec Sym F`, whose rank is proved to be `finrank R F`);
