invariance under isomorphism of bundles is proved, not assumed: an isomorphism of coordinate algebras induces an isomorphism of total spaces (`specIsoOfAlgEquiv`), the two certified gradings are proved to agree along it (`dimension_comap_algEquiv`), the bundle pullbacks are proved to correspond (`pullbackBundle_algEquiv`, `flatPullbackOpen_flatPullbackBundle`), and the two flat pullbacks along the mutually inverse open immersions are proved mutually inverse, giving an isomorphism of rational Chow groups (`VectorBundle.chowEquivOfAlgEquiv`); on the zero section, the vertex section is proved to be a closed immersion because an `R`-algebra map to `R` is automatically surjective, giving the Chow pushforward `VectorBundle.zeroSectionPushforward : A_i(X) →ₗ[ℚ] A_i(E)`, and for a closed immersion which is also an open immersion, flat pullback is proved to be a retraction of the pushforward (`cyclesOfDimension.flatPullbackOpen_properPushforward`, `openImmersionPullback_pushforward_eq_id`, specialized in `VectorBundle.zeroSectionGysin`), which is the self-intersection formula in the case where the top Chern class is `1`; in rank zero the structure map is proved surjective, every point of the total space is proved to be the generic point of the preimage of its image, the bundle pullback is proved to coincide with the existing open-immersion flat pullback along the projection, the projection and zero section are proved to be mutually inverse isomorphisms, and the shift is `0`, so the positive-rank construction genuinely restricts to the rank-zero identity of `Gysin.lean`; what is still missing is the descent of `flatPullbackBundle` to rational equivalence, and with it homotopy invariance, the positive-rank zero-section Gysin inverse, Chern classes, the projective-bundle formula and Whitney sum; the order-of-vanishing obstruction to this descent is now removed by `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackDescent.lean`: `Ring.ord_algebraMap_of_flat_local_of_map_maximalIdeal` and `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.ord_flatLocalFunctionFieldMap` prove that a flat local extension with `m_R · S = m_S` preserves the order of vanishing without any unramifiedness (so the transcendental residue extension of the bundle stalk is allowed; the polynomial localisation is instantiated in `VectorBundle.ord_algebraMap_localizationAtPrime_map_C`), `VectorBundle.coheight_bundlePoint` proves that the generic point of a preimage has the same codimension (flatness gives going down), `VectorBundle.ord_bundlePoint` proves the equality of orders at every bundle point, and `VectorBundle.pullbackBundle_principalCycle` proves the principal-divisor comparison `π^* div(f) = div(π^* f)`, hence `VectorBundle.pullbackBundle_principalCycle_mem_totalRationalRelations`, for every generator whose integral closed subscheme is the whole base `Spec R` (`R` a Noetherian domain); `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackChow.lean` supplies exactly that presentation and completes the descent: an arbitrary `IntegralClosedSubscheme` of `Spec R` is presented as `Spec (R ⧸ p)` with `p` prime (`IsClosedImmersion.Spec_iff`, `IsIntegral.of_isIso`, `affine_isIntegral_iff`, `Ideal.Quotient.isDomain_iff_prime`), its restricted bundle is the polynomial algebra `MvPolynomial ι (R ⧸ p)` with the surjection `VectorBundle.quotBundleHom : A ↠ MvPolynomial ι (R ⧸ p)` whose kernel is proved to be the extended ideal `p · A` (`VectorBundle.ker_quotBundleHom`), and the resulting closed immersion `E_V ⟶ E` identifies `E_V` with the preimage of `V` on cycle-carrying points by the ideal identity `VectorBundle.comap_quotBundleHom_map_algebraMap` and its geometric form `VectorBundle.quotBundleImmersion_base_bundlePoint`; the compatibility `π^* ∘ V_* = (E_V)_* ∘ π_V^*` of `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackBundle` with residue-degree pushforward along the two closed immersions is proved (`VectorBundle.pullbackBundle_map_quotImmersion`), and combined with `VectorBundle.pullbackBundle_principalCycle` over the Noetherian domain `R ⧸ p` and the transport of a principal cycle along the presentation isomorphism (`map_isIso_principalCycle`) it exhibits the pullback of every principal-divisor generator as a principal-divisor generator on the total space (`VectorBundle.pullbackBundle_divisor_mem_totalRationalRelations`), hence `VectorBundle.totalRationalRelations_pullbackBundle`, the `DescendingMap` `VectorBundle.ofBundle` and the flat pullback on rational Chow groups `VectorBundle.chowPullbackBundle : A_i(Spec R) →ₗ[ℚ] A_{i+r}(E)` with `chowPullbackBundle_quotientMap`, proved invariant under isomorphism of bundles (`VectorBundle.chowEquivOfAlgEquiv_comp_chowPullbackBundle`) and therefore independent of the trivialization; the base is not assumed irreducible or reduced, only `R` Noetherian and the rank finite. Still missing on top of this are homotopy invariance (surjectivity of `chowPullbackBundle`), the positive-rank zero-section Gysin inverse, and with them Chern classes, the projective-bundle formula and the Whitney sum formula. `IntersectionTheory/EtalePullback.lean` adds the flat pullback along étale morphisms: `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackEtale` pulls back rational cycles along an arbitrary étale morphism with all multiplicities one, local finiteness of the support being proved from local quasi-finiteness (`Curves.locallyQuasiFinite_of_etale`) by restricting to an affine chart over an affine chart, where the morphism is quasi-finite and quasi-compact; additivity, ℚ-linearity, functoriality (`pullbackEtale_id`, `pullbackEtale_comp`), agreement with `pullbackOpen` on open immersions and injectivity for surjective morphisms are proved, and `cyclesOfDimension.flatPullbackEtale` is the graded version under the explicit relative-dimension-zero hypothesis; `height_le_height_base_of_etale` and `DimensionFunction.apply_le_of_etale` prove unconditionally that the closure dimension does not decrease along an étale morphism, the reverse inequality being proved for specializing étale morphisms (`DimensionFunction.apply_eq_of_etale_of_specializingMap`) and false in general since an open immersion is étale, so `EtalePresentationGroupoid` keeps the two relative-dimension-zero statements as data exactly as `OpenPresentationGroupoid` and carries the Vistoli package (`cycles`, `mem_cycles_iff`, `relations`, `chow`, `quotientMap`, `chowEquivSchemeChow`), with `OpenPresentationGroupoid.toEtale` showing that the two theories agree (`toEtale_cycles`, `toEtaleChowEquiv`); finally `DeligneMumfordStack.chosenEtaleAtlasSelfOverlap` produces the arrow scheme `R = U ×_X U` of a chosen étale surjective atlas with both projections proved étale and surjective, and `DeligneMumfordStack.etalePresentation`/`vistoliChow` give the first genuine Vistoli rational Chow group of a stack in the repository, conditional only on the two dimension gradings and their leg-compatibility, which `etalePresentationOfSpecializing` supplies from specializing legs. Still missing is the pullback of rational equivalence along étale morphisms (a comparison of orders of vanishing on a possibly non-integral étale cover), without which the Vistoli groups have no refinement functoriality and the descent sequence `0 → A(X) → A(U) ⇉ A(R)` cannot be shown exact. `IntersectionTheory/LocalizationExact.lean` completes the localization sequence in the middle: restriction of a cycle along a closed immersion is constructed (`AlgebraicCycle.pullbackClosed`) and a cycle vanishing off the image of a closed immersion is proved to be the pushforward of its restriction, in graded form `cyclesOfDimension.properPushforward_pullbackClosed`; the closure step of Fulton, Prop. 1.8, is proved rather than assumed: the scheme-theoretic image of `Z → V → X` is proved reduced, irreducible and locally Noetherian (`Scheme.isReduced_image`, `irreducibleSpace_image`, `isLocallyNoetherian_image`), hence an integral closed subscheme `IntegralClosedSubscheme.closureIn`, the comparison from `Z` to the trace of that closure on `V` is an isomorphism (`isIso_traceLift`, from the lemma that a closed immersion with dense image into a reduced scheme is an isomorphism), so `Z → Z̄` is a dominant open immersion with range exactly the part of `Z̄` over `V`, its function field is canonically identified (`Scheme.dominantFunctionFieldEquiv`), and `RationalFunctionGenerator.pullbackOpen_divisor_closureIn` proves that the principal divisor of the closed-up generator restricts to the given one; consequently `totalRationalRelations_le_map_pullbackOpen` proves, for a Noetherian `X`, that every principal-divisor relation on an open subscheme is the restriction of a relation on `X`, and `RationalEquivalenceSystem.DescendingMap.ker_openImmersionPullback_le_range` with its specialisations proves `ker(A_i(X) → A_i(U)) ≤ im(A_i(Y) → A_i(X))`, `ker_openImmersionPullback_eq_range_of_homogeneous` giving the exactness equality together with the composite-zero statement, plus the corollary `openImmersionPullback_bijective_of_forall_ne` that restriction is an isomorphism when the complement carries no point in dimension `i`. Exactly one input is still assumed, as an explicit theorem hypothesis: that the ungraded canonical span of principal divisors is graded (`hproject`, implied by `PrincipalDivisorsHomogeneous` via `project_mem_totalRationalRelations_of_homogeneous`), the catenarity/equidimensionality statement that a Weil divisor on an integral closed subscheme `W` lives in dimension `dim W − 1`, which Mathlib v4.33.1 lacks for general schemes and which `ChowGroup.lean` deliberately avoids in its definition of rational equivalence (Round 14.) `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyRankOne.lean` proves the rank-one core of Fulton's Proposition 1.9 with no hypothesis beyond Noetherianity of the base: for a point `V` of the trivial line bundle `Spec R[X]` which dominates its image `W` in `Spec R` without being the whole preimage of `W`, `VectorBundle.exists_rankOne_generator` produces a rational function on the integral closed subscheme `Spec (R/p)[X]` whose principal divisor has coefficient `1` at `V` and coefficient `0` at every other point over the generic point of `W`; the commutative-algebra core `VectorBundle.exists_localGenerator` passes to the principal ideal domain `K[X]` over the fraction field, clears denominators, and shows that the chosen element generates the prime in every localisation and lies in no other prime with zero contraction, while `VectorBundle.ord_algebraMap_eq_one` and `VectorBundle.ord_algebraMap_eq_zero_of_notMem` compute Mathlib's `Scheme.ord` on an affine scheme from `Ring.ord` of the local ring. `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopy.lean` proves Fulton's Proposition 1.9 for a trivialised affine vector bundle `π : Spec A → Spec R` with `A ≃ₐ[R] MvPolynomial ι R`, `ι` finite and `R` Noetherian: `VectorBundle.chowPullbackBundle_surjective` shows that the flat pullback `chowPullbackBundle e dimX dimE i RX RE : A_i(Spec R) →ₗ[ℚ] A_{i+r}(Spec A)` is surjective. The proof runs entirely at the level of cycles, through the predicate `VectorBundle.BundleSurjective` whose rank is a free integer parameter, and is an induction on `Nat.card ι` (`VectorBundle.bundleSurjective_of_card`): rank zero is `bundleSurjective_of_isEmpty`, rank one over an arbitrary Noetherian ring is `bundleSurjective_polynomial` (a Noetherian induction on the image of the base point of a subvariety, using well-foundedness of strict reverse inclusion of primes), and the step factors `MvPolynomial ι R` as `Polynomial (MvPolynomial ι' R)` and composes the two flat pullbacks (`pullbackBundle_tower`, `bundleSurjective_of_algEquiv`, `baseDimension`, `principalDivisorsHomogeneous_base`). Two inputs are explicit hypotheses and are not proved in this file: `VectorBundle.RankOneKey`, the rank-one key lemma, which is proved separately in `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyRankOne.lean` as `VectorBundle.exists_rankOne_generator`, and `PrincipalDivisorsHomogeneous (Spec (CommRingCat.of A)) dimE`, homogeneity of principal divisors on the total space, the same hypothesis carried by the localisation sequence of `IntersectionTheory/LocalizationExact.lean`. From surjectivity the file derives `VectorBundle.exists_pullback_eq` and the first isomorphism `VectorBundle.chowQuotientEquiv : A_i(Spec R) ⧸ ker π^* ≃ₗ[ℚ] A_{i+r}(Spec A)`, both unconditional apart from those two hypotheses. The zero-section Gysin map `VectorBundle.zeroSectionGysinEquiv`, with `zeroSectionGysinEquiv_pullback`, `pullback_zeroSectionGysinEquiv` and `eq_zeroSectionGysinEquiv_of_pullback_eq`, is defined as the inverse of `π^*` under the *additional explicit hypothesis* that `π^*` is injective; injectivity is the other half of homotopy invariance (Fulton, Theorem 3.3(a)) and needs Chern classes of vector bundles, which this development does not yet have, so it remains an input rather than a theorem. The rank-one key lemma is discharged (`VectorBundle.rankOneKey`), so `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyKey.lean` restates Fulton's Proposition 1.9 with no hypothesis other than homogeneity of principal divisors on the total space: `VectorBundle.chowPullbackBundle_surjective'` and `VectorBundle.exists_pullback_eq'` (every rational Chow class on a trivialised affine vector bundle over a Noetherian ring is a flat pullback from the base), `VectorBundle.chowQuotientEquiv'` (`A_i(X) ⧸ ker π^* ≃ A_{i+r}(E)`), and the zero-section Gysin isomorphism `VectorBundle.zeroSectionGysin'` inverse to `π^*` under the explicit hypothesis that `π^*` is injective, which is full homotopy invariance (Fulton, Theorem 3.3(a)); that injectivity needs Chern classes of vector bundles and remains the one missing input on the intersection-theory side of the virtual class. (Round 15.) `IntersectionTheory/LocalOrdSymmetry.lean` proves Fulton's symmetric local identity (*Intersection Theory*, Appendix A.2–A.3) unconditionally: `VectorBundle.localOrdSymmetry : VectorBundle.LocalOrdSymmetry` says that for a Noetherian domain `D`, a prime `Q` admitting no chain `⊥ < q' < q < Q`, and nonzero `a, s` whose only common prime below `Q` is `Q` itself, the two intersection numbers `∑_{q<Q, s∈q} ord_{D_q}(s)·ord_{D_Q/qD_Q}(a)` and `∑_{q<Q, a∈q} ord_{D_q}(a)·ord_{D_Q/qD_Q}(s)` agree. The proof is self-contained: the multiplicity pair `chiCoker b M = length (M/bM)`, `chiKer b M = length (ker (b·))` is shown to be additive along short exact sequences (`chi_add_submodule`, an explicit four-map replacement for the snake lemma, formulated additively so that no subtraction in `ℕ∞` occurs); `devissage` then proves Fulton's Lemma A.2.7 by Noetherian induction on ideals using associated primes and the exactness of localization (`lengthAt`), in a relative form over a base ring so that no length has to be transported between a ring and a quotient; `local_key` specialises this to a Noetherian local domain `A` of "dimension at most two", and Koszul symmetry (`chiKer_quotient_comm`, via the relation module `{(x,y) | a x = s y}`) identifies the correction terms of the two sides. The passage from `D` to `A = D_Q` uses Krull's principal ideal theorem to rule out that the maximal ideal is minimal over `(s)`, the order-isomorphism between primes of `D_Q` and primes of `D` below `Q`, and the iterated localization isomorphism `(D_Q)_P ≅ D_{P∩D}` (`ord_comap_eq`). The general helpers `ord_ringEquiv` (invariance of `Ring.ord` under ring isomorphisms) and `length_subtype_eq_of_smul` / `length_quotient_eq_of_smul` (length over a ring and over a quotient ring) are available for reuse; nothing in the file is left as a hypothesis. The new file `IntersectionTheory/BundleSectionGysin.lean` constructs the cycle-level Gysin map of a constant section `σ_c : Spec R → Spec R[T]` of the trivial line bundle and establishes its two defining properties. `VectorBundle.sectionMap` is the spectrum of `Polynomial.evalRingHom c` and is a closed immersion; `VectorBundle.sectionRestrict` restricts cycles along it, and `VectorBundle.sectionGysin` is the resulting Gysin map `z ↦ ∑ᶠ V, z V · σ_c^{-1}(div_V (T - c))`. `VectorBundle.sectionGysin_pullbackBundle` proves unconditionally that this map inverts the flat pullback `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackBundle (polyTrivialization R)` on cycles, the key input being `VectorBundle.sectionRestrict_divisor_sectionPoly`, which shows that the section meets each fibre transversally in a single point of height one. On the relations side, `VectorBundle.exists_generator_eq_elementGenerator_sub` writes every principal divisor on the total space as a difference of the divisors of two polynomials on a single subvariety, and `VectorBundle.sectionRestrict_elementGenerator_mem` shows that the restriction along `σ_c` of a principal divisor carried by a subvariety contained in the section is again a principal divisor on the base, because such a subvariety is a closed subscheme of the base (`VectorBundle.sectionQuotientHom`). `VectorBundle.divisor_sectionPoly_support` identifies the support of the divisor of `T - c` on a subvariety `V(P)` with the set of components of `V(P) ∩ σ_c(X)`. Combining these, `VectorBundle.sectionGysin_elementGenerator_mem` proves statement (B) of the injectivity programme, but only modulo an explicit hypothesis `hident`, the cycle-level symmetric local identity; deriving `hident` from `VectorBundle.LocalOrdSymmetry` needs the identification of the stalks of `Spec D` and `Spec (D ⧸ q)` with localisations and is not done here. The file contains no `sorry` and no new axiom, and the copy of `VectorBundle.LocalOrdSymmetry` in it must be deleted when the companion file proving it is imported. The new file `IntersectionTheory/BundleSectionGysinIdentity.lean` removes the last gap in statement (B) of the injectivity programme: it derives the cycle-level identity which agent I2's `VectorBundle.sectionGysin_elementGenerator_mem` had to assume from Fulton's symmetric local identity in a two-dimensional local domain. `VectorBundle.scheme_ord_eq_localization` identifies Mathlib's `Scheme.ord` of a global section of an integral Noetherian affine scheme at a codimension-one point with the length-theoretic `Ring.ord` of the localisation of the coordinate ring, using the invariance of `Ring.ord` under ring isomorphisms (`VectorBundle.SectionGysinIdentity.ring_ord_ringEquiv`) and uniqueness of localisations; `VectorBundle.SectionGysinIdentity.ring_ord_quot_quot` identifies the order of vanishing on the subvariety `V(V)` at a point `Q` with the order of vanishing in the quotient `(R[T] ⧸ P)_Q ⧸ (V ⧸ P)(R[T] ⧸ P)_Q`, which is the shape occurring in `VectorBundle.LocalOrdSymmetry`. On the geometric side `VectorBundle.quotPointMap` indexes the points of `V(P)` by the primes of `R[T] ⧸ P` and lets both cycle-level sums be reindexed over `PrimeSpectrum (R[T] ⧸ P)`; `VectorBundle.exists_nat_termProduct` then matches each summand with the corresponding summand `VectorBundle.symTerm` of the local identity, and `VectorBundle.termProduct_eq_zero_of_chain_failure` shows that all summands vanish when the local chain condition fails, the catenarity input being `VectorBundle.SectionGysinIdentity.height_eq_two_ambient`, deduced from the dimension formula `HasDimensionFormula` for the prime quotients of `R[T]`. The resulting theorem `VectorBundle.sectionGysin_elementGenerator_eq_finsum` is exactly the hypothesis `hident`, and `VectorBundle.sectionGysin_elementGenerator_mem'` is statement (B) with `hident` discharged; its only remaining hypotheses are `hsym : VectorBundle.LocalOrdSymmetry` (proved in the companion file, which cannot be imported here because it is written concurrently) and `hdim : ∀ P prime, HasDimensionFormula (Polynomial R ⧸ P)`. The file contains no `sorry` and no new axiom. `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopyInjective.lean` completes homotopy invariance of the rational Chow groups of a trivialised affine vector bundle over a Noetherian ring, proving the injectivity half that round 14 had to assume. Two hypotheses on the base ring are isolated: `VectorBundle.UnitDifferences R` (an infinite subset with unit pairwise differences, supplied by `VectorBundle.unitDifferences_of_field` for any nontrivial algebra over an infinite field) and `VectorBundle.HasUniversalDimensionFormula R` (the dimension formula for every prime quotient of every finitely generated polynomial algebra over `R`); both are inherited by `MvPolynomial ι' R` (`unitDifferences_mvPolynomial`, `HasUniversalDimensionFormula.mvPolynomial`), which is what makes the induction on the rank possible. `VectorBundle.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_of_dimensionFormula` shows that the dimension formula forces every principal divisor to be homogeneous — its support consists of the height-one points of the subvariety `V(P)` carrying it (`VectorBundle.exists_quotient_divisor_form`, `VectorBundle.exists_height_one_of_map_principalCycle`), and the dimension formula gives each of them dimension `dim V(P) - 1` (`VectorBundle.dimension_add_one_eq_ringKrullDim`) — which discharges the hypothesis `hhom` of round 14's surjectivity theorem. In rank one, `VectorBundle.bundleInjective_polynomial` restricts a relation along a general constant section: the relation is decomposed into finitely many generators, each written as a difference of two polynomial divisors on a subvariety (I2's `exists_generator_eq_elementGenerator_sub`); `VectorBundle.exists_good_translate` chooses a constant `c` whose section avoids all the subvarieties and all the primes minimal over `P + (a)` and `P + (b)`, and `VectorBundle.hgood_of_avoid` deduces from Krull's principal ideal theorem (`VectorBundle.height_map_eq_one_of_mem_minimalPrimes`) the good-position hypothesis of Fulton's symmetric local identity; the section Gysin map of `IntersectionTheory/BundleSectionGysin.lean` then returns the cycle itself on one side (`sectionGysin_pullbackBundle`) and a sum of principal divisors on the other (`sectionGysin_elementGenerator_mem'`, with `hsym` discharged by `VectorBundle.localOrdSymmetry`). `VectorBundle.bundleInjective_of_card` runs the induction on the rank and `VectorBundle.chowPullbackBundle_injective`, `chowPullbackBundle_bijective`, `chowPullbackBundle_equiv` and `zeroSectionGysinEquiv''` give the resulting isomorphism `A_i(Spec R) ≃ A_{i+r}(E)` and its inverse, the zero-section Gysin map, with no remaining hypothesis beyond `UnitDifferences R` and `HasUniversalDimensionFormula R`. The file contains no `sorry` and no new axiom. (Round 17.) `IntersectionTheory/CycleGluing.lean` makes rational algebraic cycles a Zariski-local notion. `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackOpen_eq_zero_of_forall` and `AlgebraicCycle.ext_of_cover` show that a cycle is determined by its restrictions to the members of an open cover, and `CycleGluing.glue` constructs the cycle glued from a `CycleGluing.Compatible` family, with `CycleGluing.pullbackOpen_glue` and `CycleGluing.glue_unique` characterising it; local finiteness of the glued support is proved by pushing a locally finite neighbourhood forward along the open embedding `(U i).ι`. `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackOpen_fundamentalCycle` proves that the fundamental cycle of a locally Noetherian scheme restricts to the fundamental cycle of any open subscheme; its two ingredients, `CycleGluing.isMax_map_iff` (an open immersion matches generic points on both sides of the specialisation order) and `CycleGluing.genericLength_eq_of_isOpenImmersion` (the generic multiplicities agree, via the stalk isomorphism and the new `CycleGluing.length_self_eq_of_ringEquiv`), are proved unconditionally. The graded counterparts `cyclesOfDimension.flatPullbackOpen_project`, `cyclesOfDimension.flatPullbackOpen_fundamental` and `CycleGluing.glue_mem_cyclesOfDimension` carry the usual relative-dimension-zero hypothesis `hdim` of `cyclesOfDimension.flatPullbackOpen`, and `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackOpen_map_fundamentalCycle` shows that the pushforward of a fundamental cycle along a closed immersion restricts to the pushforward of the fundamental cycle of the trace `g ⁻¹ᵁ V = Z ×_X V`. Finally `CycleGluing.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_restrict` proves that homogeneity of principal divisors descends to open subschemes of a Noetherian scheme unconditionally, while the converse `CycleGluing.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_of_cover` needs the explicit hypothesis `hcommon` (any two points where a principal divisor is nonzero lie in a common member of the cover); without it the local dimensions attached to different members of the cover cannot be compared, and closing that gap would require catenarity of `X`. No gluing statement at the level of Chow groups is proved: the rational-equivalence relations are not known to be Zariski-local. `IntersectionTheory/HomogeneityLocal.lean` makes homogeneity of principal divisors a Zariski-local property. The local condition is `HomogeneityLocal.CovByDimension dimension`, namely that the certified dimension grading drops by exactly one along the covering relation `x ⋖ η` of the specialisation order; `HomogeneityLocal.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_of_covByDimension` proves unconditionally that it implies `PrincipalDivisorsHomogeneous X dimension`, which removes the auxiliary hypothesis `hcommon` of `CycleGluing.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_of_cover`. The proof combines the new order lemma `HomogeneityLocal.coheight_eq_one_iff_covBy` (a point of a preorder with a greatest element has coheight one exactly when it is covered by that element) with `HomogeneityLocal.covBy_map_of_isClosedImmersion`, so that the whole support of the divisor of a rational function on an integral closed subscheme is covered by the image of its generic point. Locality in both directions is `HomogeneityLocal.covByDimension_restrict` and `HomogeneityLocal.covByDimension_of_cover`, proved from `HomogeneityLocal.covBy_map_of_isOpenImmersion` and `HomogeneityLocal.covBy_of_covBy_map`. On an affine scheme `HomogeneityLocal.covByDimension_of_dimensionFormula` derives the criterion from the dimension formula for all prime quotients of the coordinate ring, by exhibiting the smaller point of a covering pair as a height-one prime of the quotient and feeding this into `VectorBundle.dimension_add_one_eq_ringKrullDim`; no Noetherian hypothesis is used. The corollaries `HomogeneityLocal.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_of_affineCover`, `HomogeneityLocal.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_of_charts` and `HomogeneityLocal.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_totalSpace` give homogeneity for an arbitrary scheme with an affine cover whose coordinate rings satisfy the dimension formula, in particular for the total space of a `VectorBundleTotalSpace.BundleData X ι` whose base charts satisfy `VectorBundle.HasUniversalDimensionFormula`. Missing: nothing is proved about *constructing* the chart dimension functions or their compatibility `hdim`, and there is still no statement that `CovByDimension` holds for a finite type scheme over a field without going through an affine cover. `IntersectionTheory/FiniteTypeDimension.lean` constructs the canonical dimension function of a scheme locally of finite type over a field and, with it, discharges the chart hypotheses that the round-17 files carry. For a structure morphism `f : X ⟶ Spec k` the file defines `FiniteTypeDimension.resTrdeg f x`, the transcendence degree over `k` of the residue field at `x`, and proves three things about it: it is invariant under open immersions (`resTrdeg_comp`, from `Scheme.Hom.residueFieldMap` being an isomorphism); on an affine scheme it computes the height of a point (`resTrdeg_spec`, from `GromovWitten.Algebra.PrimeSpectrum.coheight_eq_toENat_trdeg_residueField` and the `Spec.residueFieldIso` dictionary); and it is finite (`resTrdeg_ne_top`, from `ringKrullDim_eq_toENat_trdeg` and `trdeg_lt_aleph0_of_finiteType`). The global dimension formula `FiniteTypeDimension.height_eq_resTrdeg : Order.height x = resTrdeg f x` follows because every chain of specialisations ending at `x` lies inside a single affine open — an open subset is stable under generalisation — so the height of `x` is computed on any affine neighbourhood, and any two of them give the same transcendence degree. This yields `FiniteTypeDimension.dimensionFunction f : DimensionFunction X` together with `dimensionFunction_comp`, the compatibility with *every* open immersion, which is exactly the hypothesis `hdim` that `cyclesOfDimension.flatPullbackOpen` and all of `HomogeneityLocal.lean` carry and that nothing in the repository could previously supply. The corollaries are hypothesis-free: `hasUniversalDimensionFormula_sections` discharges `huniv` of `HomogeneityLocal.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_of_charts`, `principalDivisorsHomogeneous` proves `PrincipalDivisorsHomogeneous X (dimensionFunction f)`, `locallyOfFiniteType_proj` shows that the total space of a finite-rank `VectorBundleTotalSpace.BundleData X ι` is again locally of finite type over `k`, `dimensionFunction_bundlePoint` proves the dimension shift `dim (bundlePoint 𝓔 x) = dim x + Nat.card ι` (discharging `hshift` of report E), and `flatPullbackBundleFiniteType` is the resulting injective graded flat pullback `Z_i(X) → Z_{i+r}(E)` with no side conditions. Missing: nothing here descends to Chow groups, and the file says nothing about schemes that are not of finite type over a field — the `DimensionFunction`-versus-open-immersion gap is closed only in that setting (and it is genuinely false in general, e.g. for the spectrum of a discrete valuation ring). `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackGlobal.lean` constructs the flat pullback of rational algebraic cycles along the total space of a vector bundle over an arbitrary scheme. Its foundation is `VectorBundle.bundlePoint_specializes_iff`, which characterises the generic point `p · A` of a fibre of a trivialised affine bundle by its specialisations, so that `BundlePullbackGlobal.chartBundlePoint_congr` can prove that the fibre point computed in a chart does not depend on the chart (the two candidates specialise to each other and a scheme is `T0`); this closes the only open point left by `VectorBundleTotalSpace.lean`. Consequently `BundlePullbackGlobal.bundlePoint : X → 𝓔.totalSpace` is well defined, injective (`bundlePoint_injective`), lies over its base point (`proj_bundlePoint`) and is computed in every chart (`bundlePoint_proj_chartι`). `BundlePullbackGlobal.pullbackBundle` is the cycle carrying the coefficient `c x` at `bundlePoint 𝓔 x`; it is proved locally finite, rational linear (`pullbackBundleLinear`) and injective, `pullbackOpen_chartι_pullbackBundle` identifies its restriction to every trivialising chart with the affine `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackBundle`, and `ext_of_chartι`/`pullbackBundle_unique` show that this property characterises it. On graded cycles, `dimension_bundlePoint_chart` proves the shift `dim_E = dim_X + r` over a chart from the polynomial Krull-dimension formula, and `flatPullbackBundleGlobal` is the resulting injective linear map `Z_i(X) → Z_{i+r}(E)`, taking the global shift as the hypothesis `hshift` because the chart dimension functions are not canonically the restrictions of the global ones. What is missing: the descent of this pullback to rational equivalence (deliberately out of scope, and still missing in the affine case as well), the identification of `pullbackBundle 𝓔` of the fundamental cycle with the fundamental cycle of the total space, and the compatibility of the affine bundle pullback with localisation of the base, which the chart-independence proof turned out not to need. `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackFundamental.lean` proves that the global flat pullback of cycles along the total space of a vector bundle takes the fundamental cycle to the fundamental cycle: `BundlePullbackGlobal.pullbackBundle_fundamentalCycle` states `pullbackBundle 𝓔 X.fundamentalCycle = 𝓔.totalSpace.fundamentalCycle` for any `𝓔 : BundleData X ι` of finite rank over a locally Noetherian scheme, closing open item 3 of the report on `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackGlobal.lean`. The affine ingredient was already available as `VirtualFundamentalClass.VirtualClass.pullbackBundle_fundamentalCycle`, so the proof only has to combine it with `pullbackBundle_unique` and with the restriction lemma `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackOpen_fundamentalCycle` of `IntersectionTheory/CycleGluing.lean`, applied both to the chart of the total space and to the chart of the base; the same computation also discharges the hypothesis `hfc` left open by `VectorBundle.PullbackBaseChange.pullbackBundle_map_baseImm_fundamentalCycle`. Two structural instances are established on the way: `isLocallyNoetherian_totalSpace`, the total space of a finite-rank bundle over a locally Noetherian scheme is locally Noetherian (via the chart cover and the Hilbert basis theorem), which is what makes the fundamental cycle of the total space well typed, and `isNoetherianRing_chart`. `flatPullbackBundleGlobal_fundamental` is the graded corollary for pure dimension functions, under the same hypotheses `hshift`, `pureX`, `pureE` that are needed to write the graded objects down. Finally `chartCover` packages the trivialising charts as a `Scheme.OpenCover`, and `isReduced_totalSpace`, `irreducibleSpace_totalSpace` and `isIntegral_totalSpace` prove that the total space of a bundle over a reduced (resp. integral) scheme is reduced (resp. integral), irreducibility being obtained from `specializes_bundlePoint_genericPoint`: the generic point of the fibre over the generic point of `X` specialises to every point of the total space. Nothing here descends to Chow groups; as in the affine case, the compatibility of the flat pullback with rational equivalence remains unproved throughout the repository. `IntersectionTheory/BundleOverSubscheme.lean` constructs the restriction `E_V = E ×_X V` of a global vector bundle `𝓔 : BundleData X ι` to an integral closed subscheme `V` of the base, as an integral closed subscheme of the total space. `BundleOverSubscheme.restrictScheme` is the categorical fibre product; `isClosedImmersion_restrictι` and `isAffineHom_restrictProj` follow from stability of closed immersions and affine morphisms under base change, and the zero section of `𝓔` restricts to a section `restrictZero` of the projection, so `restrictProj` is surjective and dominant (`isDominant_restrictProj`). The local structure is completely determined: `isPullback_specMap_mvPolynomialMap` proves that affine space is stable under base change of the base ring, pasting it with `isPullback_chartHom` (the trace of `V` on an affine open is the `Spec` of the restriction of coordinate rings) and Task B's `BundleData.isPullback_chart` yields `isPullback_chartιRestrict`, which exhibits `Spec (MvPolynomial ι Γ(V, W_j))` as the part of `E_V` lying over the `j`-th chart of `E`, with `opensRange_chartιRestrict` and `iSup_opensRange_chartιRestrict` showing these charts are the preimages of the charts of `E` and cover `E_V`; `surjective_chartHom`, `chartQuotEquiv` and `chartQuotSpecIso_hom_quotBundleImmersion` rewrite the chart in the form `Spec (MvPolynomial ι (Γ(X, U_j) ⧸ p_j))` used by the affine theory of `BundlePullbackChow.lean`. Consequently `E_V` is locally Noetherian and reduced (chart by chart) and irreducible, the last from the new fact `specializes_genericBundlePoint`: the generic point of the fibre of `E` over the generic point of `V` lies in `E_V` and specialises to every one of its points, proved from `BundlePullbackGlobal.chartBundlePoint_specializes_iff`; hence `isIntegral_restrictScheme`, `restrictSubscheme 𝓔 V : IntegralClosedSubscheme 𝓔.totalSpace`, the description `restrictι_genericPoint` of the generic point, and the function-field map `pullbackFunctionField`. What is missing is the arithmetic on cycles: the identity `pullbackBundle 𝓔 (V.pushforward (principalCycle f)) = (restrictSubscheme 𝓔 V).pushforward (principalCycle (pullbackFunctionField f))` and the descent of the global bundle pullback to rational Chow groups are not proved, because the chart-wise comparison of `pullbackFunctionField` with the affine `Scheme.dominantFunctionFieldMap` of `GradedCone.projection` is not established. `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackGlobalChow.lean` proves the comparison `π^* (ι_V)_* div(f) = (ι_{E_V})_* div(π_V^* f)` for the flat pullback along the total space of a global vector bundle `𝓔 : BundleData X ι` and descends that pullback to rational Chow groups, closing open items 1–3 of the report on `BundleOverSubscheme.lean`. Two general facts are established first: `dominantFunctionFieldMap_comp`, the functoriality of the canonical function-field pullback of dominant morphisms of integral schemes (proved from `Scheme.Hom.stalkMap_comp` after `subst`ing the two generic-point identifications), which neither Mathlib nor the repository had; and `VectorBundle.comap_mvPolynomialMap_map_C`, the identity `comap (MvPolynomial.map φ) (map C P) = map C (comap φ P)` for a surjective `φ`, whose geometric form `VectorBundle.bundlePoint_specMap` says that the affine space over a closed subscheme of the base is exactly the preimage of that subscheme at the generic points of the fibres. Combining the latter with `BundlePullbackGlobal.bundlePoint_proj_chartι` gives `BundleOverSubscheme.restrictι_chartιRestrict_bundlePoint`, the chart description of the generic points of the fibres of `E_V = E ×_X V`, and combining the former with G1's square `chartιRestrict_restrictProj` gives `BundleOverSubscheme.pullbackFunctionField_chart`, the chart compatibility of `pullbackFunctionField` with `Scheme.dominantFunctionFieldMap (GradedCone.projection Γ(V,W_j) (MvPolynomial ι Γ(V,W_j)))` (the two projections are *equal*, by `chartQuotProj_eq_projection`). With the chart instances `isDomain_chartOpen`, `isIntegral_chartBundle`, `isDominant_chartQuotProj`, `isDominant_chartιRestrict` (all conditional on the chart meeting `V`, which is `nonempty_chartOpen`) the affine comparison `VectorBundle.pullbackBundle_principalCycle` then yields the pointwise identity `pullbackBundle_pushforward_principalCycle_apply`, and hence the cycle identity `pullbackBundle_pushforward_principalCycle`, unconditionally. From it `pullbackBundle_divisor_mem_totalRationalRelations` and `totalRationalRelations_pullbackBundleGlobal` show that the global flat pullback preserves the canonical span of principal divisors, so `ofBundleGlobal` is a `DescendingMap` and `chowPullbackBundleGlobal` is the induced map `A_i(X) →ₗ[ℚ] A_{i+r}(E)`, compatible on every trivialising chart with the affine `VectorBundle.chowPullbackBundle` (`openImmersionPullback_chowPullbackBundleGlobal`). What is still missing is upstream: the dimension-shift hypothesis `hshift` of `BundlePullbackGlobal.flatPullbackBundleGlobal` and the chart dimension hypotheses `hU`, `hP` are still hypotheses, because a `DimensionFunction` is not canonically compatible with open immersions anywhere in this repository. (Round 18.) Global homotopy property of vector bundles (Fulton, Prop. 1.9 and Thm. 3.3(a)). Surjectivity: `IntersectionTheory/SupportedCycles.lean` (cycles and principal-divisor spans supported in a closed subset), `BundleHomotopyClosed.lean` (`VectorBundle.exists_pullback_supported`: the affine Fulton 1.9 on `Spec R` for cycles supported over `V(I)`, with the base cycle supported in `V(I)` and the relation generated by divisors supported over `V(I)`, obtained on `Spec (R ⧸ I)` and pushed forward), `BundleHomotopyGlobal.lean` (`BundlePullbackGlobal.exists_pullback_supported` by well-founded induction on the closed subsets of a Noetherian `X`, `chowPullbackBundleGlobal_surjective` for every `𝓔 : BundleData X ι` over a compact locally Noetherian base with the universal dimension formula on the charts, and `chowPullbackBundleFiniteType_surjective`/`mem_range_chowPullbackBundleFiniteType` with no hypothesis for `X` compact and locally of finite type over a field). Injectivity, for bundles with a global trivialisation over a compact scheme locally of finite type over an infinite field: `GeneratorInvariance.lean` (`divisor_eq_of_residueFunction_eq`: the principal divisor of a generator depends only on the image of the generic point and the function in its residue field; `divisor_mul`, compatibilities with `restrictOpen`, `closureIn`, `closedImage`) and `GeneratorInvarianceAffine.lean` (residue functions of the affine generators `elementGenerator`/`sectionGenerator`, `divisor_sub_eq_of_residueFunction_mul_eq`); `BundleSectionGysinExplicit.lean` (the affine section Gysin of a generator as an explicit finite sum of `sectionGenerator` divisors for a constant avoiding finitely many ideals, and finiteness of the bad constants from a field); `LineBundleInjective.lean` (injectivity for any rank when one chart trivialises the bundle over all of `X`; canonical generators `pointGenerator`), `LineBundleInjectiveGlobal.lean` (the global Gysin map `globalGysin` of the constant section `t = c` on `X × 𝔸^1`, glued from the affine `sectionGysin`, with `globalGysin_pullbackBundle` and chart independence of the class of `t - c`), `LineBundleInjectiveGysin.lean` and `LineBundleInjectiveRelation.lean` (the Gysin image of a principal divisor is a global relation: a finite sum of `pointGenerator` divisors indexed by the points of `s_c⁻¹(W)`, checked pointwise on charts; the rank-one theorem `pullbackBundle_injective_rankOne` and `rankOneInjective k`), and `BundleHomotopyInjectiveGlobal.lean` (rank induction through the tower `X × 𝔸^{Option ι'} ≅ (X × 𝔸^{ι'}) × 𝔸^1`, `pullbackBundle_trivialData_injective`, `pullbackBundle_injective_of_globalTrivialisation`, `chowPullbackBundleGlobal_injective` under `[Infinite k]`). Injectivity for a bundle without a global trivialisation (Fulton 3.3(a) in general) needs Chern classes of line bundles and the projective bundle formula and is not attempted. The first Chern class of a line bundle is constructed on cycles and, modulo the descent statement, on Chow groups: `IntersectionTheory/LineBundleData.lean` gives Čech line bundle data on affine opens (`LineBundleData`, `trivial`, `tensor`, `inv`, `dual`), rational sections of the restriction to an integral closed subscheme with chart coordinates (`RationalSection`, `coord`), the chart-independent order of vanishing at a codimension-one point (`ord`, `ord_well_defined`) and the divisor cycle (`divisorCycle`, `divisor`), with additivity under scaling and tensoring, agreement with `Scheme.principalCycle` on the trivial bundle, and the fact that two rational sections have divisors differing by a `RationalFunctionGenerator.divisor` (`divisor_mem_totalRationalRelations_of_sub`). `IntersectionTheory/FirstChernClass.lean` defines `c1Cycle L : Z_{i+1}(X) → A_i(X)` (`c1Cycle_single`: the class of the divisor of any rational section on the closure of the point), the descent statement `KillsRelations L dim i` (Fulton Prop. 2.5) and, given it, `c1 L : A_{i+1}(X) → A_i(X)` by `Submodule.liftQ`, with `c1_trivial = 0`, `c1_tensor` and `c1_dual`, under `[NoetherianSpace X]` and the catenarity hypothesis `HomogeneityLocal.CovByDimension dim` (satisfied for schemes of finite type over a field by `HomogeneityLocal.lean`). `IntersectionTheory/DivisorSymmetryAffine.lean` proves Fulton's symmetric identity (Thm 2.4) on `Spec A` for a Noetherian domain with the dimension formula: for nonzero `a, b` with no common height-one prime, `∑_{q, b ∈ q} ord_q(b)·[div ā on V(q)] = ∑_{q, a ∈ q} ord_q(a)·[div b̄ on V(q)]` (`VectorBundle.Affine.divisorSymmetry`, unconditional via `localOrdSymmetry`). `IntersectionTheory/FirstChernClassDescent.lean` reduces the descent statement to that identity: `Scheme.IsUnitAt` (a rational function is a unit of the local ring at a point, Fulton's `v ∉ |D|`, which is stronger than `ord = 0` on non-normal schemes) and `residueUnitAt`, the restriction `RationalSection.restrict` of a section to the closure of a codimension-one point where it is a unit, the cycle-level statement `SymmetricIdentity` of Theorem 2.4 for a generator `(W, r)` and a section `s` with no common support components, and `killsRelations_of_symmetricIdentity`: if every generator admits such a section satisfying the identity then `KillsRelations` holds and `c₁` descends. Still open: the symmetric identity for rational sections on a general `W` (only the affine element case is proved), the moving lemma producing a section with no common support components with `div r`, Chern classes of higher rank, the projective bundle formula, Whitney sum and the self-intersection formula. The affine moving lemma is proved in `Algebra/SemilocalMoving.lean` (`exists_unit_mul_isUnit_atPrime`): for a Noetherian domain, finitely many distinct nonzero primes with one-dimensional localisations and prescribed units of the fraction field, one unit of the fraction field makes every prescribed unit a local unit at every one of the primes simultaneously, by the Chinese remainder theorem in the semilocalisation at the complement of the union of the primes and an Artinian-quotient argument at each place; this produces, on an affine integral scheme, a rational section of a line bundle with no common support components with a given divisor. |
| 3: cotangent complexes | `CotangentComplex/*`, `CotangentComplex/PerfectRank.lean`, `CotangentComplex/Transitivity.lean`, `CotangentComplex/TransitivityEtale.lean`, `CotangentComplex/TransitivityEtaleGeneral.lean`, `CotangentComplex/AtlasDescent.lean`, `CotangentComplex/AtlasDescentGlobal.lean` |  Mathlib derived categories, homology-defined amplitude, affine presentation/conormal comparison, flat base change in the affine model, and a constructor sending an actual affine presentation to its derived object; `CotangentComplex/PerfectComplex.lean` now adds the affine (module) model of perfectness as fixed definitions rather than fields: `IsFiniteFree` (Mathlib freeness plus finiteness), `IsStrictlyPerfect` (a bounded complex all of whose terms are finite free), `IsPerfect E` (existence of a strictly perfect complex together with an actual isomorphism `Q(K) ≅ E` in `DerivedCategory (ModuleCat R)`), and `HasTorAmplitudeIn` (vanishing cohomology of the termwise tensor with an arbitrary module). Proved: a finite free module in one degree is strictly perfect and perfect; shifts, finite direct sums and mapping cones of strictly perfect complexes are strictly perfect (two-out-of-three for the strict notion); base change along an arbitrary ring homomorphism preserves strict perfectness (with the missing `ModuleCat.extendScalars` additivity instance supplied here); shifts and biproducts of perfect derived objects are perfect; a complex supported in `[a,b]` has Tor amplitude in `[a,b]`. The rank is the honest alternating `finsum` of `Module.finrank`, proved invariant under isomorphism, additive on direct sums and on cones (`rank(cone φ) = rank G − rank F`), multiplied by `Int.negOnePow n` under shift, and equal to `rank(K⁰) − rank(K⁻¹)` in the two-term range. `GlobalTwoTermResolution E` stores an actual `[-1,0]` complex of finite free modules plus an actual derived isomorphism onto `E`, with transport along derived isomorphisms proved functorial (`replace_refl`, `replace_trans`), rank-invariant, and implying perfectness and Tor amplitude in `[-1,0]`; an affine presentation whose conormal and ambient differential modules are finite free supplies such a resolution of its own derived object, with virtual rank `finrank(CotangentSpace) − finrank(Cotangent)`. Still absent: a geometric cotangent complex and its Jacobi--Zariski transitivity triangle, perfectness local on an atlas (no derived category of module sheaves on a stack site), the tensor product of two strictly perfect complexes in general (Mathlib's `HomologicalComplex.tensorObj` presents `⨁_{p+q=n} K^p ⊗ L^q` as a coproduct over an infinite index set and the comparison with the finite biproduct of its finitely many nonzero summands is unavailable), homotopy invariance of the termwise dual and hence representative-independence of the dual of a perfect object, and a derived base-change functor on `DerivedCategory (ModuleCat R)` itself; the arbitrary-predicate global-resolution constructor stays inactive; `CotangentComplex/PerfectDual.lean` adds the dual, tensor, triangle and base-change operations on the affine model, all as constructions: the termwise dual `dualComplex K` with `(K^∨)^i = (K^{-i})^∨` and the transposed differential carrying the standard sign `(-1)^{i+1}`, proved to preserve strict perfectness, to be contravariantly functorial (`dualComplexMap`), to satisfy `rank (K^∨) = rank K` for the honest alternating `finsum`, and to admit a biduality isomorphism `K ≅ K^∨∨` given by evaluation twisted by `(-1)^i` (the twist is what makes the squares commute, and termwise it is Mathlib's reflexivity of finite free modules); the termwise tensor product with a finite free module, proved strictly perfect with `rank (K ⊗ M) = rank K · finrank M`, together with the tensor by a finite free module in a single degree `n` and its sign `(-1)^n`; two-out-of-three for perfectness in a distinguished triangle of `DerivedCategory (ModuleCat R)` in all three positions (`IsPerfect.of_distTriang₁/₂/₃`), obtained by realising the derived morphism between two strictly perfect complexes as an actual chain map through K-projectivity (`exists_chainMap_of_derivedMap`), taking its mapping cone, and comparing triangles with Mathlib's triangulated five lemma, with the rank identity `rank E' = rank E + rank E''` proved for the resulting resolutions; stability of perfect objects under cones; and derived base change of a global two-term resolution, which is again an actual `[-1,0]` complex of finite free `S`-modules resolving its own derived image, is rank-preserving (`rank_baseChange`, `baseChange_virtualRank`), and is independent of the chosen resolution (`nonempty_baseChange_iso`, proved through `quasiIso_baseChangeFunctor_map`); the shifted dual `K^∨[1]` and the tensor product of a two-term resolution with a finite free module are again two-term resolutions, with virtual ranks `-virtualRank` and `virtualRank · finrank M`; `CotangentComplex/SquareZero.lean` adds affine square-zero deformation theory as constructions, with no result-bearing field: for a square-zero ideal `M` of an `R`-algebra `B` (the class `IsSquareZero`), the kernel is given genuine module structures over every algebra over `B ⧸ M` (`Coeff`, built from `Module.IsTorsionBySet.module`), the lifting problem `Lift R M A` for an `R`-algebra map `A → B ⧸ M` is defined, and its solutions are proved to form an actual `AddTorsor` under `Derivation R A (Coeff M)` once one solution exists (`Lift.sub`, `Lift.vadd`, `Lift.instAddTorsor`, `Lift.equivDerivation`); existence for formally smooth `A` and uniqueness for formally unramified `A` are derived from Mathlib (`nonempty_lift`, `subsingleton_lift`, `unique_lift`), and the automorphisms of the extension, the endomorphisms of `B` that are the identity on `B ⧸ M` and on `M`, are proved to be exactly `Der_R(B ⧸ M, M)` compatibly with composition and to be bijective (`Aut.equivDerivation`, `Aut.derivation_comp`, `Aut.bijective`); for an actual presentation `P : Algebra.Extension R A` the obstruction cocycle `obstructionMap` of a lift on the ambient ring is constructed as an honest `A`-linear map `I ⧸ I² → M`, the coboundaries `coboundary` are proved to be exactly the maps factoring through Mathlib's cotangent complex `I ⧸ I² → A ⊗ Ω[P.Ring⁄R]`, i.e. through the differential of this layer's `AffinePresentation.twoTerm` (`mem_coboundary_iff_comp_cotangentComplex`, `mem_coboundary_iff_comp_twoTerm`), so `ObstructionGroup` is the actual cokernel of `Hom_A(A ⊗ Ω[P.Ring⁄R], M) → Hom_A(I ⧸ I², M)`; the class `obstruction` is proved independent of the chosen ambient lift (`obstruction_eq`) and `obstruction_eq_zero_iff` proves that it vanishes if and only if the lifting problem for `A` is solvable, unconditionally for a polynomial presentation (`nonempty_lift_iff_obstruction_generators`), and `obstructionGroupMap_obstruction` proves naturality of the class under a pushforward of the coefficient module along a morphism of square-zero extensions; `CotangentComplex/SquareZeroExt.lean` now supplies both, as constructions with no result-bearing field: `restrictHom`/`coboundary_eq_range`/`obstructionGroupEquivExtOne` present `ObstructionGroup R M P` as the literal cokernel of `Hom_A(K⁰,M) → Hom_A(K⁻¹,M)` for the two-term complex `K = [I ⧸ I² → A ⊗ Ω]` of `AffinePresentation.twoTerm`; `chainHomEquiv` identifies the chain maps `K ⟶ M[1]` with `Hom_A(I ⧸ I², M)`, `homotopyOfFactor` and `eq_of_homotopy_to_coeffComplex` construct a homotopy from a factorisation through the differential and conversely, so `nonempty_homotopy_iff` proves that two such chain maps are homotopic exactly when their difference is a coboundary and `homotopyExtOneEquiv` identifies `ObstructionGroup R M P` with `Hom_{K(ModuleCat A)}(K, M[1])` with no hypothesis on the presentation; when both terms of `K` are projective, `isKProjective_presentationComplex` (from `CochainComplex.isKProjective_of_projective`) upgrades this through `CochainComplex.IsKProjective.Qh_map_bijective` to the actual derived group, `derivedExtOneEquiv : ObstructionGroup ≃ (Q K ⟶ Q M[1])` and `obstructionGroupEquivDerivedExt : ObstructionGroup ≃ DerivedExt (AffinePresentation.derivedObject R A P) M 1`, with `obstructionGroupEquivDerivedExt_obstruction_eq_zero_iff` proving that the class in that derived `Ext¹` vanishes if and only if the lifting problem for `A` is solvable; dually `cotangentH0Equiv` identifies `H⁰K` with `Ω[A⁄R]`, `extZeroEquivDerivation` identifies `Ext⁰ = Hom_A(H⁰K, M)` with `Der_R(A,M)`, `exists_unique_extZeroVAdd` proves that the lifts form a simply transitive `Ext⁰`-set, and `chainHomEquivZero`/`extZeroEquivSubtype`/`homotopyExtZeroEquiv`/`derivedExtZeroEquiv` realise this `Ext⁰` as `Hom_{K(ModuleCat A)}(K, M[0])` and, under the same projectivity, as `Hom_{D(ModuleCat A)}(Q K, Q M[0])`. Base change of the extension along `R → R'` is constructed: `baseIdeal` is the ideal of `R' ⊗[R] B` generated by `M`, proved square zero with no flatness hypothesis (`isSquareZero_baseIdeal`), `range_baseCoeffMap` identifies it with the image of `R' ⊗[R] M` and `baseIdealEquiv` upgrades this to an isomorphism `R' ⊗[R] M ≅ M'` when `R'` is `R`-flat, `basePush` exhibits `B → R' ⊗[R] B` as a morphism of square-zero extensions over `A`, and `obstruction_baseChange` proves that the obstruction class of the base-changed problem is the image of the original under `obstructionGroupMap`. The lifting groupoid is constructed rather than assumed: `Aut.instGroup` makes the automorphisms of the extension a group, isomorphic to the additive group `Der_R(B ⧸ M, M)` via `Aut.mulEquivDerivation`; `LiftHom` and `instGroupoidLift` make the solutions of the lifting problem an actual `CategoryTheory.Groupoid`; `nonempty_liftHom_iff` proves that two lifts are isomorphic exactly when their difference derivation lies in the image of `autDerivationMap : Der_R(B ⧸ M, M) →+ Der_R(A, M)`; `liftIsoClassEquiv` identifies the set of isomorphism classes with `Der_R(A, M)` modulo that image once one lift exists; and `endEquivKer` identifies the automorphism group of every lift with `ker autDerivationMap`, compatibly with composition (`endEquivKer_comp`). The point case is `obstruction_eq_zero_of_formallySmooth`, `obstruction_mvPolynomial_eq_zero` and `nonempty_lift_mvPolynomial` (every square-zero lifting problem for a polynomial algebra is unobstructed), together with `subsingleton_obstructionGroup_of_ker_eq_bot` for the tautological presentation. Still absent here: the derived `Ext¹` identification for a presentation whose conormal module is not projective (this needs the stupid-truncation distinguished triangle of a two-term complex *together with a computation of its connecting morphism*; Mathlib v4.33.1's `Algebra/Homology/Embedding/StupidTrunc.lean` defines only the truncation functor and lists the inclusion and projection as TODO, and `DerivedCategory.triangleOfSES` comes with no formula for its third map), a comparison with `Full.cotangentExt` of the presentation-independent `L_{A/R}`, and base change of the source algebra `A` itself (`SquareZero.Push` fixes the source algebra, so only the coefficients are base changed); `Modules/Derived.lean` now sets up the derived-category operations on the module categories the repository actually has: for any abelian `A` with a chosen derived category it exposes Mathlib's cohomology functors `cohomologyFunctor A n` together with the factorisation through `Q`, the cohomology-of-a-shift isomorphism `cohomologyShiftIso` with both shift-sequence coherences (`cohomologyShiftIso_zero`, `cohomologyShiftIso_add`), the canonical truncation functors `τ≤n`, `τ≥n`, and the truncation triangle (the cokernel sequence `τ≤n K ↪ K ↠ K/τ≤n K` is proved short exact, its image `truncLETriangle` is proved distinguished, the third term is identified with `τ≥(n+1) K` in the derived category (`truncLEX₃IsoTruncGE`), and the resulting `truncTriangle K n₀ n₁ : τ≤n₀K ⟶ K ⟶ τ≥n₁K ⟶ (τ≤n₀K)⟦1⟧` is proved distinguished with its first two maps computed as `Q(ιTruncLE)` and `Q(πTruncGE)`), with `Ext` available via `hasExt`; these are instantiated both for `ModuleCat R` and for sheaves of modules on a ringed site (`modulesAbelian` records that Mathlib's abelian structure on `SheafOfModules` applies under the two sheafification hypotheses already carried by `Modules/Stack.lean`, so no abelianness is assumed); derived base change along a ring map `f : R →+* S` is constructed as the termwise `extendScalars` functor on cochain complexes and proved well defined on quasi-isomorphisms (bounded-above complexes of projectives are K-projective, `isKProjective_of_isStrictlyLE` and `isKProjective_of_isStrictlyPerfect`, base change preserves homotopies and homotopy equivalences, hence a quasi-isomorphism between such complexes stays a quasi-isomorphism after base change, `quasiIso_baseChangeFunctor_map`, and induces `baseChangeDerivedIso`); pseudofunctoriality is proved rather than assumed: the identity and composition comparisons `baseChangeIdIso`, `baseChangeComp'` are the mates of Mathlib's `restrictScalarsId`/`restrictScalarsComp'` under the extension/restriction adjunction, their components are computed on pure tensors, and the left unit, right unit and associativity coherence laws are proved (`baseChangeComp'_id_left`, `baseChangeComp'_id_right`, `baseChangeComp'_assoc`); still absent are the comparison of base change with the derived tensor by a complex of free modules (Mathlib has no natural isomorphism `extendScalars f ⋙ restrictScalars f ≅ − ⊗_R f_*S` compatible with the `Module.compHom` instance used by the `⊗ₜ[R,f]` notation) and the derived tensor/internal Hom/duality `RHom(E,R)` of strictly perfect complexes with `rank_dual` (Mathlib has no linear-dual functor `(ModuleCat R)ᵒᵖ ⥤ ModuleCat R`, and the monoidal structure on `CochainComplex (ModuleCat R) ℤ` is not available as instances here); `CotangentComplex/Full.lean` now constructs the cotangent complex of a ring map itself: for `R → S` in one universe, `Full.cotangentComplex R S` is the actual object of `DerivedCategory (ModuleCat S)` obtained by localising the two-term presentation complex of `Algebra.Generators.self R S`, and its independence of the presentation is proved rather than assumed: a morphism of `Algebra.Extension`s induces an honest chain map `Full.map` which is strictly functorial (`map_id`, `map_comp`), any two morphisms induce chain homotopic maps via an actual `Homotopy` built from Mathlib's `Algebra.Extension.Hom.sub` (`Full.homotopy`), consequently `Q_map_eq_of_homotopy` and `derivedMap_eq` show the induced derived morphism `derivedMap` is canonical (it does not depend on the chosen morphism of extensions), and `derivedIso` upgrades a pair of opposite morphisms to an isomorphism with full coherence (`derivedIso_refl`, `derivedIso_trans`, `derivedIso_symm`, `derivedIso_eq`); for arbitrary polynomial presentations this yields `generatorsIso` with `generatorsIso_refl`/`generatorsIso_trans`/`generatorsIso_symm` and `presentationIso : L_{S/R} ≅ AffinePresentation.derivedObject R S P.toExtension` with `presentationIso_trans`/`presentationIso_self`, so the affine comparison with `AffinePresentation.twoTerm` is a derived isomorphism and the transitivity maps are natural, and `comparePresentation` gives a canonical map to the derived object of every extension via `Algebra.Extension.defaultHom`; the cohomology is computed as actual isomorphisms (`homologyNegOneIso`/`cohomologyNegOne` identify `H⁻¹` with Mathlib's `Algebra.H1Cotangent R S`, `homologyZeroIso`/`cohomologyZero` identify `H⁰` with `Ω[S⁄R]` via the cokernel of the conormal differential and `toKaehler`, and `isZero_cohomology`/`hasCohomologicalAmplitude` give amplitude `[-1,0]` in this layer's sense), from which the criteria are proved: `formallyUnramified_iff` (`H⁰ = 0`), `formallySmooth_iff` (`H⁰` projective and `H⁻¹ = 0`), `formallyEtale_iff`, and `formallyEtale_iff_isZero` (`L_{S/R} ≅ 0`, proved through `isZero_Q_obj`: an acyclic complex has zero image under `Q`), whence `isZero_cotangentComplex_of_isLocalization` shows the cotangent complex of a localisation vanishes; the Jacobi--Zariski sequence for `R → S → T` is available as actual maps `jzH1Map`, `jzDelta`, `jzBaseChange`, `jzOmegaMap` between the cohomology objects of the three cotangent complexes together with exactness at every spot (`exact_jzH1Map_jzDelta`, `exact_jzDelta_jzBaseChange`, `exact_jzBaseChange_jzOmegaMap`, `surjective_jzOmegaMap`); base change is proved in the form `baseChangePresentationIso` (the base change of a polynomial presentation computes `L_{(T⊗S)/T}`), with `cohomologyNegOneBaseChangeIso`, the flat statement `h1CotangentBaseChangeEquivOfFlat` and `kaehlerBaseChangeEquiv`, and `cotangentExt` exposes `Extⁿ(L_{S/R}, M)` with `subsingleton_cotangentExt_of_formallyEtale` its vanishing in the étale case; still absent are the full untruncated simplicial cotangent complex (Mathlib v4.33.1 has no simplicial commutative algebras), Jacobi--Zariski as a distinguished triangle (Mathlib's `δ` exists only on `H¹`, with no chain-level connecting morphism), the identification `Ext¹(L_{A/R}, M) ≅ SquareZero.ObstructionGroup` (no usable `Ext`-of-a-two-term-complex or t-structure adjunction API), base change stated over `T ⊗ S` rather than `T` (no `T ⊗[R] S`-module instance on `T ⊗[R] M`), and the lci criterion (no Mathlib notion of local complete intersection linked to the conormal module); `CotangentComplex/Global.lean` now globalises the ring-map cotangent complex to affine charts of a morphism of schemes and to the charts of an étale atlas, with every comparison constructed and every coherence proved rather than assumed. The technical device is restriction of scalars to a common ground ring: Mathlib's `Algebra.Extension.Hom` is general enough to relate presentations over *different* base rings, and `restrictHom`/`mapOver` turn such a morphism into an honest chain map of presentation complexes viewed over a ring `S₀` mapping into both targets, so that `mapOver_id` and `mapOver_comp` are *strict* equalities of chain maps and the cocycle condition for refinements needs no coherence isomorphism; `homotopyOver` (built from `Algebra.Extension.Hom.sub`, which Mathlib states in the same generality) proves that two morphisms of extensions over the same base map induce chain-homotopic comparisons, whence `derivedMapOver_eq`: the induced morphism in `DerivedCategory (ModuleCat S₀)` is canonical, and `derivedMapOver_id`/`derivedMapOver_comp` make it functorial. Specialised to a tower `R → S → T` this gives `towerComparison : L_{S/R} ⟶ L_{T/R}` in `DerivedCategory (ModuleCat R)` with `towerComparison_self` and `towerComparison_trans` proved from canonicity alone. Geometrically, `AffineChart f V` stores only an affine open `U ⊆ f⁻¹V` of the source together with its affineness and containment (no embedding, no presentation, no cotangent complex is a field) and `AffineChart.cotangentComplex` is the actual object `Full.cotangentComplex Γ(Y,V) Γ(X,U)` of `DerivedCategory (ModuleCat Γ(X,U))`, with `AffineChart.objectOverBase` its avatar over `Γ(Y,V)`; `AffineChart.comparison` is the restriction morphism of a refinement `U' ⊆ U` (the scalar tower `isScalarTower_res` is proved from `Scheme.Hom.appLE_map`, not assumed) and `AffineChart.comparison_trans` proves that comparison maps commute with chart refinements. The local model is exhibited: `localComplex_X_negOne`, `localComplex_X_zero`, `localComplex_d`, `localComplex_isZero` identify the presentation complex with `[I/I² → Ω_{M/V}|_U] = [P.ker.Cotangent → Γ(U) ⊗_{Γ(M_U)} Ω_{Γ(M_U)/Γ(V)}]` in degrees `-1,0`, `embeddingIso`/`embeddingIso_hom`/`isIso_comparePresentation` make the comparison with `L_{Γ(U)/Γ(V)}` an actual isomorphism in the derived category for an embedding into an affine space over `V`, with `embeddingIso_trans`/`embeddingIso_self` proving that two embeddings give coherent comparisons and `embeddingCotangentSpaceBasis` exhibiting degree `0` as free on the coordinate differentials; for an arbitrary formally smooth ambient ring only the cohomology comparisons `cohomologyZeroLocalIso` and `cohomologyNegOneSmoothLocalIso` (the latter via `Algebra.Extension.equivH1CotangentOfFormallySmooth`) are available, because turning `Full.comparePresentation` into a quasi-isomorphism would need a naturality statement for `ShortComplex.moduleCatHomologyIso`, which Mathlib lacks, and a morphism of extensions back to the canonical polynomial presentation, which formal smoothness does not supply (it lifts only along square-zero surjections). On the étale side, `isZero_cotangentComplex_of_etale` proves `L_{U'/U} = 0`, `cohomologyZeroEtaleIso` together with `jzBaseChange_eq_cohomologyZeroEtaleIso` and `isIso_jzBaseChange_of_formallyEtale` prove that `H⁰` is the honest base change along a formally étale map and that this isomorphism *is* the Jacobi–Zariski map, `cohomologyNegOneLocalizationIso` does the same for `H⁻¹` along a localisation, and `jzH1Map_eq_zero`/`jzDelta_eq_zero`/`jzOmegaMap_eq_zero` record the resulting collapse of the Jacobi–Zariski sequence; `AffineChart.basicOpenChart` with `isLocalization_basicOpenChart`, `formallyEtale_basicOpenChart`, `isZero_cotangentComplex_basicOpenChart`, `cohomologyZeroBasicOpenIso`, `cohomologyNegOneBasicOpenIso` instantiate all of this for Zariski refinements, and `AffineChart.coverChart`, `etale_appLE` (from `HasRingHomProperty.appLE` for `Etale`), `isZero_cotangentComplex_etaleCover` and `cohomologyZeroEtaleCoverIso` for an étale cover of a chart. For Deligne–Mumford stacks `etale_atlasPullbackPresentation_fst` extracts from `StackChart.IsEtaleSurjective` that every base change of the atlas is an actual scheme with étale projection, and `isZero_cotangentComplex_atlasTransition`, `cohomologyZeroAtlasTransitionIso` and `exists_etaleAtlas_isZero_cotangentComplex_transition` package the affine-local complexes on the atlas together with the proved vanishing of every chart-transition complex and the degree-zero base change; the last statement produces the atlas from the stack's own `etaleAtlas` field, so no atlas and no embedding occurs in its formulation. Still absent: the comparison maps are not proved to be isomorphisms after derived base change (this needs the comparison of `Modules.Derived.baseChangeFunctor` with the derived tensor product, already recorded as unavailable), `H⁻¹` base change along a general étale rather than localising map (this needs a compatible pair of presentations satisfying the bijectivity hypothesis `J ≃ Q ⊗_P I` of `Algebra.Extension.tensorH1CotangentOfFormallyEtale`), the smooth-embedding comparison for a non-polynomial smooth ambient, refinement of the target open `V` (which is the Jacobi–Zariski direction and would need the triangle, not just the exact sequence), comparison across two *different* atlases (`Stacks/AtlasRefinement.lean` supplies only 2-categorical common refinements, with no passage from a refinement 2-cell to a scheme morphism of chart schemes), and any assembly of the chart family into a sheaf of complexes on `Sites.smallEtaleStackRingedSite`, which would require the derived category of `SheafOfModules` together with effectivity of descent data and a resolution of the universe mismatch between that site's `CommRingCat.{u+1}`-valued structure sheaf and the single-universe `Full.cotangentComplex`; `CotangentComplex/PerfectDualInvariance.lean` closes the homotopy-invariance gap of the termwise dual: `dualHomotopy` transposes a homotopy of chain maps through the sign convention of `dualComplex`, so `dualComplexMap` preserves homotopy equivalences (`dualHomotopyEquiv`) and descends to a contravariant functor `dualFunctor` on the homotopy category of cochain complexes of `R`-modules; combined with K-projectivity of strictly perfect complexes, `exists_homotopyEquiv_of_derivedIso` shows that every isomorphism `Q K ≅ Q L` of strictly perfect complexes is `Q` of a homotopy equivalence and `quasiIso_dualComplexMap` that duals of quasi-isomorphisms are quasi-isomorphisms, hence the dual of a perfect object is well defined: `IsPerfect.dualObject` dualises a chosen strictly perfect representative, `IsPerfect.dualObjectIso` identifies the dual of any other representative with it, the comparison isomorphisms `dualIso` are independent of the chain map realising them (`dualIso_hom_eq`) and compose contravariantly (`dualIso_refl`, `dualIso_trans`), the dual object is perfect (`isPerfect_dualObject`), biduality holds (`bidualObjectIso`), and duality is fully faithful on strictly perfect complexes (`dualDerivedHomEquiv`, `dualPairingSymmEquiv`, via `bidualityIso_naturality`); still missing are the invariance of the alternating rank under homotopy equivalence (the Euler-characteristic formula), isolated as the explicit hypothesis `RankHomotopyInvariant` of `IsPerfect.rank_eq_of_rep` and `IsPerfect.rank_dualObject` (unconditionally only `rank (dualComplex h.rep) = h.rank` is proved), and the identification of `Hⁱ(K^∨)` with the Ext groups `Hom_D(Q K, Q(R)⟦i⟧)` (Round 12.) `CotangentComplex/PerfectRank.lean` discharges the hypothesis `RankHomotopyInvariant` of `PerfectDualInvariance.lean` for every commutative ring: `rank_eq_of_homotopyEquiv` proves that homotopy equivalent strictly perfect complexes have the same rank. The proof shows first that a contractible strictly perfect complex has rank zero (`rank_eq_zero_of_homotopy_id_zero`) by base changing to the residue field of a maximal ideal, where the contracting homotopy makes the complex exact and the alternating sum of dimensions of a bounded exact family of finite-dimensional vector spaces vanishes by rank–nullity and telescoping (`finsum_negOnePow_finrank_eq_zero`, `finrank_eq_boundaryRank_add`); then a homotopy equivalence has contractible mapping cone, because in the homotopy category the cone of an isomorphism is a zero object (`Pretriangulated.Triangle.isZero₃_iff_isIso₁` on the distinguished cone triangle, `HomotopyCategory.isZero_quotient_obj_iff`), and rank is additive on cones. Hence `rankHomotopyInvariant : RankHomotopyInvariant R`, and the rank of a perfect object of `D(R)` is unconditionally well defined on every strictly perfect representative (`IsPerfect.rank_eq_of_rep'`) and equal to the rank of its dual (`IsPerfect.rank_dualObject'`). The transitivity sequence (issue #57): `CotangentComplex/Transitivity.lean` completes the Jacobi–Zariski sequence of `Full.lean` (`jzH1Map`, `jzDelta`, `jzBaseChange`, `jzOmegaMap`) with the missing flat base-change arrow `H⁻¹(L_{S/R}) ⊗_S T → H⁻¹(L_{T/R})` (`jzH1BaseChange`) and its exactness (`exact_jzH1BaseChange_jzH1Map`, `T` flat over `S`), bundling the five arrows and their exactness into `TransitivitySequence` with every field proved; for `T` formally étale over `S` the base change of `H⁰` is an isomorphism unconditionally (`hZeroBaseChangeIso`) and that of `H⁻¹` is surjective. `CotangentComplex/TransitivityEtale.lean` proves the `H⁻¹` base change is an isomorphism when `T` is a localisation of `S` (`hNegOneBaseChangeIso_of_isLocalization`, via Mathlib's `tensorH1CotangentOfIsLocalization`) and records why the general étale case needs a compatible pair of presentations that the canonical generators cannot provide. Still open: the distinguished triangle in the derived category, the general étale `H⁻¹` isomorphism, atlas descent and the sheaf of cotangent complexes on the small étale site, and the comparison for a non-polynomial smooth ambient. `CotangentComplex/TransitivityEtaleGeneral.lean` closes the étale case: `bijective_jzH1BaseChange_of_etale` proves `H⁻¹(L_{S/R}) ⊗_S T → H⁻¹(L_{T/R})` bijective for `S → T` étale (packaged as `hNegOneBaseChangeIso`), by supplying the compatible presentations Mathlib's `tensorH1CotangentOfFormallyEtale` needs — a presentation-independence transport, the lift of a standard étale pair along `MvPolynomial S R ↠ S` with its kernel computation, and the flat kernel base-change criterion — for standard étale algebras, then localising on `T`; so the truncated cotangent complex satisfies étale base change in both degrees. Only the non-finitely-presented formally étale case remains open. `CotangentComplex/AtlasDescent.lean` packages the étale base-change isomorphisms into descent data along an étale cover `S → T_j` of rings: transition isomorphisms `Hⁱ(L_{T_j/R}) ⊗ (T_j ⊗_S T_l) ≅ Hⁱ(L_{T_j ⊗_S T_l/R})` for `i ∈ {0, -1}`, the cocycle condition on triple overlaps and effectivity (the transitions are the canonical base changes of `Hⁱ(L_{S/R})`), bundled as `EtaleDescentDatum`/`cotangentDescentDatum`; the scheme- and stack-level packaging along the affine charts of an atlas is the remaining step. `CotangentComplex/AtlasDescentGlobal.lean` packages `H⁰`/`H⁻¹` of the affine-local cotangent complex into presheaves on the category of affine charts of `f : X ⟶ Y` over an affine open of `Y` (objects `hZeroObj`/`hNegOneObj`, restriction maps with the composition law `hZeroMap_comp`/`hNegOneMap_comp`, quasi-coherence along basic-open refinements `isQuasiCoherent_hZeroPresheaf`/`isQuasiCoherent_hNegOnePresheaf`); the identity law, hence a bundled functor, is blocked by an instance diamond on the diagonal chart, and the packaging along the étale atlas of a Deligne–Mumford stack is not attempted. |
| 4: cone stacks | `Cones/TwoTermQuotient.lean`, `Cones/Picard.lean`, `Cones/DerivedPicard.lean`, `Cones/Stack.lean`, `Cones/Quotient.lean`, `Cones/Criteria.lean`, `Cones/CriteriaBundle.lean`, `Cones/DerivedCriteria.lean`, `Cones/DerivedTruncation.lean`, `Cones/DerivedTruncationRoof.lean`, `Cones/PerfectTwoTerm.lean`, `Cones/DualShortExact.lean`, `Cones/QuotientTorsor.lean`, `Cones/StackFibreProducts.lean`, `Cones/StackCoherence.lean`, `Cones/QuotientTorsorStack.lean`, `Cones/StackProdCoherence.lean`, `Cones/StackBaseChangeComp.lean`, `Cones/DeltaComparison.lean`, `Cones/DeltaComparisonSequence.lean`, `Cones/DerivedPicardFunctoriality.lean`, `Cones/DerivedPicardFunctorialityRoof.lean` |   actual Picard groupoids, fixed-base groupoid fibres, pullback-natural addition/actions, contraction and stabilizers, chain-homotopy 2-isomorphisms, quasi-isomorphism equivalences, an explicit `[K ≃ K]` acyclic-summand homotopy equivalence, vector-bundle-stack ranks derived from an actual local finite-free matrix quotient presentation, and a constructed rank-zero bundle stack whose fibres are explicitly equivalent to `[0/0]`. `Cones/DerivedPicard.lean` now makes `h¹/h⁰` functorial and derived: two-term complexes of `R`-modules are given a `Category` instance and `h¹/h⁰` is an honest functor `picardFunctor : LinearTwoTermComplex R ⥤ Cat`, with specified pseudofunctor comparison 2-cells (`picardCompIso`, `picardIdIso`) whose associativity and both unit coherence laws are proved; they are identities, and strictness is proved from the chain-level formulas rather than assumed. Chain homotopies are the invertible 2-cells: identity, vertical composition, inverse, both whiskerings and horizontal composition are constructed (`ChainHomotopy.refl/vcomp/symm/postcomp/precomp/hcomp`) and `ChainHomotopy.natTrans` is proved to take each of them to the corresponding operation on natural transformations (`natTrans_refl`, `natTrans_vcomp`, `natTrans_symm`, `natTrans_postcomp`, `natTrans_precomp`, `natTrans_hcomp`). A cochain complex of modules has a functorial underlying two-term complex in degrees `-1,0` (`cochainFunctor`, `cochainPicardFunctor`), and Mathlib homotopies and homotopy equivalences of such complexes restrict to the repo's chain-level notions (`ofHomotopy`, `ofHomotopyEquiv`). For a derived object `E` with a `GlobalTwoTermResolution F`, `h¹/h⁰(E)` is defined as `F.picard`, with vertex automorphisms proved to be `h⁰` (the kernel of the differential) and isomorphism classes proved to be `h¹` (its cokernel). Resolution independence is proved unconditionally: the resolving complex is proved K-projective (bounded above, free hence projective terms), so Mathlib's `CochainComplex.IsKProjective.Qh_map_bijective` lifts the derived comparison isomorphism of any two global two-term resolutions of the same `E` to an actual chain map realizing it (`exists_chain_realization`), that chain map is a quasi-isomorphism and therefore a homotopy equivalence of cochain complexes (`exists_homotopyEquiv`), and hence the two Picard groupoids are equivalent (`hasChainComparison`, `nonempty_picardEquivalence`), with the explicit hypothesis form `Comparison`/`HasChainComparison` retained for the future sheaf-level statement and proved stable under identity, composition and transport along `E ≅ E'`. Functoriality in composites is proved on the nose in the forward direction and up to a canonical natural isomorphism on inverses (`quotientEquivalence_comp_functor`, `quotientEquivalenceCompInverseIso`, `transPicardEquivalenceInverseIso`, all from uniqueness of right adjoints), and the inverse of the equivalence attached to a quasi-isomorphism is proved to agree with the chain-level inverse (`HomotopyEquivalence.quasiIsoInverseIso`), in particular with the projection off the acyclic summand `[K ≃ K]`, which is also proved to be a strict retraction (`AcyclicSummand.inverseIsoProjection`, `inclusion_quotientFunctor_comp_projection`). Still absent: canonicity of the comparison equivalence (the realizing chain map is unique only up to homotopy, so the equivalence is canonical only up to the `ChainHomotopy.natIso` 2-cells, and no uniqueness statement is proved); a comparison of the elementwise kernel/cokernel quasi-isomorphism predicate with Mathlib's `QuasiIso`, which would need the homology of a `[-1,0]`-complex of `ModuleCat R` identified elementwise with the kernel and cokernel of its differential; and any sheaf-level version, since there is no derived category of module sheaves on a stack site; `Cones/Quotient.lean` additionally constructs the quotient `[C/E]` of an affine cone by a vector-bundle action as an actual groupoid, fibrewise over affine test schemes and with no conclusion stored as presentation data: `ConeQuotient.QuotientGroupoid A B` has as objects the `B`-points of `C` and as arrows the `B`-points of `E` translating one point into the other, with constructed `Category` and `Groupoid` instances, and the hom-sets are proved to be exactly the orbit translations and the automorphism groups exactly the stabilisers (`homEquiv`, `nonempty_hom_iff`, `autEquivStabilizer`), so nothing is collapsed or enlarged; the projection `C → [C/E]` is `QuotientGroupoid.of`, the vertex is `vertexPoint` (the image of the cone vertex), and the contraction action on the quotient is constructed as `contractionFunctor`, well defined precisely because of the proved `𝔾ₘ`-equivariance of the action, with its laws constructed as natural isomorphisms `contractionOneIso`, `contractionMulIso`, `contractionZeroIso` (contraction by `0` is the constant functor at the vertex) and `contractionFunctor_vertexPoint`;
