reindexing along a map of test algebras is `mapQuotient` and is proved strictly functorial (`mapQuotient_id`, `mapQuotient_comp`) and to commute with contraction (`mapQuotient_comp_contractionFunctor`), which is packaged as an honest functor `quotientPrestack : CommAlgCat R ⥤ Cat`, the affine quotient prestack; an equivariant morphism induces `quotientFunctor : [C'/E'] ⥤ [C/E]`, strictly compatible with identities, composition and reindexing, and a homotopy between two equivariant morphisms (a map `H : C' → E` carrying `φ₁` to `φ₂` whose failure of `E'`-invariance is exactly the difference `u₂ - u₁` of the bundle maps, `ConeQuotient.Homotopy`) is proved to induce the expected invertible `2`-cell `Homotopy.natIso`; the criterion for two quotient presentations to have equivalent quotients is proved as a theorem rather than stored, `isEquivalence_quotientFunctor` deducing `Functor.IsEquivalence` from orbit-surjectivity, injectivity of `l' ↦ l' ∘ u` on translations and liftability of translations; that the construction really is the torsor quotient is verified in two cases, `translationQuotientEquivalence` proving `[E/E] ≃ Spec R` (the translation action is simply transitive, `translationAction_hom_unique`) and `freeQuotientEquivalence` proving `[C ×_{Spec R} E / E] ≃ C`, and the general statement is packaged as `IsExactSequence` for a sequence `E → C → D` (invariance, surjectivity on points, fibres equal to orbits, freeness, all hypotheses and none a stored conclusion) with `IsExactSequence.quotientEquivalence` proving that the quotient groupoid is then the discrete groupoid of `D`-points and `isExactSequence_freeAction` exhibiting `E → C × E → C` as exact; what remains is `[C/E]` as an `FppfStack`, hence as a `ConeStack` with `StackHom` projection and vertex and with `contractionPullbackIso`, because `Stacks/QuotientStack.lean` still exports only the torsor groupoids `ActionTorsor` and their `pullbackPseudofunctor` and the fppf stackification of a groupoid-valued prestack is absent from the repository and from Mathlib, the passage from the algebra-points prestack to a stack on all test schemes (which needs `fppfYoneda.obj (Spec S)` identified with the functor of `R`-algebra points), the geometric form of the equivalence criterion (cartesian square with `E' → E` surjective and `C' = C ×_E E'`, since a surjection of vector bundles is not surjective on `B`-points for an arbitrary ring `B` and the hypotheses of `isEquivalence_quotientFunctor` can only be discharged fppf-locally), fibre products of cone actions (blocked by the missing `Sym(F ⊕ F') ≃ Sym(F) ⊗ Sym(F')`), and the comparison of `[C/E]` with the `h¹/h⁰` Picard groupoid of `Cones/DerivedPicard.lean`, which needs the sheaf-level quotient; `Cones/Criteria.lean` now proves the cohomological criteria that convert statements about `h⁰` and `h¹` into statements about the `h¹/h⁰` groupoid, with no conclusion stored in a structure field: the automorphism group of every object is `h⁰` (`PicardCriteria.autEquivKernel`), every nonempty hom-set is an `h⁰`-torsor (`homEquivKernel`), isomorphism classes are exactly `h¹` (`isoClass`, `isoClass_eq_iff`, `isoClass_surjective`, `isoClass_map`), and for a chain map `φ` the induced functor is faithful iff `h⁰(φ)` is injective (`faithful_iff`), full iff `h⁰(φ)` is surjective *and* `h¹(φ)` is injective (`full_iff`), essentially surjective iff `h¹(φ)` is surjective (`essSurj_iff`), and an equivalence iff `φ` is a quasi-isomorphism (`isEquivalence_iff`), the last being the converse of the previously one-directional `quotientEquivalence`; the closed-immersion criterion is `PicardCriteria.IsCohomologicalMono` (`h⁰(φ)` bijective and `h¹(φ)` injective), proved equivalent to full faithfulness (`iff_full_and_faithful`, `fullyFaithful`), to imply injectivity on isomorphism classes (`injective_isoClass`), and, when `φ` is degreewise injective, left-cancellability of the induced functor, i.e. monomorphy (`quotientFunctor_cancel`); degreewise short exact sequences `0 → K' → K → K'' → 0` are `PicardCriteria.ShortExact`, six hypotheses and no conclusions: the connecting map `δ : h⁰(K'') → h¹(K')` is constructed from lifts and proved lift-independent (`exists_delta_lift`, `delta_well_defined`, `deltaFun_eq`, `delta`), and the six-term cohomology sequence is proved exact at every spot (`injective_kernelMap`, `exact_h0_middle`, `exact_h0_right`, `exact_h1_left`, `exact_h1_middle`, `surjective_cokernelMap`); geometrically this gives that `[K¹/K⁰] → [K''¹/K''⁰]` is essentially surjective (`essSurj_quotientFunctor`), that the fibres of the induced map on hom-sets are torsors under `h⁰(K')` with a unique translating automorphism (`hom_fibre_torsor`), that the translation action of `[K'¹/K'⁰]` (`translate`, `translate_zero`, `translate_add`, `translateInvarianceIso`) has as orbits exactly the fibres on isomorphism classes (`nonempty_iso_map_iff`), and that an acyclic quotient makes the inclusion an equivalence (`isQuasiIsomorphism_of_acyclic`, `quotientEquivalenceOfAcyclic`); `Cones/CriteriaBundle.lean` adds the finiteness and base-change half: the virtual rank `rank E¹ - rank E⁰` (`PicardCriteria.virtualRank`) is proved invariant under quasi-isomorphism of two-term complexes of finite free modules (`virtualRank_eq_of_isQuasiIsomorphism`) by exhibiting the mapping-cone sequence `0 → E⁰ → F⁰ × E¹ → F¹ → 0`, proving its exactness from the two cohomology bijections (`coneAlpha_injective`, `coneBeta_surjective`, `ker_coneBeta`) and splitting it against projectivity of `F¹`; hence the derived virtual rank of a global two-term resolution is the naive Euler characteristic of its underlying two-term complex (`rank_eq_virtualRank`, `virtualRank_resolution`) and is independent of the chosen resolution (`virtualRank_resolution_eq`, via `DerivedPicard`'s unconditional `hasChainComparison`), and the displayed rank of a `VectorBundleStack` is the virtual rank of the two-term complex of its local matrix presentation (`stackRank_eq_virtualRank`, `virtualRank_freeTwoTermComplex`); the fibres of `h¹/h⁰(Eᵛ)` over the affine objects of the big fppf site are the translation groupoids of the complexes of `B`-points `[Hom_R(E¹,B) → Hom_R(E⁰,B)]` (`dualPoints`); reindexing along a map of test algebras is postcomposition and is proved strictly functorial (`dualPointsMap_id`, `dualPointsMap_comp`), so the fibres assemble into an honest prestack `dualPrestack : CommAlgCat R ⥤ Cat` with no unproved coherence datum; a chain map dualises contravariantly (`dualHom`, `dualHom_id`, `dualHom_comp`) and commutes strictly with reindexing (`dualHom_quotientFunctor_naturality`), a chain homotopy dualises over every test algebra (`dualChainHomotopy`), so a chain homotopy equivalence induces equivalences on every fibre with no flatness hypothesis (`dualHomotopyEquivalence`, `dualQuotientEquivalence`), the fibrewise equivalence criterion is `dualIsEquivalence_iff` with its "after every base change" form `dualEquivalence_of_forall_quasiIso`, and the automorphism group of every object of the fibre over `Spec B` is proved to be `Hom_R(h¹(E), B)` (`dualH0Equiv`), which is the explicit small-to-big comparison in degree zero; still absent: the fppf stackification of `dualPrestack`, so none of these criteria is yet available as a statement about `ConeStack`s or `StackHom`s, for the same reason as for `quotientPrestack`; any statement that `h¹` of the dual commutes with base change, which is false without flatness, so the hypotheses of the criteria must be imposed test algebra by test algebra; the derived-category form, turning a distinguished triangle in `D(R)` into a `ShortExact` of two-term complexes, which needs the mapping cone of complexes of modules compared with the elementwise kernel/cokernel predicate; and the comparison of `PicardCriteria.ShortExact` with the geometric `ConeQuotient.IsExactSequence`, which needs the still-missing sheaf-level identification of `[C/E]` with the Picard groupoid (Round 12.) `Cones/DerivedCriteria.lean` closes the gap between the elementwise `h¹/h⁰` theory and Mathlib's homological algebra. `LinearTwoTermComplex.toCochainComplexHom` realizes a chain map of two-term complexes as a morphism of cochain complexes, inverse on the nose to `ofCochainComplexHom`, and `homologyNegOneIso`/`homologyZeroIso` identify the homology of a complex supported in degrees `-1, 0` with the kernel and the cokernel of its differential (all other degrees vanish, `isZero_homology_toCochainComplex`). The main comparison `isQuasiIsomorphism_iff_quasiIso` proves that the repo's elementwise predicate `Hom.IsQuasiIsomorphism` is *equivalent* to Mathlib's `QuasiIso`, equivalently (`isIso_Q_map_toCochainComplexHom_iff`) to invertibility in `D(R)`. On the derived side, `ShortExact.cochainShortExact` upgrades a degreewise short exact sequence of two-term complexes to a short exact sequence of cochain complexes, whence a distinguished triangle in `D(R)` (`ShortExact.triangleOfSES_distinguished`) and the identification of the mapping cone of the inclusion with the quotient complex (`ShortExact.quasiIso_descShortComplex`); conversely `shortExact_toCoker` builds the short exact sequence with quotient `LinearTwoTermComplex.coker φ` from any degreewise injective chain map, and `ShortExact.shortExactPicard` packages the proved Picard-groupoid consequences. Finally `GlobalTwoTermResolution.nonempty_homotopy_of_Q_map_eq` shows that a derived morphism between resolving complexes has a chain realization unique up to homotopy, so `Comparison.picardEquivalence_unique_up_to_natIso` (and its inverse counterpart) make the resolution-independence equivalence of `h¹/h⁰` canonical up to natural isomorphism. Still missing: the comparison of `ShortExact.delta` with Mathlib's connecting map `ShortComplex.ShortExact.δ` of the long exact homology sequence, and any statement over a base other than a ring. `Cones/DerivedTruncation.lean` extends `h¹/h⁰` from two-term complexes to arbitrary cochain complexes of `R`-modules. `LinearTwoTermComplex.twoTermTrunc K = [K⁻¹/im d⁻² → K⁰]` is functorial (`twoTermTruncHom`, `twoTermTruncFunctor`) and comes with a projection `truncProjection K` from `K`, which is proved to be a quasi-isomorphism in degree `-1` unconditionally and in degree `0` whenever `K.d 0 1 = 0` (`quasiIsoAt_truncProjection_negOne`, `quasiIsoAt_truncProjection_zero`); consequently `homologyNegOneIsoTrunc` and `homologyZeroIsoTrunc` identify `h⁰` and `h¹` of the truncation with Mathlib's `H⁻¹(K)` and `H⁰(K)`. From this, `isQuasiIsomorphism_twoTermTruncHom` shows that a chain map which is a quasi-isomorphism in degrees `-1` and `0` truncates to an elementwise quasi-isomorphism, so `truncQuotientEquivalence` gives an equivalence of Picard groupoids, and `ofHomotopyTrunc` descends a homotopy of chain maps to a chain homotopy of truncations needing only `K.d 0 1 = 0` — the component `K⁻¹ ⟶ L⁻²` is killed by the quotient. For derived objects, `TruncPresentation E` (a K-projective complex with no differential out of degree `0` representing `E`) defines `h¹/h⁰(E)`, and `TruncPresentation.nonempty_picardEquivalence` proves independence of the presentation by realizing the comparison isomorphism as a chain map; `GlobalTwoTermResolution.nonempty_picardEquivalence_truncPresentation` shows this agrees with the two-term construction of `Cones/DerivedPicard.lean`. The hypothesis `K.d 0 1 = 0` is necessary (otherwise `h¹` of the truncation is the cokernel of `d⁻¹`, not `H⁰`); what remains missing is independence for arbitrary, non-K-projective representatives, which would require representing a derived isomorphism by a roof of quasi-isomorphisms and truncating the intermediate complex (`nonempty_truncQuotientEquivalence_of_roof` is ready for such input). `Cones/DerivedTruncationRoof.lean` removes the K-projectivity hypothesis from the resolution independence of `h¹/h⁰`. `LinearTwoTermComplex.nonempty_truncQuotientEquivalence_of_Q_iso` proves that any two cochain complexes of `R`-modules with no differential out of degree `0` whose images in the derived category are isomorphic have equivalent Picard groupoids of two-term truncations; the proof represents the isomorphism by a roof `K ← N → K'` of quasi-isomorphisms (`DerivedCategory.right_fac`, i.e. the calculus of fractions of `HomotopyCategory.quasiIso`) and composes the legs with `N.ιTruncLE 0`, which is a quasi-isomorphism in all degrees `≤ 0` and whose source has no differential out of degree `0`. This yields a presentation-free definition of `h¹/h⁰(E)` for a derived object: `TruncRep E` is any complex with `d⁰ = 0` representing `E`, `TruncRep.picard` its Picard groupoid and `TruncRep.nonempty_picardEquivalence` the independence statement, with `TruncRep.vertexAutEquiv` identifying vertex automorphisms with `H⁻¹`. Such a representative exists for every complex without cohomology in positive degrees (`exists_rep_d_zero_one_eq_zero`, `TruncRep.ofIsLE`, via `τ≤0`), and `TruncPresentation.nonempty_picardEquivalence_truncRep` together with `GlobalTwoTermResolution.nonempty_picardEquivalence_truncRep` prove that this agrees with the K-projective presentations of `Cones/DerivedTruncation.lean` and with the two-term resolutions of `Cones/DerivedPicard.lean`. The hypothesis `d⁰ = 0` on both complexes is necessary and remains; what is still missing is a representative with `d⁰ = 0` for derived objects with nonzero positive cohomology, and the functoriality of `E ↦ h¹/h⁰(E)`. `Cones/PerfectTwoTerm.lean` proves the affine form of "`h¹/h⁰(Eᵛ)` is a vector-bundle stack when `E` is perfect of amplitude `[-1,0]`". The top of a bounded complex of finitely generated projectives that is exact in positive degrees is chopped at the level of modules: `projective_ker_of_surjective` plus the downward induction `projective_finite_cocycles` show that all cocycles `Z^i = ker d^i` with `i ≥ 0` are finitely generated projective. The bottom is chopped by `perfectTwoTerm K = [K⁻¹/im d⁻² → Z⁰(K)]`, whose degree-zero term is always finitely presented (`finitePresentation_perfectTwoTerm_degreeZero`) and is projective as soon as it is flat (`projective_perfectTwoTerm_degreeZero_of_flat`), flatness being exactly what a Tor-amplitude hypothesis provides; when the complex vanishes in degree `-2` no hypothesis is needed. The resulting property `IsVectorBundleComplex` — both terms finitely generated projective — is inherited by the complexes of `B`-points of the dual (`isVectorBundleComplex_dualPoints`, from `projective_finite_hom_of_projective_finite`), which is the fibrewise vector-bundle-stack statement, and it holds unconditionally for the global two-term resolutions of `Cones/DerivedPicard.lean` (`GlobalTwoTermResolution.isVectorBundleComplex`). On the derived side, `quasiIso_truncProjection` shows that a complex exact outside `[-1,0]` with no differential out of degree `0` is quasi-isomorphic to its two-term truncation, so `truncRepOfExact` produces a `TruncRep` of `Q.obj K` whose Picard groupoid is `h¹/h⁰` of the derived object, and `perfectQuotientEquivalence` identifies the perfect two-term model with that truncation. What remains missing is the deduction of flatness of `K⁻¹/im d⁻²` from `HasTorAmplitudeIn K (-1) 0` (it needs Tor computed by the finite projective resolution `K^a → ⋯ → K⁻¹`), and the complex-level homotopy equivalence `K ≃ L`, which none of the `h¹/h⁰` statements require. `Cones/DualShortExact.lean` turns a degreewise split short exact sequence of two-term complexes into a short exact sequence of abelian cone stacks. `PicardCriteria.ShortExact.IsDegreewiseSplit` asks for degreewise retractions of the injection; the splitting lemma (`exists_section_of_retraction`, `exists_retraction_of_section`) shows this is the same as degreewise sections of the surjection, and `ShortExact.isDegreewiseSplit_of_projective` proves that a projective third term — the two-term shadow of a perfect third term in a distinguished triangle — always splits the sequence. Dualising then gives `ShortExact.shortExact_dualComplexHom` for module coefficients and `ShortExact.shortExact_dualHom` for the complexes of `B`-points, with all six conditions proved; `shortExactPicard_dualComplexHom` and `shortExactPicard_dualHom` package the geometric consequences (essential surjectivity of `h¹/h⁰(Kᵛ) → h¹/h⁰(K'ᵛ)`, hom-fibres torsors under `h⁰(K''ᵛ)`, iso-fibres the orbits of `h¹/h⁰(K''ᵛ)`). The six-term sequence of the duals is stated through `dualModuleH0Equiv` as `0 → Hom(h¹K'', N) → Hom(h¹K, N) → Hom(h¹K', N) → h¹(K''ᵛ) → h¹(Kᵛ) → h¹(K'ᵛ) → 0` (`injective_precompModule_cokernelMap`, `ker_precompModule_cokernelMap`, `dualDelta`, `ker_dualDelta`), the first two statements needing no splitting hypothesis. Combining with `shortExact_toCoker` gives the derived-triangle input: a degreewise injective chain map with projective cokernel terms has an exact dual sequence for every coefficient module and every test algebra. For an obstruction theory the sequence degenerates: a degreewise injective obstruction theory is a quasi-isomorphism, so `IsObstructionTheory.acyclic_coker` shows that both `h⁰(coker φ)` and `h¹(coker φ)` vanish, and with a splitting the dual chain map is itself a quasi-isomorphism, upgrading the fully faithful comparison of `IsObstructionTheory.isCohomologicalMono_dualComplexHom` to an equivalence of the dual Picard groupoids. Missing: naturality of the dual six-term sequence in the coefficients and in the test algebra, so the exact sequence is not yet stated as a sequence of prestacks. **Cone quotients as torsor prestacks (`Cones/QuotientTorsor.lean`).** For an affine cone with a vector-bundle action `A : ConeAction R S F` and a basis `b` of `F` indexed by `σ`, the bundle is realised as a group algebraic space `vectorBundleGroup σ` and the cone as `coneActionSpace A b : AlgebraicSpaceAction`, with all group and action axioms proved (via `GrpObj.ofRepresentableBy` for the representable presheaf of groups `X ↦ (σ → Γ(X,⊤))`, and `actOn_zero`/`actOn_add` deduced from the `ConeAction` axioms). Because `AlgebraicSpaceGroup` is an absolute group object in fppf sheaves, the relative bundle `E = Spec Sym_R F` cannot be used directly; the ℤ-model `𝔾ₐ^σ = Spec ℤ[xᵢ]`, which base-changes to `E` over `Spec R` and has the same points over every `R`-algebra, is used instead. The comparison `trivialTorsorFunctor A b : [C/E](B) ⥤ ActionTorsor …(Spec B)` sends a point to the trivial torsor with target `(e,s) ↦ e·x(s)` and a translation to the corresponding translation; it is proved `Full` and `Faithful` (`trivialTorsorFunctorFullyFaithful`), the key input being `eq_translationMap`: every equivariant self-map of the trivial torsor over the base is a translation. Object-level naturality in the test algebra (`trivialTorsorFunctorNaturality`) compares `mapQuotient` with `ActionTorsor.pullbackFunctor`. `isoTrivialOfSection` proves unconditionally, from `principal_isIso`, that a torsor with a global section is isomorphic to a trivial one; essential surjectivity (`essSurj_trivialTorsorFunctor`) is therefore reduced to two explicit hypotheses, neither of which is proved: `AllTorsorsTrivial` (the vanishing of `H¹(Spec B, E)`) and the requirement that the point of the cone produced by a section lie over the given `R`-algebra structure. The pseudofunctor coherence of the naturality isomorphism and the stack property remain open. **Base change and fibre products of cone stacks (`Cones/StackFibreProducts.lean`).** For `C : ConeStack base O` and `f : StackHom base' base`, `ConeStack.baseChange` builds a cone stack over `base'` whose total stack is the genuine two-pullback `fiberTwoPullbackStack C.projection f` of `Stacks/TwoPullback.lean` (fibres: triples `(x, b, α : projection x ≅ f b)`), whose projection is the second projection of that two-pullback and whose vertex is the lift of `f ≫ C.vertex` produced by the bicategorical universal property `StackTwoPullback.canonicalLift`; its section property `baseChangeVertexProjectionIso` is that universal property's own comparison cell rather than an assumption. The fibrewise contraction `baseChangeContraction` contracts the first component and transports the comparison isomorphism through `contractionProjectionIso`; it lies over the new base on the nose. The same construction applied to the two projections of `C D : ConeStack base O` gives `ConeStack.prod`, together with the two projections `prodHomFst`, `prodHomSnd` as morphisms of cone stacks, which are strictly equivariant for the contractions. The total stacks, projections, vertices, section cells and contraction functors are unconditional; the five contraction coherence isomorphisms require compatibilities of `contractionOneIso`, `contractionMulIso`, `contractionZeroIso`, `contractionVertexIso` and `contractionPullbackIso` with `contractionProjectionIso` which `ConeStack` does not encode, and which are therefore explicit `Prop` hypotheses (`ConeStack.BaseChangeCoherence`, `ConeStack.ProdCoherence`) of the two constructions; no field of the produced cone stacks is assumed. Establishing those coherences (or adding them to `ConeStack` itself) and the pseudofunctoriality of base change remain open. **Coherence of cone stacks (`Cones/StackCoherence.lean`).** `ConeStack.IsCoherent` records the five higher coherence laws of the contraction data that `ConeStack` omits: each of `contractionOneIso`, `contractionMulIso`, `contractionZeroIso`, `contractionVertexIso` and `contractionPullbackIso` must project, through `contractionProjectionIso` — and, for the zero law, the section comparison `vertexProjectionIso`, for the reindexing law the strong-naturality cell `projectionNaturality` of the projection — to the expected comparison of the base. `BaseChangeCoherence.of_isCoherent` and `ProdCoherence.of_isCoherent` prove that these five laws imply all ten coherence hypotheses of `ConeStack.baseChange` and `ConeStack.prod`, so `baseChangeOfCoherent` and `prodOfCoherent` construct base changes and fibre products of coherent cone stacks with no hypotheses at all; `isCoherent_baseChange` shows every base change is coherent (its projection comparison is an identity), so iterated base changes remain unconditional. The concrete cone stacks of `Cones/Stack.lean` are coherent: `isCoherent_zeroConeStack`, `isCoherent_zeroAbelianConeStack`, `isCoherent_zeroVectorBundleStack`. The file also supplies the missing fibre-component lemmas for `StackIso2.symm`, `associator`, `whiskerLeft`, `whiskerRight`, the natural isomorphism `StackHom.naturalityIso`, and the comparison formulas for the points of the two-pullback constructions. Coherence of a fibre product of coherent cone stacks, and any coherence theorem asserting that all diagrams commute, remain open. (Round 13.) `GromovWitten/AlgebraicGeometry/Cones/QuotientTorsorStack.lean` supplies the ingredient recorded as missing in round 12: the scalar contraction of the affine cone quotient `[C/E]` realised as a torsor prestack.  Since fppf sheafification is unavailable, the pushout torsor `r_* P` is not constructed; instead the file isolates the groupoid `ConeQuotient.TrivialPoint G U T` of *trivialised* `G`-torsors over `T` with an equivariant map to `U` (objects are `T`-points of `U`, arrows are `T`-points of `G` translating one into the other), proves that `TrivialPoint.embedding : TrivialPoint G U T ⥤ ActionTorsor G U T` is fully faithful (`TrivialPoint.embeddingFullyFaithful`) and that it is an equivalence (`TrivialPoint.embeddingEquivalence`) under the single explicit hypothesis `AllTorsorsTrivial G T` (the vanishing of `H¹(T, G)`, not proved here).  Contractions are packaged as `ConeQuotient.ActionTwist G U T`, a *datum* consisting of a monoid endomorphism `grp` of the group of `T`-points of `G` and a self-map `pt` of the `T`-points of `U` satisfying the twisted equivariance `pt (g · t) = grp g · pt t`; it induces `ActionTwist.functor`, an endofunctor of `TrivialPoint G U T`, and, by transport along `embeddingEquivalence`, `ActionTwist.torsorContraction` on the whole torsor groupoid together with `ActionTwist.embeddingTorsorContractionIso` identifying the two on trivialised torsors.  For the cone quotient the twist is constructed, not assumed: `ConeQuotient.scaleRing A r φ` contracts a *ring*-valued point `φ : S →+* B` of the cone by `r : B` with no `R`-algebra structure on `B`, and `ConeQuotient.scaleRing_actOn` proves `c_r (v · x) = (r v) · c_r x` unconditionally, which is exactly the `pt_smul` field of `ConeQuotient.coneTwist A b r`.  All five cone-stack coherence laws are then proved unconditionally on trivialised torsors: `coneTwistOneIso`, `coneTwistMulIso`, `coneTwistZeroIso` and `coneTwistVertexIso` (these two take an explicit `IsConeVertex A.coaction ε` hypothesis, as in `Cones/Quotient.lean`), `coneTwistProjectionIso` (contraction is over the base) and `coneTwistPullbackIso` (compatibility with base change along any ring map `B →+* D`), the latter resting on the new `bundlePoint_naturality`.  Finally `quotientToTrivialPoint` factors `trivialTorsorFunctor` through `TrivialPoint` (`quotientToTrivialPoint_comp_embedding`, an equality of functors proved by `rfl`) and `contractionComparison` shows that the torsor contraction restricted to trivial torsors *is* `QuotientGroupoid.contractionFunctor A B r`; `coneTorsorContraction` and `coneTorsorContractionComparison` are the corresponding statements for all torsors under `AllTorsorsTrivial (vectorBundleGroup σ) (coneScheme B)`.  What remains missing is the pushout torsor `r_* P` for a general torsor (hence the unconditional contraction on `ActionTorsor`), the transport of the multiplicativity/zero/vertex/base-change isomorphisms from `TrivialPoint` to `ActionTorsor` (only the unit law, `coneTorsorContractionOneIso`, is transported), and the assembly into a `ConeStack base O`, which additionally needs `Pseudofunctor.IsStack` for `pullbackPseudofunctor` and a base stack for `Spec R`. `Cones/StackProdCoherence.lean` closes the fibre-product gap left open by `Cones/StackCoherence.lean`.  `ConeStack.isCoherent_prod` proves that `ConeStack.prod C D hp` is coherent whenever its **first** factor `C` is — the second factor needs no hypothesis beyond the `ProdCoherence C D` already required to form the product — and `ConeStack.isCoherent_prodOfCoherent` specialises this to `ConeStack.prodOfCoherent`, so iterated fibre products of coherent cone stacks, and mixed iterations with `ConeStack.baseChangeOfCoherent`, are unconditional.  The five laws are proved as `ConeStack.prodIsCoherent_one/mul/vertex/zero/pullback`; the unit, multiplication and vertex laws are literally the laws of `C` evaluated at `CategoricalPullback.fst p`, while the zero and reindexing laws use the two new component computations `ConeStack.prod_vertexProjectionIso_hom_app` and `ConeStack.prod_projectionNaturality_hom_app`, which identify the section comparison of the vertex of the fibre product and the strong-naturality cell of its projection with those of the first factor.  The universal property of the fibre product on fibres is `ConeStack.prodFiberOverEquiv : (prod C D hp).FiberOver T b ≌ C.FiberOver T b × D.FiberOver T b`, built from `prodFiberOverFunctor`, `prodFiberOverInverse`, `prodFiberOverUnitIso` and `prodFiberOverCounitIso` using the two-pullback description of the points, together with the new general constructor `ConeStack.FiberOver.isoMk`; `prodFiberOverFunctor_obj_fst_object` and `prodFiberOverFunctor_obj_snd_object` say it is compatible with `prodHomFst`/`prodHomSnd`, and `prodHomFst_equivariant_eq`/`prodHomSnd_equivariant_eq` record that both projections are strictly equivariant for the contractions (their `overBase` cells are computed by `prodHomFst_overBase_appIso_hom_app` and `prodHomSnd_overBase_appIso_hom_app`).  Finally `ConeStack.baseChangeIdEquivalence` gives the unit half of pseudofunctoriality of base change: the total stack of `baseChange C (id)` is equivalent to `C.total` as a `StackEquivalenceData`, compatibly with both projections (`baseChangeIdProjectionIso`, `baseChangeIdFstIso`).  What remains missing is the composition half of pseudofunctoriality — comparing `baseChange C (vcomp f g)` with `baseChange (baseChange C g hc) f` — which requires a pasting law identifying an iterated genuine two-pullback with a genuine two-pullback of the composite cospan (the repository proves pasting only for representable properties, in `Stacks/StackProducts.lean`); the identity comparison is also only at the level of total stacks, not upgraded to a `ConeStack.Iso`; and no coherence theorem asserting that all diagrams of a coherent cone stack commute is attempted. `Cones/StackBaseChangeComp.lean` proves the **pasting law for genuine bicategorical pullbacks of fppf stacks** and deduces the composition half of the pseudofunctoriality of base change of cone stacks, which `Cones/StackProdCoherence.lean` left open.  The enabling result is `StackTwoPullback.projectionIso2`: two morphisms into a genuine two-pullback are 2-isomorphic as soon as their two projections are, compatibly with the comparison cell — a strengthening of the uniqueness clause `IsBilimit.lift_unique`, which produces an invertible 2-cell with no control on its projections; it is obtained from the hom-category bijection `projectedTwoCell_bijective` (`projectionModification`, `projectionModification_fst/_snd`, `projectionIso2_fst_app`, `projectionIso2_snd_app`), together with the new modification-whiskering lemmas `whiskerRightModification_vcomp/_id/_toNatTrans_app`.  The pasting law itself is `StackTwoPullback.pastingEquivalence`: for `P : Genuine p g`, `Q : Genuine P.snd f` and any `R : Genuine p (vcomp f g)` it constructs a `StackEquivalenceData Q.pullback R.pullback` whose two legs are the bilimit lifts `pasteHom` and `pasteInv` of the pasted cone `pastedCone` and of the iterated cones `innerCone`/`midCone`, and whose inverse laws `pasteRoundTripIso` and `pasteBackIso` are proved from the three universal properties through the comparison-face computations `pasteRoundTripClassifies`, `pasteInnerCompat` and `pasteBackClassifies`; `pastingEquivalenceFst` and `pastingEquivalenceSnd` identify the projections of `R` with the composite `Q.fst ≫ P.fst` and with `Q.snd`.  On cone stacks this gives `ConeStack.baseChangeCompEquivalence`, an equivalence between the total stack of `baseChange (baseChange C g hc) f hc'` and that of `baseChange C (vcomp f g) hc''`, with `baseChangeCompEquivalenceProjection`, `baseChangeCompEquivalenceFst` and `baseChangeCompEquivalenceVertex` proving that it commutes with the projection to the new base, with the projection to `C.total`, and with the vertices (the last through the new comparison-face theorem `baseChangeCompVertexClassifies`); `baseChangeOfCoherentCompEquivalence` states the same for a coherent cone stack, where no coherence hypothesis has to be supplied.  What remains missing is the upgrade of this comparison (and of `baseChangeIdEquivalence`) to a `ConeStack.Iso`: the forward `overBase` and `mapVertex` cells are now available, but the compatibility of the comparison with the scalar contractions (the `equivariant` field of `ConeStack.Hom`) and the whole backward `ConeStack.Hom` are not proved; no associativity coherence for three composable base changes is attempted. The connecting map of a short exact sequence of two-term complexes agrees with Mathlib's (issue #56): `Cones/DeltaComparison.lean` computes the identifications `homologyNegOneIsoKer`/`homologyZeroIsoCoker` of `DerivedCriteria.lean` on homology classes and proves `delta_eq_δ`, that `PicardCriteria.ShortExact.delta` is Mathlib's `ShortComplex.ShortExact.δ` of the associated short exact sequence of cochain complexes, with `injective_delta_iff` transporting exactness statements across the identification. Still open: a base other than a ring and the big fppf site construction (blocked by the missing fppf stackification). `Cones/DeltaComparisonSequence.lean` completes the correspondence: the identifications of `h⁰`/`h¹` with Mathlib's homology are natural in chain maps (`kerLinearEquiv_naturality`, `cokerLinearEquiv_naturality`), each of the six exactness statements of the repo's sequence is equivalent to the corresponding statement about Mathlib's homology sequence (`exact_h0_middle_iff`, `exact_h1_middle_iff`, `exact_h1_left_iff`, `exact_h0_right_iff`, `injective_kernelMap_iff`, `surjective_cokernelMap_iff`), and `homology_exact_sequence` derives the repo's six-term exact sequence from `ShortComplex.ShortExact.homology_exact₁/₂/₃`. Functoriality of `E ↦ h¹/h⁰(E)` on derived morphisms (issue #59): `Cones/DerivedPicardFunctoriality.lean` realises every derived morphism `f : E ⟶ E'` between the objects of two K-projective global two-term resolutions by a chain map, unique up to chain homotopy (`GlobalTwoTermResolution.realize`, `realize_spec`, `realize_unique_upto_homotopy`, generalising `exists_chain_realization` from isomorphisms), and packages this as a functor `picardMap f : F.picard ⥤ F'.picard` with unit and composition coherence up to specified natural isomorphisms (`picardMap_id`, `picardMap_comp`) and an honest equivalence for invertible `f` (`picardEquivOfIso`); `Cones/DerivedPicardFunctorialityRoof.lean` extends this to arbitrary, possibly non-projective, truncation representatives `TruncRep` through the roof equivalences with a chosen auxiliary K-projective resolution (`TruncRep.picardMapRoof`, `picardMapRoof_id`, `picardMapRoof_comp`, `picardMapRoofEquivOfIso`), recovering `TruncRep.nonempty_picardEquivalence` as the case `f = 𝟙`. Strict 2-categorical coherence (pentagon and unit laws as equalities, and comparison across different auxiliary resolutions) remains open because the chain-level realisations and homotopy witnesses are non-canonical; representatives with `d⁰ = 0` cannot exist for objects with cohomology in positive degrees, so that item of the issue is void. |
| 5: intrinsic cones | `IntrinsicNormalCone/*`, `Cones/NormalConeAction.lean`, `Cones/NormalConeDimension.lean`, `Algebra/DimensionFormula.lean`, `Cones/NormalConeProduct.lean`, `Algebra/TensorSubspaceDistrib.lean`, `Cones/NormalConeProductInj.lean`, `Cones/AssociatedGradedGrading.lean`, `Cones/NormalConeProductInj2.lean`, `Cones/RefinementQuotient.lean`, `Cones/EmbeddingIndependence.lean`, `Cones/NormalConeBaseChange.lean`, `Cones/IntrinsicPullbackSequence.lean`, `Cones/NormalConeProductInj3.lean`, `Cones/NormalSheafProduct.lean`, `Cones/RefinementNormalSheaf.lean`, `Cones/SmoothAmbient.lean`, `Cones/SmoothAmbientEtale.lean`, `Cones/SmoothAmbientEtaleAction.lean`, `Cones/SmoothAmbientEtaleConeAction.lean`, `Cones/IntrinsicConeGluing.lean`, `Cones/IntrinsicConeGluingPoly.lean`, `Cones/IntrinsicConeGluingCover.lean`, `Cones/IntrinsicConeDescent.lean`, `Cones/PicardBaseChange.lean`, `Cones/ConormalBaseChange.lean`, `Cones/IntrinsicConeDescentInfinite.lean`, `Cones/SmoothQuasiregular.lean`, `Cones/SmoothIntrinsicNormalSheaf.lean`, `Cones/SmoothFormula.lean` |   the honest local-embedding type remains; the former records supplying local cones, descent, purity, abelian hulls, and relative comparisons are inactive; the proper point has a separately constructed global zero intrinsic cone. The affine local-embedding comparison is now proved in `Cones/NormalSheafPicard.lean`: `NormalSheafPicard.bundleQuotientEquivDualPoints` is an isomorphism of categories between the quotient groupoid of `ConeQuotient.bundleTranslationAction d` and the Picard groupoid of the `B`-points of the dual of `[N → F]`, strictly compatible with reindexing (`toDualPoints_naturality`) and with the `𝔾ₘ`-contraction (`contractionFunctor_comp_toDualPoints`), assembled into `NormalSheafPicard.quotientPrestackIsoDualPrestack` in `CommAlgCat A ⥤ Cat`; specialised to the conormal map it gives `NormalSheafPicard.AffineIntrinsicNormalSheaf.normalSheafPrestackIsoDualPrestack` and, through the honest chain-homotopy equivalence `conormalHomotopyEquivalence` with `CotangentComplex.AffinePresentation.twoTerm` of the presentation `R → R/I` (`quotientExtension_ker : ker = I`), the statement `normalSheafQuotientEquivPresentation` that the affine intrinsic normal sheaf `h¹/h⁰(L^∨)` of `R/I` over `k` is `[N_{U/M}/T_M|_U]` over every test algebra, with automorphisms `h⁰` and isomorphism classes `h¹` (`autEquivH0`, `isoClass_eq_iff`); the same file supplies `symProdEquiv : Sym(M × N) ≃ Sym(M) ⊗ Sym(N)`, the algebra input previously recorded as missing for fibre products of cone actions. Vistoli's lemma, the input to the intrinsic normal *cone*, is proved in the polynomial model in `Cones/ConeTranslation.lean`: for `M = 𝔸^σ_A = Spec R` and any ideal `I ⊆ R`, the translation `x ↦ x + ε t⁻¹` of Laurent polynomials preserves the extended Rees algebra (`ConeTranslation.translate_mem`, from the Taylor expansion `exists_translateLaurent_eq`), descends to the special fibre `gr_I(R)` of the deformation space and, through flat base change of the associated graded ring along `R → R[ε]`, yields the `R`-algebra coaction `ConeTranslation.coaction : gr_I(R) → gr_I(R)[ε_σ]` of the tangent bundle `T_M|_U = U × 𝔸^σ` on the affine normal cone, computed on degree-one classes as `[f t] ↦ [f t] + Σ_i ε_i [∂_i f]` (`coaction_degreeOne`) and proved to satisfy the counit and coassociativity laws (`coaction_counit`, `coaction_coassoc`; on `B`-points `translatePoint_zero`, `translatePoint_add`); the closed immersion `C_{U/M} → N_{U/M}` is proved equivariant, the coaction being compatible with the translation coaction `nsCoaction` of the normal sheaf `Spec Sym(I)/I·Sym(I)` through `nsToGr` (`coaction_comp_nsToGr`), which is exactly the statement that the tangent action on the normal sheaf preserves the normal cone; the action is packaged as the morphism of schemes `ConeTranslation.actionMap : C_{U/M} × 𝔸^σ → C_{U/M}`. What remains is the fppf stackification of these prestacks, the non-affine base, the passage from the naive presentation complex to the actual `L_X` of a stack, and Vistoli's lemma for a general smooth ambient `M` (only the ambient affine space, the local model, is treated) (Round 12.) **The tangent action on the affine normal cone as a cone action** (`Cones/NormalConeAction.lean`). The polynomial-model coaction `ConeTranslation.coaction : gr_I(R) →ₐ[R] gr_I(R)[ε_σ]` is packaged as an honest `ConeQuotient.ConeAction (R/I) (gr_I(R)) ((R/I) ⊗_R Ω[R⁄A])` — `NormalConeAction.normalConeAction` — for `R = A[x_i]`; the bundle is literally that of `AffineNormalCone.normalSheafTangentAction`, the `𝔸¹`-contraction is `GradedCone.normalConeCoactionQuot`, and all four `ConeAction` axioms are theorems: the unit and associativity laws come from `ConeTranslation.translatePoint_zero`/`translatePoint_add` through `NormalConeAction.translate_act`, and the `𝔾ₘ`-equivariance `NormalConeAction.conic_act` is deduced from that of the normal sheaf using surjectivity of `Sym_{R/I}(I/I²) ↠ gr_I(R)`. The bridge is `NormalConeAction.tensorEquiv`, built from `SymmetricAlgebra.equivMvPolynomial` for the basis `NormalConeAction.tangentBasis` of coordinate differentials and `MvPolynomial.algebraTensorAlgEquiv`. The mathematical core is `NormalConeAction.tangentToPoly_conormalMap`: the conormal map `I/I² → (R/I) ⊗ Ω` is the Taylor derivation `f ↦ Σ_i ε_i ∂_i f`, proved by uniqueness of derivations of a polynomial ring; from it `NormalConeAction.isEquivariant_nsToGrAlg` gives Vistoli's lemma in cone-action form, namely that the closed immersion `C_{U/M} ↪ N_{U/M}` is equivariant. Consequently `NormalConeAction.normalConeObstructionCone` instantiates `PicardCriteria.obstructionCone` unconditionally with the affine intrinsic normal cone and is fibrewise fully faithful and injective on isomorphism classes; `NormalConeAction.isEquivalence_quotientFunctor_of_bijective` shows `[C/T](B) ≌ [N/T](B)` whenever `Sym(I/I²) → gr_I(R)` is bijective (the lci case of `Cones/RegularSequence.lean`); and when `I/I² = 0`, in particular for `I = ⊥`, `NormalConeAction.nonempty_hom_of_subsingleton` and `NormalConeAction.autEquivTangent_of_subsingleton` identify `[C/T](B)` with the classifying groupoid of `T_M(B)`. What remains missing is the passage from this affine polynomial model to a general ambient smooth scheme and the global gluing of the resulting cone stacks. **Purity of the affine normal cone (`Cones/NormalConeDimension.lean`).** For a field `k`, a finitely generated `k`-domain `R` with `ringKrullDim R = n`, and a proper ideal `I`, the extended Rees algebra `A = R[It, t⁻¹]` is proved to be a Noetherian domain of finite type over `k` (`fg_extendedRees`, `finiteType_extendedRees`, `isNoetherianRing_extendedRees`) of Krull dimension `n + 1` (`ringKrullDim_extendedRees`, via `R[X] ↪ A ↪ R[t,t⁻¹]` and transcendence degrees). The parameter `t⁻¹` is a nonzerodivisor and is a non-unit whenever `I ≠ ⊤`, and every prime of `A` minimal over it has height exactly one (`height_eq_one_of_mem_minimalPrimes_parameterIdeal`, Krull's principal ideal theorem). Since `A/(t⁻¹) ≅ gr_I(R)`, this gives unconditionally `dim gr_I(R) ≤ n` and `dim (gr_I(R) ⧸ q) ≤ n` for every ideal `q` (`ringKrullDim_associatedGradedRing_le`, `ringKrullDim_quotient_le_of_ideal`), and unconditionally `dim gr_⊥(R) = dim R`. Granting the classical dimension formula `dim (A ⧸ p) + ht p = dim A` — stated as the explicit hypothesis `HasDimensionFormula`, since Mathlib does not yet know that finite type algebras over a field are catenary — every minimal prime `q` of `gr_I(R)` satisfies `dim (gr_I(R) ⧸ q) = n` (`ringKrullDim_quotient_eq_of_mem_minimalPrimes`), `dim gr_I(R) = n` (`ringKrullDim_associatedGradedRing`), and `Spec (gr_I R)` is pure of dimension `n` in the repository's sense (`pureTopologicalDimension_normalCone : Curves.PureTopologicalDimension n (AffineNormalCone.scheme R I)`). This is the affine algebraic form of Fulton B.6.6, the input to Behrend–Fantechi's statement that the intrinsic normal cone is pure of dimension zero. What remains missing is the dimension formula itself, and the globalisation from `Spec (gr_I R)` to the quotient stack `[C_{U/M}/T_M|_U]`. **The dimension formula for finite type domains over a field (`Algebra/DimensionFormula.lean`).** `ringKrullDim_quotient_add_height` proves, unconditionally, that for a field `k`, a finitely generated `k`-algebra `A` which is a domain and a prime `p`, `dim (A ⧸ p) + ht p = dim A` (Stacks 02JX, Matsumura 5.6). The proof is in three steps: `le_ringKrullDim_quotient_span_singleton` shows that cutting a polynomial ring in `s` variables by one nonzero non-unit equation leaves dimension at least `s - 1`, by presenting the ring as `D[X]` in a variable occurring in the equation and observing that `D` injects into the quotient; `ringKrullDim_quotient_succ_eq_of_height_eq_one` handles a height-one prime `P` of a finite type `k`-domain `B` by Noether normalization, using Mathlib's going-down for integral extensions of integrally closed domains to see that the contraction of `P` to the polynomial ring again has height one, hence is principal; `ringKrullDim_quotient_add_height_aux` then treats a general prime by induction along a chain realising its height, each step of which is saturated. As a consequence `hasDimensionFormula` discharges the `HasDimensionFormula` hypothesis of `Cones/NormalConeDimension.lean`, so `ringKrullDim_associatedGradedRing'` and `pureTopologicalDimension_normalCone'` give unconditionally that the affine normal cone `Spec (gr_I R)` of a proper ideal of an `n`-dimensional finitely generated domain over a field is pure of dimension `n` — the affine algebraic form of Fulton B.6.6 used by Behrend–Fantechi. What remains is the globalisation from the affine normal cone to the quotient stack `[C_{U/M}/T_M|_U]`. **Product formula for the affine normal cone (`Cones/NormalConeProduct.lean`).** For `k`-algebras `R`, `R'` and ideals `I`, `I'`, `productIdeal` is the ideal `J = I ⊗ R' + R ⊗ I'` of `U × U'` inside `M × M'`, and `grTensorToGr : gr_I(R) ⊗[k] gr_{I'}(R') →ₐ[k] gr_J(R ⊗[k] R')` is the canonical comparison map, built from the two maps of Rees algebras `reesLeftAlg`, `reesRightAlg` induced by the inclusions of the factors. `grTensorToGr_surjective` proves unconditionally that it is surjective — so `C_{U×U'/M×M'} → C_{U/M} × C_{U'/M'}` is a closed immersion — via `adjoin_productGenerators`, which shows that `Rees_J` is generated over `R ⊗[k] R'` by the degree-one monomials of the generators `I ⊗ 1` and `1 ⊗ I'`. `grTensorToGr_degreeOne_left` and `grTensorToGr_degreeOne_right` record compatibility with the canonical degree-one generators `conormalToAssociatedGraded`. The isomorphism `C_{U×U'/M×M'} ≅ C_{U/M} × C_{U'/M'}` (`grTensorEquiv`) and the resulting dimension equality are stated with the injectivity of `grTensorToGr` as an explicit hypothesis: over a field it follows from `J^n/J^{n+1} ≅ ⊕_{a+b=n} (I^a/I^{a+1}) ⊗_k (I'^b/I'^{b+1})`, whose distributivity input (`(A ⊗ R') ∩ (R ⊗ B) = A ⊗ B` for subspaces) is not in Mathlib and is not formalised here. Still missing: that injectivity; the normal sheaf analogue `N_{U×U'/M×M'} ≅ N_{U/M} × N_{U'/M'}`, which needs the same input plus base change for symmetric algebras on top of `NormalSheafPicard.symProdEquiv`; and `dim(A ⊗_k B) = dim A + dim B` for finite type `k`-domains, needed to turn the isomorphism into `dim C_{U×U'/M×M'} = dim R + dim R'`. **Distributivity for tensor products of subspaces (`Algebra/TensorSubspaceDistrib.lean`) and the product-ideal filtration (`Cones/NormalConeProductInj.lean`).** Writing `tensorSub A B` for the image of `A ⊗[k] B` in `V ⊗[k] W`, `tensorSub_top_inf_top_tensorSub` proves the distributivity law `(A ⊗ W) ⊓ (V ⊗ B) = A ⊗ B` over a field, without bases: over a field every subspace is the range of an idempotent endomorphism, `range (e ⊗ f) = A ⊗' B`, and membership in `A ⊗' B` is the fixed-point condition `(e ⊗ f)x = x`, so the statement follows from `(e ⊗ 1) ∘ (1 ⊗ f) = e ⊗ f`. On the ideal side, `Ideal.sup_pow_eq_sum` gives `(I ⊔ J)^n = Σ_{a ≤ n} I^a J^{n-a}` in any commutative ring (the binomial expansion in the idempotent semiring of ideals) and `productIdeal_pow_eq` specialises it to `J^n = Σ_{a+b=n} I^a I'^b` for the ideal of `U × U'`, upgrading the earlier one-sided bound. These are the two inputs to the identification `J^n/J^{n+1} ≅ ⊕_{a+b=n} (I^a/I^{a+1}) ⊗_k (I'^b/I'^{b+1})`. That identification, and hence the injectivity hypothesis of `grTensorEquiv`, is still open: it additionally needs `(A₁ ⊓ A₂) ⊗' ⊤ = A₁ ⊗' ⊤ ⊓ A₂ ⊗' ⊤` (flatness of the second factor) and a splitting of the finite part of the `I`-adic filtration, and the passage from degreewise to global injectivity needs a grading on `AffineNormalCone.associatedGradedRing`, which the repository does not yet carry. **The grading of the affine normal cone (`Cones/AssociatedGradedGrading.lean`).** `mem_reesIdeal_iff` proves that the ideal `I · Rees_I(R)` defining `AffineNormalCone.associatedGradedRing R I` is homogeneous, by combining the two existing coefficient lemmas of `Cones/Affine.lean` and `Cones/DeformationSpace.lean` into the description `q ∈ I · Rees_I(R) ↔ ∀ m, coeff m q ∈ I^{m+1}`. Polynomial truncation then descends to a family of `R`-linear projections `grProj R I n` on `gr_I(R)` which are idempotent (`grProj_idem`), pairwise orthogonal (`grProj_comp_of_ne`) and sum locally finitely to the identity (`sum_grProj`), with homogeneous parts `grPiece R I n`. The resulting `injective_of_degreewise` is the tool for deducing injectivity of a comparison map from injectivity in each degree. Two consequences are recorded: `associatedGradedBaseRingHom_injective` (the degree-zero part is exactly `R ⧸ I`) and `conormalToAssociatedGraded_injective` (the degree-one part is exactly the conormal module `I/I²`, so the comparison `N_{U/M} → C_{U/M}` is injective in degree one). The grading is given as orthogonal projections rather than as a Mathlib `GradedAlgebra` instance: Mathlib has no grading on `Polynomial`, none on `reesAlgebra`, and no graded structure on a quotient by a homogeneous ideal, and a `DirectSum.Decomposition` would require a non-canonical degree bound on quotient elements. Still open, and now the only obstruction to `grTensorEquiv` becoming unconditional: the lattice identity `(A₁ ⊓ A₂) ⊗' ⊤ = A₁ ⊗' ⊤ ⊓ A₂ ⊗' ⊤` (available in Mathlib in principle through `Module.Flat.rTensor_exact`), the degreewise isomorphism `J^n/J^{n+1} ≅ ⊕_{a+b=n} (I^a/I^{a+1}) ⊗_k (I'^b/I'^{b+1})`, and the induced total grading on `gr_I(R) ⊗[k] gr_{I'}(R')`. **The lattice of tensor products of subspaces, completed (`Cones/NormalConeProductInj2.lean`).** Over a field, `ker_rTensor_eq` proves `ker (f ⊗ 1) = (ker f) ⊗' ⊤` for every linear map `f`, by left exactness of `- ⊗ W` (`Module.Flat.rTensor_exact` with `LinearMap.exact_subtype_ker_map`), together with the bridging lemma `tensorSub_top_eq_range_rTensor` and the generalisation `range_tensorMap'` of the earlier range computation to arbitrary linear maps. Applying it to `(A₁.mkQ).prod (A₂.mkQ)` and the canonical isomorphism `(P × Q) ⊗ W ≅ (P ⊗ W) × (Q ⊗ W)` gives the one-sided distributivity law `inf_tensorSub_top : (A₁ ⊗' ⊤) ⊓ (A₂ ⊗' ⊤) = (A₁ ⊓ A₂) ⊗' ⊤`, its right-hand mirror `inf_top_tensorSub`, and, combined with `tensorSub_top_inf_top_tensorSub`, the two-sided law `inf_tensorSub : (A₁ ⊗' B₁) ⊓ (A₂ ⊗' B₂) = (A₁ ⊓ A₂) ⊗' (B₁ ⊓ B₂)`. Binary meets in the lattice of tensor products of subspaces are therefore fully computed. What remains for the injectivity of `grTensorToGr` is the distributivity of `⊓` over the *finite sums* `Σ_{a+b=n} I^a ⊗' I'^b`, which does not follow from binary meets and is best obtained by splitting the finite part of the `I`-adic filtration into complements `I^a = I^(a+1) ⊕ C_a`, and then the total-degree projections on `gr_I(R) ⊗[k] gr_{I'}(R')` intertwining with `AffineNormalCone.grProj`, at which point `AffineNormalCone.injective_of_degreewise` applies. Accordingly `grTensorEquiv` still carries its injectivity hypothesis. `Cones/RefinementQuotient.lean` proves the refinement lemma for local embeddings in the polynomial model, the compatibility that lets the local presentations `[C_{U/M}/T_M|_U]` glue. For an arbitrary ring `R`, ideal `I` and variable set `τ`, the ideal `polyExt τ I = I·R[y_τ] + (y_τ)` of the zero-section embedding has all its powers computed coefficientwise (`pow_eq_coeffFiltration`: `f ∈ (I')^N` iff `coeff m f ∈ I^(N − |m|)` for every `y`-monomial `m`), and from this the comparison map `prodMap : gr_I(R)[z_τ] → gr_{I'}(R[y_τ])`, sending `z_t` to the degree-one class of the new coordinate `y_t`, is proved surjective (`prodMap_surjective`, the degree-one class of a monomial of `y`-degree at least two vanishing) and injective (`prodMap_injective`, by lifting the coefficients to the Rees algebra and reading off the coefficientwise description `mem_map_rees_iff` of `I·Rees(I)`), giving the product formula `prodEquiv`, i.e. `C_{U/M'} = C_{U/M} × 𝔸^τ`, with no noetherian, regularity or flatness hypothesis. In the polynomial model `M = 𝔸^σ_A`, `M' = 𝔸^{σ⊕τ}_A` the ideal of the graph embedding `x ↦ (x, g(x))` is `graphIdeal A σ τ I g`, proved to present the same subscheme (`graphQuotientEquiv : R'/I' ≃ₐ[A] R/I`, both directions explicit), to equal `I·R' + (y_τ)` after the change of variables `y_t ↦ y_t + g_t` (`map_shear_graphIdeal`, `graphIdeal_zero_eq`), and `refinementEquiv` transports `prodEquiv` along `sumRingEquiv : A[x_σ,y_τ] ≃ (A[x_σ])[y_τ]`, computed on the degree-zero, degree-one and new-coordinate generators. The tangent translation action of `Cones/ConeTranslation.lean` respects this decomposition on `B`-points (`translatePoint_comp_refinementEquiv`, using the compatibility `taylor_incl` of the Taylor derivation with the inclusion of variables): the `𝔸^{σ⊕τ}`-action is the product of the `𝔸^σ`-action on `C_{U/M}` and the translation action of `𝔸^τ` on itself. The gate statement is `refinementQuotientEquivalence`: for every test algebra `B` the functor `refinementFunctor` between the action groupoids `ConeGroupoid` of `translatePoint` — i.e. `[C_{U/M'}/T_{M'}|_U](B) → [C_{U/M}/T_M|_U](B)` — is fully faithful and essentially surjective, so the quotient presentation of the intrinsic normal cone is unchanged by the refinement. For the normal sheaves only half is available: `nsProdMap : N_{U/M}[z_τ] → N_{U/M'}` is constructed, proved surjective (`nsProdMap_surjective`) and proved compatible with the closed immersions `C ⊆ N` (`nsToGr_comp_nsProdMap`), but its injectivity, hence `N_{U/M'} = N_{U/M} × 𝔸^τ` and the corresponding equivalence of quotient groupoids, is not proved; also missing are a general smooth `M' → M` (only the projection `𝔸^{σ⊕τ} → 𝔸^σ` with a graph embedding is treated) and any scheme-level or non-affine statement. `Cones/EmbeddingIndependence.lean` completes the affine gate: a lift `h` of one presentation `A[x_{σ'}] ↠ S` into another `A[x_σ] ↠ S` induces `presMap : gr_{I'} → gr_I` and the comparison functor `compareFunctor` of quotient groupoids, whose action on tangent vectors is the transpose Jacobian `compareVec` and whose well-definedness is the chain rule `taylor_liftHom` (`translatePoint_comp_presMap`); the comparisons compose strictly (`compareFunctorCompIso`), the identity lift gives the identity (`compareFunctorIdIso`), and two lifts of the same presentation give canonically isomorphic functors (`liftIso`, from `point_degreeOneRaw_sub`: the degree-one class of `h'(f) − h(f)` is the Taylor image of the classes `[h' t − h t]`), whence `presentationEquivalence` (two presentations have equivalent quotient groupoids over every test algebra) and `cocycleIso` (the Behrend–Fantechi cocycle condition for three presentations); `ker_jointPres` identifies the common refinement of two embeddings with the graph embedding of `RefinementQuotient`, and `nsToGr_comp_nsPresMap`, `point_comp_nsToGr`, `nsTranslatePoint_comp_nsPresMap` prove the comparison compatible with the closed immersions `C ⊆ N` and with the tangent translation on the normal sheaf. Still missing: the injectivity of `nsProdMap` (the product formula for normal *sheaves*, hence the normal-sheaf analogue of `refinementQuotientEquivalence`), a general smooth ambient `M' → M` (only the projection `𝔸^{σ⊕τ} → 𝔸^σ` and graph embeddings are treated), and any non-affine base or stackification. **Scalar extension of the affine normal cone and of its tangent action** (`Cones/NormalConeBaseChange.lean`). For a flat extension of coefficients `A → A'` — the case of a field extension `k ⊆ k'` — with `R = A[x_σ]`, `R' = A'[x_σ]` and `I' = I R'`, the affine local model of the intrinsic normal cone is unchanged. `NormalConeBaseChange.flat_mvPolynomial` lifts flatness to the polynomial rings and `NormalConeBaseChange.grBaseChangeEquiv` identifies `gr_{I'}(R')` with `R' ⊗_R gr_I(R)` (an instance of `AffineNormalCone.grTensorEquiv`), compatibly with the degree-zero and degree-one generators. The tangent action is unchanged: `NormalConeBaseChange.taylor_map` shows the Taylor derivation commutes with coefficient extension, whence `NormalConeBaseChange.coaction_comp_grMap` for the coaction of `Cones/ConeTranslation.lean` and `NormalConeBaseChange.translatePoint_grMap` for the action on `B`-points. At the level of the quotient groupoids of `Cones/NormalConeAction.lean`, `NormalConeBaseChange.pointEquiv` is the bijection of objects coming from the universal property of the base change and `NormalConeBaseChange.pointEquiv_translatePoint` shows it intertwines the actions, while `NormalConeBaseChange.translatePoint_of_hom` and `homOfTranslatePoint` describe the arrows of either groupoid as tangent vectors `σ → B` with the same translation law. Finally, for `σ` finite over a field, `NormalConeBaseChange.finrank_tangent` and `NormalConeBaseChange.ringKrullDim_gr` give `rank T_M|_U = #σ = dim gr_I(R)` for a proper ideal, so `NormalConeBaseChange.normalCone_dim_sub_rank_eq_zero` records that the two integers attached to the presentation `[C_{U/M}/T_M|_U]` differ by zero. Still missing: the packaging of the object and arrow bijections into an equivalence of quotient groupoids (which needs the `R/I → R'/I'` algebra plumbing), and the compatibility of the scalar extension with the closed immersions `Sym(I/I²) ↠ gr_I(R)`. `Cones/IntrinsicPullbackSequence.lean` builds Behrend–Fantechi's intrinsic pullback sequence in the affine model of a tower `R → S → T` presented by Mathlib generators `Q : Generators S T`, `P : Generators R S`. The chain-level Jacobi–Zariski triangle is realized as two chain maps `jzToComp`, `jzOfComp` between the presentation complexes `T ⊗_S [I/I² → Ω]`, `[J/J² → Ω]` and `[K/K² → Ω]`, whose degreewise exactness (middle exactness in both degrees, surjectivity on the right, injectivity on the left in degree `0`) is transported unconditionally from Mathlib's `Algebra.Generators.Cotangent.exact`, `CotangentSpace.exact`, `map_ofComp_surjective` and `map_toComp_injective`. Adding the single hypothesis that `T ⊗_S I/I² → J/J²` is injective, `shortExact_jz` turns the triangle into a `ShortExact` sequence of two-term complexes, from which the six-term Jacobi–Zariski cohomology sequence follows (`jzDelta`, `jz_exact_h0_middle`, `jz_exact_h0_right`, `jz_exact_h1_left`, `jz_exact_h1_middle`, `jz_surjective_h1`). When the relative conormal module `K/K²` is projective — the local complete intersection condition, the degree-`0` splitting being automatic since `Ω` of a presentation is free — `isDegreewiseSplit_jz` makes the sequence degreewise split, and `shortExact_dual_jz`/`shortExactPicard_dual_jz` dualise it: over every test algebra `B` the sequence `h¹/h⁰(L_{T/S}ᵛ)(B) → 𝔑_{T/R}(B) → (𝔑_{S/R} ×_{Spec S} Spec T)(B)` is exact in the Layer-4 sense, with `𝔑_{T/R}(B) → (𝔑_{S/R} ×_{Spec S} Spec T)(B)` essentially surjective, hom-fibres torsors under `h⁰(L_{T/S}ᵛ)(B)` and iso-fibres the orbits of the relative Picard groupoid. Finally `dualJzLeftEquivalence` identifies the last term with the intrinsic normal sheaf of `S` over `R` evaluated at `B` as an `S`-algebra, by the base-change adjunction. Missing: the two carried hypotheses are not derived from an lci or flatness assumption on `S → T`, and the comparison with the derived Jacobi–Zariski triangle of `CotangentComplex/Full.lean` is not formalized. (Round 13.) `Cones/NormalConeProductInj3.lean` closes the last gap in the product formula for the affine normal cone: over a field `k`, for `k`-algebras `R`, `R'` with ideals `I`, `I'` and `J = productIdeal k R R' I I'`, the comparison map `AffineNormalConeProduct.grTensorToGr : gr_I(R) ⊗[k] gr_{I'}(R') →ₐ[k] gr_J(R ⊗[k] R')` is injective (`grTensorToGr_injective`), so that `grTensorEquivOfField` is an isomorphism of `k`-algebras `gr_I(R) ⊗[k] gr_{I'}(R') ≃ₐ[k] gr_J(R ⊗[k] R')` (that is, `C_{U×U'/M×M'} ≅ C_{U/M} × C_{U'/M'}`) and `ringKrullDim_associatedGradedRing_productIdeal_of_field` is the unconditional equality of Krull dimensions; the injectivity hypotheses of `grTensorEquiv` and `ringKrullDim_associatedGradedRing_productIdeal` in `Cones/NormalConeProduct.lean` are thereby discharged, and the "what is not proved" paragraphs of `Cones/NormalConeProductInj.lean` and `Cones/NormalConeProductInj2.lean` are superseded.  The linear-algebra input is `GromovWitten.Algebra.ker_tensorMap` (`ker (f ⊗ g) = ker f ⊗' ⊤ ⊔ ⊤ ⊗' ker g` over a field, proved from `ker_rTensor_eq`, `ker_lTensor_eq` and `inf_tensorSub_top` by splitting off `ker f` with an idempotent) and its consequence `ker_tensorMap_le_ker_tensorMap`.  The geometric input is the monomial description of the graded pieces, `AffineNormalCone.grMonomial k R I n : I^n →ₗ[k] gr_I(R)` with `grMonomial_eq_zero_iff` and `ker_grMonomial` (the degree-`n` part is `I^n/I^(n+1)`), the bidegree decomposition `AffineNormalConeProduct.exists_sum_biProj` of `gr_I(R) ⊗ gr_{I'}(R')` by `biProj a b = grProj a ⊗ grProj b`, the computation `grTensorToGr_biMonomial` of the image of a bidegree monomial, and the separating functional `grQuotCoeff a b : gr_J(R ⊗ R') →ₗ[k] (R ⧸ I^(a+1)) ⊗[k] (R' ⧸ I'^(b+1))` (the degree-`(a+b)` coefficient read modulo `I^(a+1)`, `I'^(b+1)`), well defined by `productIdeal_pow_le_ker` which uses `productIdeal_pow_eq`.  Everything in the file is unconditional; `k` is a field throughout, which is genuinely needed (the kernel formula for `f ⊗ g` fails over a general base).  Not proved here: the analogous product formula for the normal *sheaf* `N_{U×U'/M×M'} ≅ N_{U/M} × N_{U'/M'}`, which still needs the degree-one statement `J/J² ≅ (I/I²) ⊗_k (R'/I') ⊕ (R/I) ⊗_k (I'/I'²)` together with base change for symmetric algebras on top of `NormalSheafPicard.symProdEquiv` and `GradedCone.baseChangeEquiv`. `Cones/NormalSheafProduct.lean` proves for the affine normal *sheaf* what `Cones/NormalConeProduct.lean` and `Cones/NormalConeProductInj3.lean` prove for the affine normal *cone*: for `k`-algebras `R`, `R'` with ideals `I`, `I'` and `J = productIdeal k R R' I I'`, the comparison map `AffineNormalSheafProduct.nsTensorToNs : N_{U/M} ⊗[k] N_{U'/M'} →ₐ[k] N_{U×U'/M×M'}` (that is, `normalSheafRing R I ⊗[k] normalSheafRing R' I' →ₐ[k] normalSheafRing (R ⊗[k] R') J`) is an isomorphism, `nsTensorEquiv`, so `N_{U×U'/M×M'} ≅ N_{U/M} × N_{U'/M'}`. The map is built from the functoriality `nsMapOfMapLe` of the normal-sheaf ring along a ring map carrying `I` into `J` (with `nsMapOfMapLe_algebraMap` and `nsMapOfMapLe_nsClass`), giving `nsLeftHom`/`nsRightHom` and, by the universal property of a tensor product of algebras, the `k`-algebra map `nsTensorToNs`, characterised on generators by `nsTensorToNs_nsClass_left` (`nsClass x ⊗ 1 ↦ nsClass (x ⊗ 1)`) and `nsTensorToNs_nsClass_right`.  Surjectivity (`nsTensorToNs_surjective`, via `algebraMap_mem_range` and `nsClass_mem_range`) holds over an arbitrary commutative base ring, by `ConeRefinement.ns_surjective` and a span induction on `productIdeal_eq_span`.  Injectivity (`nsTensorToNs_injective`) uses that `k` is a field, exactly as for the cone: over a field `I ⊆ R` and `I' ⊆ R'` are direct summands of `k`-vector spaces, so there are `k`-linear retractions `π : R → I`, `π' : R' → I'` (`LinearMap.exists_leftInverse_of_injective`), and the `k`-linear map `Λ (a ⊗ b) = nsClass (π a) ⊗ [b] + [a] ⊗ nsClass (π' b)` is `nsBase`-semilinear along `J` (`nsBase`, `nsBase_eq_zero_of_mem`, the generator computations and the span induction inside `exists_nsInverse`), which is precisely the input of the universal property `ConeRefinement.exists_nsRingHom` of `normalSheafRing`; the resulting ring map is a left inverse of `nsTensorToNs`.  The choice of the retractions is irrelevant on `J`, where `Λ` is pinned down by `Λ (x ⊗ 1) = nsClass x ⊗ 1` and `Λ (1 ⊗ y) = 1 ⊗ nsClass y` — so the degree-one statement `J/J² ≅ (I/I²) ⊗_k (R'/I') ⊕ (R/I) ⊗_k (I'/I'²)` is obtained implicitly and never has to be proved separately.  Finally `nsToGrK_comp_nsTensorToNs` (pointwise `nsToGr_nsTensorToNs`, and `nsToGr_nsTensorEquiv` for the isomorphisms) is the commuting square relating `nsTensorToNs`, `grTensorToGr` and the closed immersions `nsToGr : N → C`, proved from `nsToGr_nsLeftHom`, `nsToGr_nsRightHom`, `grLeft_algebraMap` and `grRight_algebraMap`; since both horizontal maps are isomorphisms over a field, the closed immersion `C_{U×U'/M×M'} ⊆ N_{U×U'/M×M'}` is the product of `C_{U/M} ⊆ N_{U/M}` and `C_{U'/M'} ⊆ N_{U'/M'}`.  Everything in the file is unconditional; `k` is a field only where the cone statement also needs it.  Not proved here: the passage from the affine model to a non-affine base or to a general smooth ambient space, and the sheafified statement for the intrinsic normal sheaf. `Cones/RefinementNormalSheaf.lean` closes the normal-sheaf gaps of `Cones/RefinementQuotient.lean` and `Cones/EmbeddingIndependence.lean`.  `exists_nsRingHom` is the universal property of `normalSheafRing A J = Sym_A(J)/J·Sym_A(J)` (a ring map `β : A →+* T` killing `J` plus an additive `β`-semilinear `L : J → T` defines a ring map out of it); applied to `β v = [v(0)]` and `L f = [f(0)] + Σ_t [∂_{y_t} f(0)] z_t` it produces a left inverse of `nsProdMap` (`nsProdMap_leftInverse`), hence `nsProdMap_injective` and the normal-sheaf product formula `nsProdEquiv : N_{U/M}[z_τ] ≃+* N_{U/M'}` for `I' = polyExt τ I`, the sheaf analogue of `prodEquiv` that was previously missing.  Transporting it along `sumRingEquiv` gives `nsRefinementEquiv : MvPolynomial τ (Ns I) ≃+* Ns (graphIdeal A σ τ I 0)`, compatible with the closed immersion `C ⊆ N` (`nsToGr_comp_nsRefinementEquiv`).  With the action axioms `nsTranslatePoint_zero`/`nsTranslatePoint_add` and the equivariance `nsTranslatePoint_comp_nsRefinementEquiv`, the action groupoid `NsGroupoid I B = [N_{U/M}/T_M|_U](B)` is defined and `nsRefinementQuotientEquivalence` proves that `nsRefinementFunctor : NsGroupoid (graphIdeal A σ τ I 0) B ⥤ NsGroupoid I B` is an equivalence of groupoids for every test algebra `B`; `coneToNsFunctor` and `coneToNsRefinementIso` say that this equivalence is compatible with the cone-level `refinementFunctor` through `nsToGr`.  The same groupoids also carry the presentation-independence package: `nsCompareFunctor` (with `nsCompareVec`, `nsPresMap_id`, `nsPresMap_comp`), `nsCompareFunctorIdIso`, `nsCompareFunctorCompIso`, canonicity `nsLiftIso` (via `point_nsClass_sub` and `nsTranslatePoint_nsLiftHomotopy`), the equivalences `nsCompareEquivalence` / `nsPresentationEquivalence` and the cocycle isomorphism `nsCocycleIso`.  Everything is proved unconditionally in the affine polynomial model; what remains open is the same as for the normal cone — a general smooth ambient space, non-affine bases and the fppf stackification. `Cones/SmoothAmbient.lean` carries the whole tangent-translation package of `Cones/ConeTranslation.lean` from the polynomial model `P = Amb A σ = A[x_i]` to an arbitrary **flat** `P`-algebra `R`, which covers all the ambient charts the global gluing needs: an open chart `D(f) ⊆ 𝔸^σ`, an arbitrary localisation `S⁻¹P`, and (finitely presented) étale or smooth charts over `𝔸^σ`.  Writing `J = I·R` (`ConeLocalisation.locIdeal`) and `gr_J(R)` (`ConeLocalisation.GrLoc`), flat base change of the Rees algebra gives `ConeLocalisation.grLocEquiv : R ⊗_P gr_I(P) ≃ₐ[R] gr_J(R)` (an instance of `AffineNormalCone.grTensorEquiv`), the generation lemma `grLoc_ringHom_ext`, and, through the base-change adjunction `AlgHom.liftEquiv`, the coaction `ConeLocalisation.locCoaction : gr_J(R) →ₐ[R] gr_J(R)[ε_σ]` characterised by `locCoaction_grLocMap` and `locCoaction_algebraMap` and unique with that property (`locCoaction_unique`). The normal sheaf is treated in parallel (`NsLoc`, `nsLocEquiv`, `locNsCoaction`), and Vistoli's lemma is carried across base change: `locCoaction_comp_nsToGr` proves that the closed immersion `C_{U/M'} ↪ N_{U/M'}` is equivariant for the tangent translation, with the point-level form `locNsTranslatePoint_comp_nsToGr`. Specialisations are `flat_of_isLocalization`, `flat_of_isLocalizationAway`, `flat_of_etale`, `flat_of_smooth`, and `locIdeal_comap`, which records that every ideal of a localisation of `A[x]` is extended, so the results apply to an arbitrary closed subscheme of an open chart. For `[Algebra.FormallyEtale (Amb A σ) R]`, `Algebra.FormallySmooth.liftOfSurjective` then produces the **Taylor expansion** `EtaleAmbient.taylor n : R →ₐ[Amb A σ] Shift A σ R n`, which reduces to the identity modulo `(ε)` (`redAlg_taylor`, `constantCoeff_of_taylor`), is unique with that property (`taylor_unique`, from `Algebra.FormallyUnramified.lift_unique'`), is compatible with the truncations `Shift A σ R n → Shift A σ R m` (`trunc_comp_taylor`), and reduces in the polynomial model to the substitution `x_i ↦ x_i + ε_i` (`taylor_self`, `taylor_X`); localisations are covered since `Algebra.FormallyEtale.of_isLocalization` provides the instance. Summing them over the coefficients of a Rees element gives a ring map `EtaleAmbient.reesPhi : Rees_J(R) →+* gr_{I'}(R[ε])` (`phiPoly_add`, `phiPoly_monomial_mul`, `phiPoly_mul`) killing `J·Rees_J(R)` (`map_le_ker_reesPhi`), hence the **tangent coaction** `EtaleAmbient.grPhi : gr_J(R) →+* gr_{I'}(R[ε])` and, in the coordinates of `ConeRefinement.prodEquiv`, `EtaleAmbient.coactionEtale : gr_J(R) →+* gr_J(R)[z_σ]`.
