The abstract cone of `PicardCriteria.obstructionConeFunctor` is instantiated with honest affine cone quotients: `PicardCriteria.obstructionCone` sends a cone `[C/T](B)` equipped with an equivariant closed immersion into the normal sheaf to `h¹/h⁰(Eᵛ)(B)`, and is proved fully faithful (`obstructionConeFullyFaithful`, via the new `quotientFunctorFullyFaithfulOfSurjective`) and injective on isomorphism classes (`obstructionCone_injective_isoClass`) — the fibrewise meaning of "closed subcone stack" — as well as natural in the test algebra and invariant under homotopy equivalence of `E`. The instantiations with the full normal sheaf (`normalSheafObstructionCone`), with the affine intrinsic normal sheaf of `I ⊆ R` (`affineNormalSheafObstructionCone`) and with the presentation cotangent complex of `R → R/I` (`presentationObstructionCone`) are unconditional. The two-term notion of an obstruction theory is identified with the derived one: `PicardCriteria.isObstructionTheory_iff_derived` and `derivedObstructionTheory` show that `IsObstructionTheory φ` is exactly `H⁰` iso and `H⁻¹` epi for `DerivedCategory.Q.map (toCochainComplexHom φ)`, using an explicit `ShortComplex.LeftHomologyMapData` identifying the cohomology of `E.toCochainComplex` in degrees `0` and `-1` with `h1 E` and `h0 E`. Perfectness in this model is finite freeness of the two terms (`IsPerfectTwoTerm`); `isFiniteFreeComplex_dualPoints` and `virtualRank_dualPoints` show that every fibre `h¹/h⁰(Eᵛ)(B)` is then the Picard groupoid of a two-term complex of finite free `B`-modules of virtual rank `-virtualRank E`, i.e. a vector-bundle stack. Finally `isObstructionTheory_iff_exact_cone` reformulates the notion as right exactness of `E⁻¹ → L⁻¹ ⊕ E⁰ → L⁰ → 0`, whence `isObstructionTheory_sum_iff` (external direct sums, both directions) and `IsObstructionTheory.baseChange` (base change along *any* `R`-algebra, with no flatness hypothesis), and `GlobalTwoTermResolution.sum` gives stability of global two-term resolutions under biproducts in the derived category. Still missing: the tangent action on the affine normal cone of `Cones/ConeTranslation.lean` is built in the polynomial model and is not packaged as a `ConeQuotient.ConeAction`, so the obstruction cone of the normal cone itself is available only through the equivariance hypotheses of `PicardCriteria.obstructionCone`. **Deformation-theoretic meaning of an obstruction theory (Behrend–Fantechi §4), affine model.** `ObstructionTheory/DeformationMeaning.lean` connects the two-term obstruction-theory criterion of `AffineCriterion.lean` with the square-zero deformation theory of `CotangentComplex/SquareZero.lean`. The Picard dual is redone with arbitrary module coefficients (`PicardCriteria.dualComplex`, `dualComplexHom`, `dualModuleH0Equiv`), and `IsObstructionTheory.isCohomologicalMono_dualComplexHom` proves that an obstruction theory dualises to a cohomological monomorphism for *every* coefficient module, with no finiteness or projectivity hypothesis. The groups `ExtOne R M P` and `ExtZero R M P` are identified on the nose with `h¹` and `h⁰` of that dual (`extOneEquivDualHOne`, `extZeroEquivDualHZero`, `obstructionGroupEquivDualHOne`), which lets the obstruction class be pushed to `obstructionE M P φ ∈ ExtOneE M E`. The two main theorems are `obstructionE_eq_zero_iff` — the obstruction of `E` vanishes exactly when the square-zero lifting problem for `A` is solvable — and `exists_unique_extZeroEVAdd` together with `liftEquivExtZeroE`: the solutions form a simply transitive set under `ExtZeroE M E ≃ Der_R(A, M)`, with `liftIsoClassEquivExtZeroE` describing the isomorphism classes of the lifting groupoid. `obstructionE_push` is naturality along a morphism of square-zero extensions. Finally the obstruction is realised as an *object* `obstructionObject M P φ F` of the cone groupoid `h¹/h⁰(Eᵛ)` whose isomorphism class is `obstructionE`, and `liftEquivTrivialisation` (with the equivariance `trivialisationOfLift_extZeroEVAdd`) proves Behrend–Fantechi's statement that the lifting groupoid is the torsor of trivialisations of that object; the splitting `derSection` of `restrictDer` makes this constructive. Missing: naturality of `obstructionE` under flat base change (only the `Push` version is proved), and the globalisation of all of this beyond the affine presentation model. **Obstruction space at a geometric point (Layer 6, final bullet).** `ObstructionTheory/PointObstruction.lean` completes the affine deformation picture of `DeformationMeaning.lean`. `obstructionE_baseChange` supplies the base-change naturality of the obstruction class of `E` along `R → R'` (via `SquareZeroExt.basePush`, over a fixed `A`; the genuine change of `A` to `R' ⊗ A` is not treated). For a point `x : A → κ` with values in a field, `fibreComplex` is the fibre `κ ⊗[A] E` and `ObSpace`/`TanSpace` are `h¹`/`h⁰` of its `κ`-dual; `finite_obSpace` shows `Ob(x)` is finite dimensional when the terms of `E` are finite. The main theorem `extOneEquivObTensor` proves that for every coefficient module `N` which is a `κ`-vector space, `h¹` of the dual complex of `E` with coefficients in `N` — where the obstruction lives — is `Ob(x) ⊗[κ] N`, so a single finite-dimensional space receives the obstruction of every square-zero deformation problem over the point; `obSpaceEquiv` identifies `Ob(x)` with `h¹(dualComplex E κ)` and `h0DualEquivDerivation` identifies the degree-zero part with `Der_R(A, N)`, in particular the tangent space with `Der_R(A, κ)`. `pointObstruction` and `pointObstruction_eq_zero_iff` give the obstruction in `Ob(x) ⊗[κ] M` and its vanishing criterion. For extensions with a one-dimensional kernel, `curvilinearObstruction` is a single element of `Ob(x)`, homogeneous under rescaling the trivialisation (`curvilinearObstruction_smulOfUnit`), and `CurvilinearObstructions` with `smul_mem_curvilinearObstructions` and `zero_mem_curvilinearObstructions` exhibits the set of such obstructions as a `κˣ`-stable cone. Still missing: the truncated-polynomial family `κ[t]/(tⁿ⁺¹) → κ[t]/(tⁿ)` realising the curvilinear extensions, and the identification of that cone with the fibre of the intrinsic normal cone. **Curvilinear extensions (Layer 6).** `ObstructionTheory/Curvilinear.lean` constructs the curvilinear square-zero extensions `κ[t]/(tⁿ⁺¹) → κ[t]/(tⁿ)`: `curvRing`, `curvIdeal`, `isSquareZero_curvIdeal` (needing `n ≥ 1`, carried as `[NeZero n]`), `curvQuotEquiv` identifying the quotient with `κ[t]/(tⁿ)`, and `curvTrivialisation` proving that the kernel `(tⁿ)/(tⁿ⁺¹)` is the `κ`-line spanned by the class of `tⁿ`. `IsJet` is the condition that the `A`-algebra structure of `κ[t]/(tⁿ)` reduces to the point modulo `t`, and `isScalarTowerCoeff` proves its essential consequence: the `A`-action on the kernel factors through the point, which is what puts the obstruction of a jet in `Ob(x) ⊗ κ`; `constIsJet` and `constLift` show the constant jet is a jet and always extends. The file also re-proves, under the correct hypotheses on the coefficients (`[Module κ (Coeff M)]`, `[IsScalarTower A κ (Coeff M)]` instead of `PointObstruction.lean`'s stronger `[Algebra κ (B ⧸ M)]`, `[IsScalarTower A κ (B ⧸ M)]`, which excludes jets of order ≥ 2), the obstruction at a point: `pointObstructionAt`, `curvilinearObstructionAt` and its vanishing criterion and `κˣ`-scaling law, and `CurvilinearObstructionsAt` with `smul_mem_curvilinearObstructionsAt` and `curvilinearObstructions_subset`. Still missing: the set of obstructions of the truncated-polynomial extensions themselves, blocked by a typeclass diamond (two non-reducibly-equal instance paths for `Semiring` and `SMul` on an iterated quotient ring) that prevents `Lift R (curvIdeal κ n) P.Ring` from elaborating; the module docstring records the diagnosis and two ways to fix it. The identification of the curvilinear cone with the fibre of the intrinsic normal cone remains untouched. **The strict curvilinear obstruction cone (Layer 6).** `ObstructionTheory/CurvilinearStrict.lean` completes the curvilinear picture by keeping the ambient ring abstract: `IsCurvilinear κ M` records an isomorphism `B ≃ₐ[κ] κ[t]/(tⁿ⁺¹)` carrying `M` onto `(tⁿ)/(tⁿ⁺¹)`, so that all instances come from the variables as in `CotangentComplex/SquareZero.lean` and the typeclass diamond that blocked `Curvilinear.lean` disappears. The transported results are `isSquareZero_of_isCurvilinear`, `curvilinearTrivialisation` (the kernel is a `κ`-line), `curvilinearQuotEquiv` (`B ⧸ M ≃ₐ[κ] κ[t]/(tⁿ)`) and `curvilinearAug_mul_eq_zero`; `IsJetOver` is the jet condition and `isScalarTower_coeff_of_isJetOver` proves that a jet makes `A` act on the kernel through the point. `CurvilinearObstructions'` is then the set of obstructions of the curvilinear extensions over all orders, curvilinear structures, jets and trivialisations, and it is proved to be contained in `CurvilinearObstructionsAt` (`curvilinearObstructions'_subset`), stable under `κˣ` (`smul_mem_curvilinearObstructions'`, by rescaling the trivialisation — `exists_units_smul_trivialisation` shows this is the `κˣ`-orbit of the canonical trivialisation, whereas reparametrising `t ↦ ct` would only give `n`-th powers) and to contain the origin (`zero_mem_curvilinearObstructions'`, from the constant `1`-jet, which lifts to `κ[t]/(t²)`); together, the strict curvilinear obstructions form a cone in `Ob(x)`. `jetOneEquivTangent` identifies the first-order jets at `x` with `Der_R(A, κ)`. Missing: the identification of this cone with the fibre of the intrinsic normal cone. `ObstructionTheory/DerivedInvariance.lean` settles two Layer-5/6 items. First, being an obstruction theory is invariant under isomorphism of the source in the derived category: `homotopyEquivalenceOfDerivedIso` turns an isomorphism `Q(E) ≅ Q(E')` of the cochain realizations of two-term complexes of finite free modules into a chain homotopy equivalence (K-projectivity, via `exists_homotopyEquiv_of_derivedIso` and `ofHomotopyEquiv`), `nonempty_chainHomotopy_of_derivedIso` upgrades the compatibility with the target `L` to a chain homotopy `φ' ∘ e ≃ φ` using the homotopy uniqueness `nonempty_homotopy_of_Q_map_eq`, and `isObstructionTheory_iff_of_derivedIso` concludes `IsObstructionTheory φ ↔ IsObstructionTheory φ'`; `nonempty_natIso_dualHom_quotientFunctor` and `nonempty_natIso_obstructionCone` show that the induced functors on the dual Picard groupoids, hence the obstruction cones of `Cones/NormalConeAction.lean`, agree up to a natural isomorphism over every test algebra. Second, the hypotheses of the intrinsic pullback sequence are discharged in the smooth case: `injective_jzToComp_degreeZero_of_flat` proves the conormal map `T ⊗_S I/I² → J/J²` injective when `S` is formally smooth over `R` and `T` is flat over `S`, and `projective_cotangent_of_formallySmooth` proves `K/K²` projective when `T` is formally smooth over `S`, so that `shortExact_jz_of_flat` and `shortExactPicard_dual_jz_of_smooth` give the intrinsic pullback sequence with no remaining hypothesis in that case. A caveat recorded in the file: plain flatness of `T` over `S` is not sufficient for the degreewise short exactness — the obstruction is the image of `H₂(L_{T/S})` in `T ⊗ H₁(L_{S/R})`, which is why formal smoothness of `S` over `R` is assumed, and Mathlib contains no injectivity statement for that map. External direct sums of obstruction theories (issue #68): `ObstructionTheory/ExternalSum.lean` constructs the external sum `externalSum k E E'` of two-term complexes over `S ⊗[k] S'`, proves rank additivity (`virtualRank_externalSum`, and the vector-bundle-stack version `virtualRank_dualPoints_externalSum`), preservation of perfectness, that an external sum of obstruction theories is an obstruction theory (`isObstructionTheory_externalSum`) and the split-triangle transport case (`isObstructionTheory_of_iso_sum`); `ObstructionTheory/ExternalSumConormal.lean` identifies, for a field `k` and ideals `I ⊆ k[x_σ]`, `I' ⊆ k[x_τ]`, the conormal complex of the product embedding `k[x_{σ⊕τ}] ⧸ (I + I')` with the external sum of the base changes of the two conormal complexes (`conormalProdEquiv`, a homotopy equivalence with zero homotopies; degree zero `J/J² ≅ (T ⊗_S I/I²) × (T ⊗_{S'} I'/I'²)` by an explicit retraction from a `k`-linear splitting of `I`, degree one through the `dX_i` bases with `degreeOneInl_D` compatibility with `KaehlerDifferential.D`), so that an external sum of obstruction theories is an obstruction theory for the product embedding with additive virtual rank (`isObstructionTheory_externalSum_conormal`). Still open: non-polynomial ambient presentations and base rings that are not fields, products of non-affine schemes or stacks, the vector-bundle-stack statement at stack level, general (non-split) triangle compatibility and pullback along general morphisms. |

`VirtualFundamentalClass/LciFormulaPure.lean` |      the proper point has a constructed Rees cone, perfect identity POT, rank-zero target, direct rank-zero resolved formula, virtual/fundamental equality, and degree one; the former general scheme/stack resolved-cone and independence packages are inactive (Round 14.) `VirtualFundamentalClass/ResolvedCone.lean` constructs the Behrend--Fantechi resolved cone `C(E) ⊆ E₁` in the affine two-term model. For a chain map `φ : E → conormalComplex k R I` the file defines the coordinate rings `bundleRing φ = Sym(E⁻¹)` and `productRing φ = gr_I(R) ⊗ Sym(E⁰)`, the coordinate-ring map `productMap φ` of `C ×_X E₀ → E₁`, `(c, e₀) ↦ φ^∨(c) + d^∨(e₀)`, and takes `C(E)` to be the closed subscheme `scheme φ = Spec (Sym(E⁻¹) ⧸ ker (productMap φ))`; `toBundle_isClosedImmersion`, `toProduct_injective` and `fromProduct_toBundle` prove that this is a closed subscheme of `E₁` through which `C ×_X E₀ → E₁` factors and whose coordinate ring embeds into that of `C ×_X E₀`, that is, that `C(E)` is the scheme-theoretic image, and `le_ideal_of_productMap_eq_zero` records the corresponding universal property. On test algebras, `mem φ B` is the condition that a `B`-point of `E₁` lie in `C(E)`; `mem_of_cone_add` shows the image of `C(B) × E₀(B)` lies in `C(E)(B)`, `mem_comp` gives naturality in `B`, and `mem_add` proves that `C(E)` is stable under translation by `E₀`, from the equivariance `map_productMap_comp_coaction` together with flatness of `Sym(E⁰)` — this one statement, and the descent `ringCoaction` of the translation coaction to `C(E)`, carry the hypothesis `[Module.Free (R ⧸ I) E.degreeOne]`. Finally `mem_obstructionCone_obj` and `mem_of_hom_obstructionCone_obj` identify the `B`-point underlying the image of an object of `[C/T](B)` under `PicardCriteria.obstructionCone` with `conePoint φ B c.point 0` and conclude that it, and everything isomorphic to it in `h¹/h⁰(E^∨)(B)`, lies in `C(E)`. The dimension theory of `C(E)` (purity, trivialisation of the `T_M`-torsor `C ×_X E₀`) and the virtual class itself are not in this file. `VirtualFundamentalClass/ResolvedConeDimension.lean` is the dimension theory of the resolved cone `C(E)` in the polynomial model `R = k[x_σ]`. Its general core is `eq_map_comap_C_of_translationCoaction`, the invariant ideal lemma: an ideal of `D[x_σ]` carried by the translation coaction `x_i ↦ x_i + t_i` into the ideal it generates in `D[x_σ][t_σ]` is extended from `D`; this is the algebraic form of the statement that a translation invariant closed subscheme of affine space is a cone over the base, and it is the tool with which the `T_M|_U`-torsor `C ×_X E₀ → C(E)` is to be trivialised. The file also proves, unconditionally, that the minimal primes of a polynomial ring are the extensions of the minimal primes of the base (`map_C_mem_minimalPrimes`, `comap_C_mem_minimalPrimes`, `map_comap_C_of_mem_minimalPrimes`) together with the dimension shift `ringKrullDim_quotient_map_C`, that `gr_I(R) ⊗ Sym(E⁰)` is a polynomial ring in `rank E⁰` variables over `gr_I(R)` (`productRingEquiv`), and that the mapping-cone surjection `L⁻¹ ⊕ E⁰ → L⁰` of an obstruction theory splits (`exists_rightInverse_coneBeta`). Combining these with the purity of the affine normal cone gives `ringKrullDim_quotient_productRing`: every irreducible component of `C ×_X E₀` has dimension `|σ| + rank E⁰`, under exactly the hypothesis `HasDimensionFormula (extendedRees R I)` already isolated in `Cones/NormalConeDimension.lean`. Purity of `C(E)` itself, `ringKrullDim_quotient_ring_eq` (`dim (ring φ ⧸ q) = rank E⁰` for every minimal prime `q`), and its packaged form `dimension_eq_of_isMax` (`∀ x, IsMax x → dim x = rank E⁰` for a certified dimension function of `C(E)`, and `dimension_toBundle_eq_of_isMax` on the ambient bundle `E₁`) carry in addition the explicit hypothesis `IsPolynomialTrivialisation φ`, that the torsor `C ×_X E₀ → C(E)` is trivial; that hypothesis is stated but not proved, and is the only gap between this file and unconditional purity of the resolved cone. `VirtualFundamentalClass/ResolvedConeTrivialisation.lean` closes the gap left by `ResolvedConeDimension.lean`: `ResolvedCone.isPolynomialTrivialisation` proves, for an obstruction theory `φ` in the polynomial model `R = k[x_σ]`, that the coordinate ring `gr_I(R) ⊗ Sym(E⁰)` of `C ×_X E₀` is a polynomial ring in `|σ|` variables over the coordinate ring of the resolved cone `C(E)`, compatibly with the inclusion `toProduct φ`; geometrically, the `T_M|_U`-torsor `C ×_X E₀ → C(E)` is trivial. The construction is the diagonal translation coaction `ResolvedCone.productCoaction` of the tangent bundle, built from Vistoli's coaction on the normal cone (`ConeTranslation.coaction`) and from the translation of `E₀` along `φ.degreeOne`; `ResolvedCone.productCoaction_productMap` shows that the resolved cone is invariant, since `coneBeta φ ∘ coneAlpha φ = 0`. A splitting of the mapping-cone surjection supplies `|σ|` fibre coordinates (`ResolvedCone.torsorCoord`) that the coaction translates by the tangent parameters, and the resulting comparison map `ResolvedCone.torsorHom : ring φ [t_σ] → gr_I(R) ⊗ Sym(E⁰)` is proved surjective (`ResolvedCone.torsorHom_surjective`, from the exactness of the mapping-cone sequence) and equivariant (`ResolvedCone.map_torsorHom_translationCoaction`); the invariant ideal lemma `eq_map_comap_C_of_translationCoaction` then forces its kernel to be extended from `ring φ`, hence zero because `toProduct φ` is injective. Consequently `ResolvedCone.ringKrullDim_quotient_ring_eq'`, `ResolvedCone.dimension_eq_of_isMax'` and `ResolvedCone.dimension_toBundle_eq_of_isMax'` give purity of `C(E)` (every irreducible component has dimension `rank E⁰`) under exactly one remaining hypothesis, the dimension formula `HasDimensionFormula (extendedRees R I)` already isolated in `Cones/NormalConeDimension.lean`. Nothing here leaves the polynomial model and no étale-local or global version is attempted. `VirtualFundamentalClass/Construction.lean` builds the Behrend--Fantechi virtual fundamental class from the resolved cone of `VirtualFundamentalClass/ResolvedCone.lean` and the zero-section Gysin map of `IntersectionTheory/BundleHomotopy.lean`. For an obstruction theory `φ : E → conormalComplex k R I` with `E⁻¹` finite free over `R ⧸ I` it defines the rank `bundleRank φ = a`, the expected dimension `virtualDimension φ = rk E⁰ - rk E⁻¹` (equal to `PicardCriteria.virtualRank E` and hence a chain-homotopy invariant, by `virtualDimension_eq_virtualRank` and `virtualDimension_eq_of_homotopyEquivalence`), and the resolved-cone cycle `resolvedConeCycle φ dimE ∈ Z_{vd+a}(E₁)` as the graded pushforward of the fundamental cycle of `C(E)` along the closed immersion `C(E) ↪ E₁`; `resolvedConeCycle_eq_properPushforward_fundamental` shows that under the purity statement proved in `ResolvedConeDimension.lean` (carried here as an explicit hypothesis) nothing is truncated. From the surjectivity of the flat pullback it derives `exists_virtualClass` and the canonical class `virtualClassQuot` in `A_{vd}(X) ⧸ ker π^*`, which need no injectivity assumption, and then the virtual class itself, `virtualClass hhom hinj : A_{vd}(X)`, with `pullback_virtualClass` (`π^*[X]^vir = [C(E)]`) and `virtualClass_unique`. Two inputs remain explicit hypotheses: homogeneity of principal divisors on `E₁` (the hypothesis of the localisation sequence) and injectivity of `π^*`, the half of homotopy invariance that needs Chern classes and is not available in this development. The file also checks the construction in rank zero: `ideal_eq_bot` and `isIso_toBundle` prove that for `E⁻¹ = 0` the resolved cone is the whole of `E₁ = X`, and `virtualClass_eq_fundamental` together with `virtualClass_point` prove that the virtual class of a reduced point with the zero obstruction theory is the class of its fundamental cycle in degree `0`. Nothing here globalises the construction beyond the affine model with a global two-term resolution, and no functoriality or deformation invariance of the virtual class is proved. `VirtualFundamentalClass/Independence.lean` proves Behrend--Fantechi, Proposition 5.3, in the affine model: the virtual class of `VirtualFundamentalClass/Construction.lean` does not depend on the chosen global two-term resolution, up to isomorphism of resolutions. For two obstruction theories `φ : E → L` and `φ' : E' → L` over the same conormal complex related by a chain map `ψ : E → E'` with bijective components and `φ' ∘ ψ = φ` in degree `-1`, `ideal_map_bundleEquiv` shows that the induced isomorphism `bundleEquiv` of the coordinate rings of `E₁` and `E'₁` carries the ideal of `C(E)` onto the ideal of `C(E')`, so that `ringEquiv`, `bundleSpaceIso` and `coneSchemeIso` identify the two resolved cones compatibly with their closed immersions (`toBundle_comp_bundleSpaceIso_inv`); `map_fundamentalCycle_of_isIso` supplies the missing Chow lemma that an isomorphism of locally Noetherian schemes carries the fundamental cycle to the fundamental cycle, and `flatPullbackOpen_resolvedConeCycleAt` and `chowEquivOfAlgEquiv_resolvedConeClassAt` then match the two resolved-cone cycles and classes. Because the two canonical trivialisation index types and the two virtual dimensions agree only propositionally, the classes are compared through a degree-generic variant `virtualClassAt`, which is proved by `rfl` to agree with `virtualClass` for the canonical choices (`virtualClassAt_trivialization`); the results are `chowPullbackBundle_eq_resolvedConeClassAt_iff` and `virtualClassQuotAt_congr` (no injectivity hypothesis at all), `virtualClassAt_congr` and `virtualClass_eq_virtualClassAt` (equality of the virtual classes themselves, with the homogeneity and injectivity hypotheses of `Construction.lean` transported rather than reassumed), and `virtualDimension_congr`. For the second half of Proposition 5.3, adding an acyclic summand `[F = F]`, the file proves `isObstructionTheory_sumAcyclic` and, with `productInl_comp_productMap`, that `C(E)` is the scheme-theoretic image of `C(E ⊕ [F = F])` under the bundle projection (`comap_ideal_sumAcyclic`), together with the inclusion `C(E') ⊆ p⁻¹(C(E))` and the invariance of the virtual dimension; the scheme-theoretic equality `C(E') = p⁻¹(C(E))` and the resulting equality of virtual classes are **not** proved, because they need the isomorphism `Sym(M ⊕ F) ≅ Sym(M)[X_j]` and a flat-base-change statement for kernels which Mathlib does not provide. Invariance under a general chain-homotopy equivalence of obstruction theories is not proved. `VirtualFundamentalClass/IndependenceAcyclic.lean` completes the second half of Behrend–Fantechi, Proposition 5.3, in the affine model: adding an acyclic direct summand `[F --id--> F]` to a global two-term resolution does not change the virtual fundamental class. `symProdTensorEquiv` supplies the decomposition `Sym(M × N) ≅ Sym M ⊗ Sym N` missing from Mathlib and `mem_map_includeLeft_of_tensorMap_eq_zero` the flat description of the kernel of `f ⊗ id`; together they give `ideal_sumAcyclic`, the scheme-theoretic equality `C(E ⊕ [F = F]) = p⁻¹(C(E))` which upgrades the two inclusions of `Independence.lean`. On the cycle side, `length_self_eq_of_flat_local` proves that a flat local extension with extended maximal ideal preserves the length of the local ring, hence `genericLength_bundlePoint` and `pullbackBundle_fundamentalCycle`: the fundamental cycle of the total space of a trivialised affine vector bundle is the flat pullback of the fundamental cycle of the base; combined with `pullbackBundle_map_quotImm` (the compatibility of flat pullback with the pushforward along the closed immersion cut out by an arbitrary, not necessarily prime, ideal) and with the trivialisation `acyclicTrivialization` of the bundle projection `p : E'₁ ⟶ E₁`, this gives `pullbackBundle_map_toBundle`, the equality `[C(E')] = p^* [C(E)]` of resolved-cone cycles. At the level of Chow groups `chowPullbackBundle_eq_resolvedConeClassAt_sumAcyclic` shows, without any injectivity hypothesis, that a class on the base is a virtual class for `E` if and only if it is one for `E ⊕ [F = F]`, and `virtualClassAt_sumAcyclic` and `virtualClass_eq_virtualClassAt_sumAcyclic` give the equality of the virtual classes themselves, the homogeneity and injectivity hypotheses being descended from `E'₁` to `E₁` rather than assumed twice. What remains missing for the full Proposition 5.3 is the reduction of a general chain-homotopy equivalence of obstruction theories to an isomorphism after adding acyclic summands; injectivity of `π^*` remains the single inherited mathematical gap. (Round 15.) The file `VirtualFundamentalClass/QuasiIsoSplitting.lean` supplies the purely algebraic input for chain-homotopy invariance of the virtual class: for a map `f : Hom E F` of two-term complexes of modules over a commutative ring `S`, `QuasiIsoSplitting.extend f : Hom E (F.sum (acyclicComplex S E.degreeOne))` and `QuasiIsoSplitting.cokernelMap f` form a complex, and `exact_extend_cokernelMap`, `extend_degreeZero_injective` and `cokernelMap_surjective` prove that `0 → E⁻¹ → F⁻¹ ⊕ E⁰ → F⁰ → 0` is short exact whenever `f` is a quasi-isomorphism. Assuming in addition that `F.degreeOne` is a projective `S`-module, `chosenSection f hf` splits this sequence, `retraction f hf` is the induced retraction (`extend_retraction`), and `iso f hf` is an isomorphism of two-term complexes `F ⊕ [E⁰ = E⁰] ⟶ E ⊕ [F⁰ = F⁰]` with an explicit inverse `isoInv f hf` (`isoInv_comp_iso`, `iso_comp_isoInv`, `iso_degreeZero_bijective`, `iso_degreeOne_bijective`, `iso_isQuasiIsomorphism`). The degree-zero homotopy formula `coprod_comp_iso_degreeZero` shows that, for `φ : Hom E L` and `ψ : Hom F L` with `ψ.degreeZero ∘ₗ f.degreeZero = φ.degreeZero`, transporting `coprod φ.degreeZero 0` along `iso f hf` gives `coprod ψ.degreeZero 0` plus `homotopy f hf ψ` composed with the differential — exactly the hypothesis shape consumed by the homotopy invariance of the resolved-cone ideal. The file also proves the general lemma `isQuasiIsomorphism_of_bijective` and the `Module.Free` / `Module.Finite` instances for the terms of a direct sum of two-term complexes. Nothing geometric is proved here; the passage to virtual classes is left to Task Q3. `VirtualFundamentalClass/HomotopyInvariance.lean` proves that the resolved cone, and hence the whole virtual-class construction, depends on an obstruction theory `φ : E ⟶ conormalComplex k R I` only up to chain homotopy in degree `-1`. The construction is the translation automorphism `HomotopyInvariance.translationAut φ h` of the coordinate ring `gr_I(R) ⊗_{R/I} Sym(E⁰)` of `C ×_X E₀` attached to a linear map `h : E⁰ →ₗ I/I²`: it is the identity on the normal-cone factor and sends a generator `ι y` to `γ(h y) ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ ι y`; `translationAlgHom_comp` and `translationAlgHom_zero` show that translations compose additively, so it is an automorphism, and `translationAut_productMap` shows it intertwines `ResolvedCone.productMap φ` with `ResolvedCone.productMap φ'` whenever `φ'.degreeZero = φ.degreeZero + h ∘ E.differential`. Consequently `HomotopyInvariance.ideal_congr` gives `ResolvedCone.ideal φ' = ResolvedCone.ideal φ`, and — after re-expressing the resolved-cone cycle through the ideal-parametrised `quotientCycle`, whose type does not mention the ideal — `resolvedConeCycle_congr`, `resolvedConeClass_congr`, `virtualClass_congr` and `virtualClassAt_congr` show that the resolved-cone cycle, the resolved-cone class and the virtual fundamental class are unchanged, with the same `hhom` and `hinj`; `virtualDimension_congr` holds by `rfl`. The chain-homotopy corollaries (`ideal_congr_chainHomotopy`, `resolvedConeCycle_congr_chainHomotopy`, `virtualClass_congr_chainHomotopy`, `virtualClassAt_congr_chainHomotopy`) and the invariance of the obstruction-theory condition (`isObstructionTheory_of_chainHomotopy`) are recorded as well. What this file does **not** contain is the invariance under a quasi-isomorphism of two different complexes `E`, `F`; that is the subject of `QuasiIsoSplitting.lean` and `QuasiIsoInvariance.lean`. `VirtualFundamentalClass/QuasiIsoInvariance.lean` completes Behrend–Fantechi, Proposition 5.3, in the affine model: `QuasiIsoInvariance.virtualClassAt_eq_of_quasiIso` proves that two obstruction theories `φ : E ⟶ L` and `ψ : F ⟶ L` on perfect two-term complexes which are related by a quasi-isomorphism `f : E ⟶ F` with `ψ⁻¹ ∘ f⁻¹ = φ⁻¹` define the same virtual fundamental class in `RX.ChowGroup`. The proof is the four-step chain `[X]^vir_φ = [X]^vir_Φ = [X]^vir_{Ψ'} = [X]^vir_Ψ = [X]^vir_ψ` through the two acyclically-extended theories `Φ = φ ⊕ 0` on `E ⊕ [F⁰ = F⁰]` and `Ψ = ψ ⊕ 0` on `F ⊕ [E⁰ = E⁰]`: the outer two equalities are round 14's `VirtualClass.virtualClassAt_sumAcyclic`, the isomorphism step is round 14's `VirtualClass.virtualClassAt_congr` applied to the splitting isomorphism `LinearTwoTermComplex.QuasiIsoSplitting.iso f hf` (its compatibility being `rfl`), and the middle step is round 15's `HomotopyInvariance.virtualClassAt_congr` applied to the degree `-1` homotopy of `QuasiIsoSplitting.coprod_comp_iso_degreeZero`, recorded here as `degreeZero_sumAcyclic_comp_iso`. The file also supplies what the intermediate complexes need: `isPerfectTwoTerm_acyclicComplex` and `isPerfectTwoTerm_sumAcyclic` for perfectness, `isObstructionTheory_comp_of_isQuasiIsomorphism` and `isObstructionTheory_sumAcyclic_comp_iso` for the obstruction-theory property of `Ψ' = Φ ∘ Θ`, and the rank identities `finrank_add_finrank_of_quasiIso`, `card_chooseBasisIndex_sum_of_quasiIso` and `virtualDimension_eq_of_quasiIso`. Two corollaries specialise the main theorem to a chain-homotopy equivalence (`virtualClassAt_eq_of_homotopyEquivalence`) and to compatibility of `φ` and `ψ` only up to a degree-zero homotopy (`virtualClassAt_eq_of_quasiIso_homotopy`), and `virtualClass_eq_of_quasiIso` states the result for the canonical trivialisations, with `VirtualClass.virtualClass φ` on the left. Nothing in this file discharges the homogeneity and injectivity hypotheses of the virtual class; those are the subject of Part I of the round. `VirtualFundamentalClass/Unconditional.lean` removes the two explicit hypotheses that round 14's construction of the virtual fundamental class had to carry, replacing them by two conditions on the base ring `R ⧸ I` alone: `VectorBundle.HasUniversalDimensionFormula (R ⧸ I)` (the dimension formula for every prime quotient of every finitely generated polynomial algebra) and `VectorBundle.UnitDifferences (R ⧸ I)` (an infinite subset with unit pairwise differences). `Unconditional.hhomOf` derives the homogeneity of principal divisors on the bundle space `E₁ = Spec Sym(E⁻¹)` from the first hypothesis via `VectorBundle.principalDivisorsHomogeneous_of_hasUniversalDimensionFormula`, and `Unconditional.hinjAt` / `Unconditional.hinjOf` derive the injectivity of the flat pullback `π^*` from both, via `VectorBundle.chowPullbackBundle_injective`; `Unconditional.virtualClass φ dimX dimE RX RE hdim hunit` is then round 14's `VirtualClass.virtualClass` with these two proofs supplied, characterised by `Unconditional.pullback_virtualClass` and `Unconditional.eq_virtualClass_of_pullback_eq` and equal to the zero-section Gysin pullback of the resolved-cone class along the now unconditional isomorphism `VectorBundle.zeroSectionGysinEquiv''` (`Unconditional.virtualClass_eq_zeroSectionGysinEquiv''`, proved by `rfl`). `Unconditional.virtualClass_eq_of_quasiIso` restates round 15's quasi-isomorphism invariance with the same two hypotheses as its only inputs: the auxiliary homogeneity `hhom₁` and injectivity `hinj₁` for the bundle space of `F ⊕ [E⁰ = E⁰]`, which `QuasiIsoInvariance.virtualClass_eq_of_quasiIso` still takes as arguments, are discharged by `hhomOf` and `hinjAt` applied to the trivialisation of that complex; the corollaries `Unconditional.virtualClass_eq_of_homotopyEquivalence` and `Unconditional.virtualClass_eq_of_quasiIso_homotopy` do the same for a chain-homotopy equivalence and for compatibility up to a degree-zero homotopy. Beyond `hdim` and `hunit` the inputs are exactly those of round 14 (`R` Noetherian, `E` perfect, `φ : E ⟶ conormalComplex k R I`); neither ring hypothesis is proved for a concrete ring anywhere in the repository, so the construction is not yet unconditional for, say, finite-type algebras over a field. (Round 16.) `GromovWitten/Algebra/FiniteTypeDimensionFormula.lean` derives the dimension formula for finitely generated algebras over a field from the height one case. `height_add_ringKrullDim_quotient_le` proves the trivial inequality `dim (A ⧸ p) + ht p ≤ dim A` in an arbitrary commutative ring by concatenating chains, via `Order.krullDim_eq_iSup_height_add_coheight_of_nonempty` and the repository lemma `PrimeSpectrum.coheight_eq_ringKrullDim_quotient`. `height_map_quotient_add_one_le` proves, for a Noetherian domain and primes `⊥ ≠ p₁ ≤ p`, that the height of the image of `p` in `A ⧸ p₁` is at least one less than `ht p`, by pulling a maximal chain back along `PrimeSpectrum.comap (Ideal.Quotient.mk p₁)` and prepending the zero ideal. `ringKrullDim_quotient_add_height_aux_of_hG1` is the induction on the height: the next-to-last term `q` of a maximal chain ending at `p` has height `n`, the image of `p` in `A ⧸ q` has height one, and the hypothesis `hG1` (the height one case, for all finitely generated `k`-domains) closes the step; `ringKrullDim_quotient_add_height_of_hG1`, `hasDimensionFormula_of_finiteType_of_hG1` and `hasUniversalDimensionFormula_of_finiteType_of_hG1` package this as `HasDimensionFormula A` and as `IntersectionTheory.VectorBundle.HasUniversalDimensionFormula R` for every finitely generated `k`-algebra `R` (not necessarily a domain). Everything in the file is proved; `hG1` is the only hypothesis, and it is already available unconditionally in the repository as `GromovWitten.Algebra.ringKrullDim_quotient_succ_eq_of_height_eq_one`, so no statement here is conjectural. `VirtualFundamentalClass/OverField.lean` removes the last two ring-theoretic hypotheses of the affine construction of the virtual fundamental class. For a finitely generated algebra `R` over an infinite field `k` and a proper ideal `I`, `OverField.hasUniversalDimensionFormula` derives `VectorBundle.HasUniversalDimensionFormula (R ⧸ I)` from `GromovWitten.Algebra.FiniteTypeDimensionFormula.hasUniversalDimensionFormula_of_finiteType` together with `OverField.finiteType_quotient`, and `OverField.unitDifferences` derives `VectorBundle.UnitDifferences (R ⧸ I)` from `VectorBundle.unitDifferences_of_field`. With these in hand, `OverField.virtualClass φ dimX dimE RX RE` is the virtual class of an arbitrary perfect two-term obstruction theory over such a ring, and every theorem of `Unconditional.lean` is restated without `hdim`/`hunit`: the defining property `pullback_virtualClass`, uniqueness (`eq_virtualClass_of_pullback_eq`, `virtualClass_unique`), the Gysin description `virtualClass_eq_zeroSectionGysinEquiv''`, and the Behrend–Fantechi invariance theorems `virtualClass_eq_of_quasiIso`, `virtualClass_eq_of_homotopyEquivalence`, `virtualClass_eq_of_quasiIso_homotopy`. In the polynomial model `R = k[x_i]_{i ∈ σ}` with `σ` finite the purity statements of `ResolvedConeDimension.lean` and `ResolvedConeTrivialisation.lean` are likewise restated without their conditional input `HasDimensionFormula (extendedRees …)`, which `OverField.hasDimensionFormula_extendedRees` supplies from `GromovWitten.Algebra.hasDimensionFormula_extendedRees`; the primed forms `ringKrullDim_quotient_ring_eq'`, `dimension_eq_of_isMax'`, `dimension_toBundle_eq_of_isMax'` have no conditional input left at all. What is still assumed everywhere is the standing affine setting itself: the instance `[IsNoetherianRing R]` (redundant, but not derivable by instance search — `isNoetherianRing_of_finiteType` discharges it), `[Nontrivial (R ⧸ I)]`, freeness and finiteness of the terms of `E`, and `[Infinite k]`; the globalisation to schemes is started in round 17 below, and nothing here reaches Deligne–Mumford stacks. `VirtualFundamentalClass/DegreeOneSummand.lean` proves that adjoining a summand `W` to the degree-one term of the obstruction complex does not move the resolved cone. For a chain map `φ : E ⟶ conormalComplex k R I` and a linear map `j : W →ₗ (R ⧸ I) ⊗[R] Ω[R⁄k]`, `LinearTwoTermComplex.addDegreeOne` and `LinearTwoTermComplex.Hom.addDegreeOne` build the complex `[E⁻¹ → E⁰ × W]` with differential `x ↦ (d x, 0)` and the chain map `φ⁰.coprod j`; `DegreeOneSummand.productMap_addDegreeOne` factors the coordinate-ring map of `C ×_X E₀ ×_X 𝔸(W) → E₁` through the injective algebra map `productInclusion φ j = id ⊗ Sym(inl)`, and `DegreeOneSummand.ideal_addDegreeOne` deduces the equality `ResolvedCone.ideal (φ.addDegreeOne j) = ResolvedCone.ideal φ` of ideals of the same ring `Sym(E⁻¹)`. From it, via the round-15 `HomotopyInvariance.quotientCycle` machinery, `resolvedConeCycleAt_addDegreeOne`, `resolvedConeClassAt_addDegreeOne` and `virtualClassAt_addDegreeOne` show that the resolved-cone cycle, the resolved-cone class and the degree-generic virtual class are unchanged, while `virtualDimension_addDegreeOne` and `coneDegree_addDegreeOne` record the shift by `Module.finrank (R ⧸ I) W` (for a nontrivial `R ⧸ I` and finite free `E⁰`, `W`) and `bundleRank_addDegreeOne`, `trivialization_addDegreeOne` record that the bundle `E₁` and its trivialisation are literally unchanged. The definition `relativeVirtualClassAt φ e dimX dimE d` places the virtual class of `φ` in the degree `virtualDimension φ + d` expected of an obstruction theory relative to a smooth base of dimension `d` (Behrend–Fantechi §7), and `virtualClassAt_addDegreeOne_eq_relativeVirtualClassAt` together with `virtualClass_addDegreeOne_eq_virtualClassAt` and `virtualClass_addDegreeOne_eq_relativeVirtualClassAt` express the absolute virtual class of `φ.addDegreeOne j`, including in the hypothesis-free form of `Unconditional.lean`, as the relative virtual class of `φ` in relative dimension `rk W`. On the obstruction-theory side the file proves `cokernelMap_comp_inclusionQ` and deduces that `H⁻¹` is unchanged (`surjective_kernelMap_addDegreeOne_iff`) and that `H⁰` descends (`isObstructionTheory_of_addDegreeOne`, under `range j ≤ range φ⁰`); what is *not* true, and is documented as such, is the converse for a nonzero `W`, since `φ.addDegreeOne j` adds `W` to `H⁰` and is therefore never an obstruction theory unless `W` is a subsingleton (`isObstructionTheory_addDegreeOne`). Missing: the identification of the adjoined datum with a genuinely relative obstruction theory over a smooth base (that is task F1c, in the polynomial model), and any statement relating the two `RationalEquivalenceSystem` degrees other than by fixing a common explicit degree. (Round 17.) `VirtualFundamentalClass/LocalisationCone.lean` performs the base change of an affine obstruction datum and of its resolved cone along an arbitrary flat, formally étale extension `R → R'` (the case `R' = Localization.Away f` being the one needed to glue the affine construction over a basic-open cover). With `I' = I·R'`, `B = R ⧸ I` and `B' = R' ⧸ I'`, the file proves `LocalisationCone.flat_baseExt : Module.Flat B B'` and the pushout square `LocalisationCone.isPushoutBaseExt : Algebra.IsPushout R B R' B'`, and constructs the two comparison *isomorphisms* `LocalisationCone.cotangentComparison : B' ⊗_B I/I² ≃ₗ[B'] I'/I'²` (from `AffineNormalCone.quotTensorIdealEquiv` and the flat base change of an ideal `AffineNormalCone.idealTensorEquiv`) and `LocalisationCone.kaehlerComparison : B' ⊗_B (B ⊗_R Ω[R⁄k]) ≃ₗ[B'] B' ⊗_{R'} Ω[R'⁄k]` (from `KaehlerDifferential.tensorKaehlerEquivOfFormallyEtale`), together with the naturality square `LocalisationCone.kaehlerComparison_baseChange_conormalMap`; this yields `LocalisationCone.baseChangeHom φ : Hom (E.baseChange B') (conormalComplex k R' I')` for every affine obstruction datum `φ`. On the cone side it proves the flat base change of the associated graded ring, `LocalisationCone.grBaseExtEquiv : B' ⊗_B gr_I(R) ≃ₐ[B'] gr_{I'}(R')`, by combining `Algebra.IsPushout.cancelBaseChangeAlg` with `AffineNormalCone.grTensorEquiv`, and hence the two coordinate-ring identifications `LocalisationCone.bundleRingEquiv` and `LocalisationCone.productRingEquiv` together with the commuting square `LocalisationCone.productRingEquiv_comp_productMap`. Flatness of `B'` over `B` then gives `LocalisationCone.ideal_map_bundleRingEquiv` (the ideal of the base-changed resolved cone is the extension of `ResolvedCone.ideal φ`) and `LocalisationCone.ringEquiv : ResolvedCone.ring (baseChangeHom φ) ≃ₐ[B'] B' ⊗_B ResolvedCone.ring φ`. Specialising to `R'` a localisation away from `f`, `LocalisationCone.isLocalization_away_baseExt` and `LocalisationCone.isLocalization_away_ring` state that `B'` and the coordinate ring of the base-changed resolved cone are the localisations of `B` and of `ResolvedCone.ring φ` away from the image of `f` — the form in which the resolved cone will be glued into a quasi-coherent algebra on `Spec B`. Everything is unconditional (no extra hypotheses beyond flatness and formal étaleness, both of which are proved automatic for localisations); what is still missing is the transfer of `PicardCriteria.IsObstructionTheory` along `baseChangeHom` and the scheme-level assembly. `VirtualFundamentalClass/GlobalCone.lean` carries the affine resolved cone of Behrend–Fantechi over to a general scheme. A `GlobalCone.GlobalConeData 𝓔 φ` consists of a vector bundle `𝓔 : VectorBundleTotalSpace.BundleData X ι`, a second quasi-coherent algebra `cone` on `X` presented as a quotient of `𝓔.algebra` by a morphism of algebra data `inclusion` with surjective components, and, over every chart `𝓔.chart j`, ring isomorphisms `chartBundle j : 𝓔.algebra.ring (𝓔.chart j) ≃+* ResolvedCone.bundleRing (φ j)` and `chartCone j : cone.ring (𝓔.chart j) ≃+* ResolvedCone.ring (φ j)` intertwining `inclusion.app` with `Ideal.Quotient.mk (ResolvedCone.ideal (φ j))`; the affine obstruction data `φ j` are parameters, not fields. `GlobalConeData.coneι` is the closed immersion `C ↪ E₁` of the cone `coneScheme = Spec_X cone` into the total space (`isClosedImmersion_coneι`), lying over the base (`coneι_toBase`), and `bundleChartι`/`coneChartι` are the open immersions of the affine models `ResolvedCone.bundleSpace (φ j)` and `ResolvedCone.scheme (φ j)` into the total space and the cone, with the commuting square `coneChartι_coneι` and the affine pullback square `isPullback_coneChart`. The main result `pullbackOpen_bundleChartι_coneCycleOf` proves that the global cone cycle `coneCycleOf` — the residue-degree pushforward of the fundamental cycle of `C` along `C ↪ E₁` — restricts on every chart to the affine resolved-cone cycle of `φ j`, and its graded form `coneCycleAt_eq_resolvedConeCycleAt` identifies the truncation with `VirtualClass.resolvedConeCycleAt (φ j)`; both are unconditional, resting on the general `pullbackOpen_map_of_isClosedImmersion` and on `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackOpen_fundamentalCycle` from `IntersectionTheory/CycleGluing.lean`. The global class `coneClassAt` and the virtual class `virtualClassOf`, `pull_virtualClassOf`, `virtualClassOf_unique`, `existsUnique_virtualClass` are defined **modulo the global homotopy property of Chow groups**: the flat pullback `pull : A_i(X) → A_d(E₁)`, its injectivity and the fact that the cone class lies in its image are explicit hypotheses, since no Chow-group pullback along a non-trivialised bundle over a non-affine base exists in the repository. Missing: no `GlobalConeData` is constructed from geometry, the compatibility of `chartBundle` with the polynomial trivialisations `𝓔.triv j` and `VirtualClass.trivialization (φ j)` is not imposed, and there is no statement that the global cone cycle is independent of the choice of charts or of the affine models. `VirtualFundamentalClass/GlobalConeAffine.lean` is the acceptance test of the globalised cone of `GlobalCone.lean`: it builds a global cone datum out of an affine obstruction datum and shows that the resulting global virtual class is the affine one. Since Mathlib gives no algebra structure on the sections of `𝒪_{Spec B}` over an open subset, the file first installs `GlobalConeAffine.specSectionsAlgebra` and then constructs, for any `B`-algebra `S`, the quasi-coherent algebra `GlobalConeAffine.constData B S` on `Spec B` with ring `Γ(U) ⊗_B S` over an affine open `U`, whose base-change squares are `Algebra.TensorProduct.cancelBaseChange`, together with the morphisms `constHom` (induced by a `B`-algebra map, with surjective components when the map is surjective) and `constAug` (induced by an augmentation `S → B`). Applying this to `S = Sym(E⁻¹)` and to `S = ResolvedCone.ring φ` gives `GlobalConeAffine.bundleData φ`, a `VectorBundleTotalSpace.BundleData` on `Spec (R ⧸ I)` with the single chart `⊤` and the polynomial trivialisation `trivChart` induced by `VirtualClass.trivialization φ` (the augmentation is evaluation at the origin, `constAug_eq_aeval_trivChart`), and `GlobalConeAffine.globalConeData φ`, a `GlobalCone.GlobalConeData` for the constant family of affine models `fun _ ↦ φ`; `isNoetherianRing_cone_ring` and the instance `isLocallyNoetherian_coneScheme` discharge the local Noetherianity that the graded part of `GlobalCone.lean` assumes. Because the chart is `⊤`, the chart morphism `GlobalConeData.bundleChartι` is an isomorphism (`isIso_bundleChartι`, from `isIso_affineι_top`), so restriction and extension of rational Chow classes along it are mutually inverse (`chartRestrict`, `chartExtend`, proved from the new general lemma `openImmersionPullback_comp_eq_self`); `chartRestrict_coneClassAt` then identifies the global cone class with `VirtualClass.resolvedConeClass φ`, and `chowPullback` — the affine `VirtualClass.bundlePullback φ` transported along the chart isomorphism — is proved injective (`chowPullback_injective`, from `OverField.hinjOf`) and to send the affine virtual class to the global cone class (`chowPullback_virtualClass`), so that the three unproved hypotheses `pull`, `hinj`, `hmem` of `GlobalCone.virtualClassOf` are all supplied in this case and `virtualClassOf_eq_virtualClass` proves that the global virtual class equals `OverField.virtualClass φ dimX dimC RX RC`. The one hypothesis carried throughout is `hdim`, the compatibility of the two certified dimension functions, which is the same gap that every restriction statement in the repository carries. Missing: nothing here identifies `chowPullback` with a geometrically defined flat pullback along `BundleData.proj` (no Chow-level global bundle pullback exists in the repository), and the global *cycle* pullback of `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackGlobal.lean` is not compared with the affine one, because that comparison needs the compatibility between `GlobalConeData.chartBundle` and `BundleData.triv` which `GlobalConeData` deliberately does not impose. `VirtualFundamentalClass/GlobalVirtualClass.lean` defines the virtual fundamental class of a general scheme with the genuine global Chow-group flat pullback. `GlobalVirtualClass.bundlePullback 𝓔 dimX dimE hshift i RX RE` is `BundleOverSubscheme.chowPullbackBundleGlobal` (report G2), the descent to rational equivalence of the flat pullback along the projection of the total space of a global vector bundle, and `GlobalVirtualClass.virtualClass 𝒞 dimX dimE hshift i RX RE hmem` is the preimage of the global cone class `GlobalCone.GlobalConeData.coneClassAt` under it, characterised by `bundlePullback_virtualClass` (`π^*[X]^vir = [C(E)]`), `virtualClass_unique`, `eq_virtualClass_of_pullback_eq` and `existsUnique_virtualClass`; the membership `hmem` of the cone class in the range of `π^*` and (for uniqueness) the injectivity `hinj` of `π^*` remain explicit hypotheses, being the global homotopy property of Chow groups, which this repository proves only over an affine base. The file first closes the compatibility that reports F and J left open: `GlobalVirtualClass.bundleChartι_eq` shows that the chart immersion `GlobalConeData.bundleChartι j` built from `chartBundle j` and the chart immersion `BundleData.chartι j` built from the polynomial trivialisation `triv j` differ exactly by `Spec` of the ring isomorphism `chartRingEquiv 𝒞 j : MvPolynomial ι Γ(X, U_j) ≃+* ResolvedCone.bundleRing (φ j)`, which lets F's cone-cycle chart comparison and G2's flat-pullback chart comparison be composed. The resulting main theorem `chartPullback_chartRestrict_virtualClass` proves, unconditionally in the chart dimension hypotheses `hU`, `hP`, `hC`, that on every chart the restriction of the global virtual class satisfies exactly the equation defining the affine virtual class of the chart model, namely `chartPullback (α|_{U_j}) = VirtualClass.resolvedConeClassAt (φ j) dimC i RC`, and `chartRestrict_virtualClass_unique` makes this a characterisation whenever the chart pullback is injective. With report L's canonical dimension functions, `virtualClassFT` gives the virtual class of a scheme locally of finite type over a field with **no** dimension hypothesis at all (`hshift` is `FiniteTypeDimension.dimensionFunction_bundlePoint`), leaving only `hmem`. Finally `virtualClass_eq_overFieldVirtualClass` proves that for report J's single-chart datum the global class is `OverField.virtualClass φ`, conditionally on the agreement `hmatch` of the geometric global pullback with J's transported affine pullback. Missing: `hmem` and `hinj` (the global homotopy property), the identification of `chartPullback` with the affine `VectorBundle.chowPullbackBundle (trivialization (φ j))` over `Spec (R j ⧸ I j)` (a base transport along `GlobalConeData.chartBase j`), and the unconditional form of `hmatch`. (Round 18.) Construction of the global cone datum from local data and the resulting unconditional virtual class. `VirtualFundamentalClass/ConeGluing.lean`: `LocalConeData 𝓔 φ` (affine obstruction models on the charts, chart identifications of base and bundle rings, and the overlap hypothesis `compat` that the resolved-cone ideals of two charts agree after extension to every affine open of the overlap); the glued cone is the closed subscheme of the total space with ideal sheaf `Scheme.Hom.ker (Sigma.desc coneChartι)`, and `kerIdeal_chart` identifies its ideal on every chart with the affine resolved-cone ideal (for finitely many charts over a quasi-separated base). `ConeGluingGlobal.lean`: `LocalConeData.toGlobalConeData : GlobalConeData 𝓔 φ` (cone algebra `AlgebraData.ofAffineHom`, inclusion from the closed immersion, chart identifications from `kerIdeal_chart`), its locally Noetherian instance, and the affine consistency `coneCycleAt_toGlobalConeData_eq_resolvedConeCycleAt`. `ConeGluingCompatible.lean`: `LocalEmbeddingData` (flat formally étale base changes of the affine models to the affine opens of the overlaps, compatible bundle-ring identifications, and a transition datum identifying the two base-changed resolved-cone ideals) with `LocalEmbeddingData.compat` derived from `LocalisationCone.ideal_map_bundleRingEquiv`, hence `toLocalConeData`; the transition datum stays a field because the repository has no transport of obstruction data across two different ambient presentations. `GlobalVirtualClassSurjective.lean`: `coneClass_mem_range`/`coneClassFT_mem_range` discharge the membership hypothesis of round 17 from the global surjectivity, giving `virtualClass'` and `virtualClassFT' f 𝒞 i RX RE`, the virtual class of a compact scheme locally of finite type over a field with a global cone datum and no hypothesis at all, characterised by `π^*[X]^vir = [C(E)]` and unique whenever `π^*` is injective. `GlobalVirtualClassUnconditional.lean`: for an obstruction bundle with a global trivialisation the injectivity is a theorem over an infinite field, so `[X]^vir` is the unique class with `π^*[X]^vir = [C(E)]` (`virtualClassFT'_unique_of_globalTrivialisation'`, `existsUnique_…`), and `LocalConeData.virtualClassFT`/`LocalEmbeddingData.virtualClassFT` give the virtual class directly from local embedding data. Uniqueness for a non-trivial obstruction bundle (Fulton 3.3(a)) remains the one open hypothesis, and Deligne–Mumford stacks are not treated. Resolution independence at the global level for a fixed obstruction bundle (issue #70): `VirtualFundamentalClass/ConeGluingIndependence.lean` defines `ChartIso 𝒞 𝒞'` (chart-wise isomorphisms of two-term complexes intertwining the affine obstruction data and the bundle-chart embeddings of two `GlobalConeData` on the same bundle `𝓔`, pure data and compatibility equations) and proves that the glued cone cycles and classes agree in every grading (`coneCycle_eq`, `coneClassAt_eq`, via cycle extension along a cover by open immersions, `ext_of_isOpenImmersion_cover`), hence for a globally trivialised bundle over an infinite field the unconditional virtual class agrees for chart-isomorphic resolutions (`virtualClassFT'_eq`). Still open: the chart-wise quasi-isomorphism variant, comparison across different bundles (needs a direct sum of `BundleData`), invariance under stack equivalence, and the definition from mere existence of a resolution, which needs injectivity of `π^*` for non-trivial bundles (issue #46). `VirtualFundamentalClass/ConeGluingQuasiIso.lean` adds the quasi-isomorphism variant: a quasi-isomorphism of two-term complexes that is bijective in degree one is bijective in degree zero as well (`bijective_degreeZero_of_isQuasiIsomorphism_of_bijective_degreeOne`), so chart-wise quasi-isomorphisms bijective in degree one (`ChartQuasiIso`) reduce to `ChartIso` and give `coneClassAt_eq_of_quasiIso` and `virtualClassFT'_eq_of_quasiIso`; quasi-isomorphisms changing the degree-one term live on different bundles and need a direct sum of `BundleData`. Towards the virtual degree (issue #69): `IntersectionTheory/ZeroCycleDegree.lean` defines the residue degree `[κ(x):k]` of a point of a scheme locally of finite type over a field, proves it positive at closed points (Zariski's lemma) and assembles the degree map `degree : Z_0(X) → ℚ` on rational zero-cycles of a quasi-compact `X` with `degree_single`; `IntersectionTheory/AffineDegreeFormula.lean` proves Fulton's affine length formula `∑_m [κ(m):k]·ord_{A_m}(a) = dim_k(A/(a))` for a finitely generated one-dimensional domain `A` over `k` (`affineLengthFormula`); and the order-of-vanishing calculus behind proper pushforward of principal divisors is proved in `Algebra/OrderFiniteExtension.lean` (Fulton Lemma A.2.3 in general: `length_A(M/aM) = n·ord_A(a)` for a finitely generated torsion-free `M` over a Noetherian domain of dimension `≤ 1`, via the Euler characteristic `chiCoker - chiKer`), `Algebra/OrderSemilocal.lean` (the semilocal length formula `length_A N = ∑_q [κ(q):κ_A]·length_{B_q} N_q` for a module-finite `A`-algebra `B` and a finite-length `B`-module `N`, `OrderSemilocal.length_eq_finsum`, by devissage) and `Algebra/OrderBirational.lean` (Fulton Example A.3.1: `ord_A(a) = ∑_q [κ(q):κ_A]·ord_{B_q}(a)` for a finite birational extension `B` of `A`, `OrderBirational.ord_eq_finsum`). `IntersectionTheory/AffineDegreeScheme.lean` translates the affine formula to the degree map on `Spec A`: `degreeCycle_principalCycle_eq_finrank` gives `deg (div a) = dim_k(A/(a))` for a nonzero regular function on an integral affine curve, `degreeCycle_map_eq_of_isClosedImmersion` gives invariance of the degree under pushforward along a closed immersion, and closed immersions are proved to induce isomorphisms on residue fields (`instIsIso_residueFieldMap_of_isClosedImmersion`). Still open: the points at infinity (`deg (div r) = 0` on a proper curve needs the finite map to `ℙ¹` or an equivalent properness input), hence `killsGradedDivisor` and an instance of `ProperDegree`, and the length formula for the degree of a proper zero-dimensional scheme. The first proper case is settled: `ProjectiveLine.lean` constructs `ℙ¹_k` as the gluing of two affine lines along `Spec k[t,t⁻¹]` (a colimit of a span of open immersions) and proves it compact, integral and locally Noetherian with structure morphism locally of finite type and first chart the complement of the point at infinity; `IntersectionTheory/ProjectiveLineDegree.lean` proves `degreeCycle_principalCycle_eq_zero`, the degree of the principal cycle of every nonzero rational function on `ℙ¹_k` is zero, by splitting the degree sum into the affine chart (`deg p − deg q` by the affine length formula) and the point at infinity (`deg q − deg p` through the transition `t ↦ t⁻¹` and `Polynomial.reverse`), with functoriality of the function-field pullback along dominant morphisms and invariance of the residue degree under open immersions along the way. The general proper curve (Fulton Prop. 1.4, pushforward along a finite map to `ℙ¹`) remains open. `Algebra/OrderDeterminant.lean` proves Fulton's Lemma A.2.6: for a Noetherian domain `A` of Krull dimension `≤ 1` and a square matrix `M` with `det M ≠ 0`, the length of the cokernel of `M` is `ord_A(det M)` (`cokerLength_eq_ord_det`, through a subtraction-free replacement of the lattice index run along Mathlib's Gaussian-elimination induction over the fraction field), hence the norm formula `length_A(B/bB) = ord_A(N_{B/A}(b))` for a finite free `A`-algebra `B` (`length_quotient_span_eq_ord_norm`) and its semilocal form `∑_q [κ(q):κ_A]·ord_{B_q}(b) = ord_A(N(b))` (`finsum_summand_eq_ord_norm`), the pushforward of the divisor of `b` to the divisor of its norm; the finite torsion-free non-free case needs a base-changed norm. `IntersectionTheory/NormPushforward.lean` proves Fulton's Proposition 1.4 for a finite free extension of one-dimensional domains: the pushforward of the principal cycle of `b` along `Spec B → Spec A` is the principal cycle of `Algebra.norm A b` (`map_principalCycle_eq_principalCycle_norm`, `properPushforward_principalCycleOf`, comparing coefficients at closed points through `finsum_summand_eq_ord_norm`, the fibre over `m` being the maximal spectrum of `B_m`), with the degree compatibility `degreeCycle (div b) = degreeCycle (div (norm b))` over `k` (`degreeCycle_principalCycleOf_norm`, from residue-degree multiplicativity); `Spec B → Spec A` is registered as finite hence proper. With the vanishing on `ℙ¹`, the theorem `deg (div r) = 0` for a regular proper curve now needs only the finite morphism to `ℙ¹` defined by `r`. `Curves/RationalFunctionToProjectiveLine.lean` constructs the domain of definition `regularLocus r` of a rational function on an integral scheme with the regular function it defines there (`regularSection`, by the sheaf condition), proves `regularLocus r ⊔ regularLocus r⁻¹ = ⊤` when the local rings are valuation rings, and builds the morphism `toProjectiveLine : W ⟶ ℙ¹_k` by gluing the two chart morphisms along `Spec k[t,t⁻¹]`, a morphism of `k`-schemes pulling the coordinate back to `r`, dominant for `r` transcendental, proper and locally of finite type, and finite by Zariski's main theorem (`isFinite_toProjectiveLine`) given separatedness of `ℙ¹_k` and the one-dimensionality of `W` as explicit hypotheses. `Curves/ProperCurveTopology.lean` proves `ℙ¹_k` separated over `k` (`ProjectiveLine.instIsSeparatedStructureMap`, by the affine-diagonal criterion `isClosedImmersion_pullback_snd_diagonal` and surjectivity of `k[t] ⊗ k[s] → k[t,t⁻¹]`) and the one-dimensionality package for curves (non-generic points closed when the generic point has height one, proper closed subsets finite, local rings of a regular one-dimensional integral scheme are valuation rings), bundled as `RegularProperCurve k`, which supplies exactly the hypotheses of `isFinite_toProjectiveLine`. `IntersectionTheory/ProperCurveDegree.lean` reduces the vanishing of the degree of a principal divisor on a proper curve to one numerical comparison: the degree of a principal cycle splits over an open and its complement, for two opens covering `W` on which `r` and `r⁻¹` are regular it is the difference of the two partial degrees (`degreeCycle_principalCycle_eq_sub_finsum`), `r` has order zero on the overlap of the two domains of definition, so on an integral scheme whose local rings are valuation rings `deg (div r) = 0` as soon as the two partial degrees agree (`degreeCycle_principalCycle_eq_zero_of_finsum_eq`); the partial degree over an affine chart is `dim_k (B ⧸ (b))` (`finsum_eq_finrank_of_openImmersion`) and the algebra evaluating both dimensions as `[K(W):k(t)]` is complete. Still open: the construction of the two affine charts from the finite morphism to `ℙ¹` and the localisation comparison of the two chart ranks over `k[t,t⁻¹]`, after which the headline `deg (div r) = 0` on a regular proper curve follows. `IntersectionTheory/ProperCurveDegreeFinal.lean` settles the algebraic case unconditionally (a rational function that is a root of a monic polynomial over `k` lies with its inverse in every valuation-ring local ring, so its principal cycle vanishes: `RegularProperCurve.degreeCycle_principalCycle_eq_zero_of_not_injective`) and, for the transcendental case, constructs the affine charts required: the canonical open immersion `Spec Γ(W,U) ⟶ W` of an affine open is a morphism of `k`-schemes, the function-field pullback along it is the canonical one, so the partial degree over an affine open is the `k`-dimension of the chart fibre (`finsum_eq_finrank_of_isAffineOpen`) and `deg (div r) = 0` as soon as the two domains of definition are affine with equal chart dimension (`degreeCycle_principalCycle_eq_zero_of_isAffineOpen`), the preimages of the charts of `ℙ¹` under the finite morphism being affine (`isAffineOpen_preimage_chart`). Still open for the transcendental case: the identification of the domain of definition of `r` with the preimage of the first chart (only `⊆` proved) and the comparison of the two chart ranks over `k[t,t⁻¹]`. `IntersectionTheory/ProperCurveDegreeTranscendental.lean` closes the geometric half of the transcendental case: the preimages of the two charts of `ℙ¹_k` under the morphism determined by `r` are exactly the domains of definition of `r` and `r⁻¹` (`preimage_chartZero_eq_regularLocus`), hence affine when the morphism is finite; the restriction over the first chart is the chart morphism of `r`, so the first chart ring is a finite `k[t]`-module (`finite_toChartHom`); the overlap is the basic open of the representing section; and the comparison of the two chart dimensions is reduced to localisations (`finrank_eq_of_isLocalizationAway`). `RegularProperCurve.degreeCycle_principalCycle_eq_zero` is unconditional for `r` algebraic over `k` and takes a `ChartPresentation` of the two chart rings for `r` transcendental; its construction from these results needs only `Ring.KrullDimLE 1` of the chart rings (integral over `k[t]`). `IntersectionTheory/ProperCurveDegreeUnconditional.lean` makes the vanishing unconditional: `RegularProperCurve.degreeCycle_principalCycle_eq_zero'` gives `deg (div r) = 0` for every nonzero rational function on a regular proper curve over a field, by constructing the chart presentation from the rings of sections of the two domains of definition (the missing input, `Ring.KrullDimLE 1` of the chart rings, is `krullDimLE_one_of_moduleFinite`: a domain module-finite over a PID has dimension at most one); `degreeCycle_divisor_eq` reduces the well-definedness of `A_0(W) → ℚ` to the dichotomy that an integral closed subscheme of a curve is the curve or a point, not yet formalised. The lci formula is proved in the affine model (`VirtualFundamentalClass/LciFormula.lean`): for the identity obstruction theory `φ = id` on the conormal complex `[I/I² → Ω_{R/k} ⊗ R/I]`, injectivity of the comparison `Sym(I/I²) → gr_I(R)` (quasi-regularity of `I`, supplied by `AffineNormalCone.QuasiregularGenerators`) forces `C(E) = E₁`, so `π^*[X] = [C(E)]` and `[X]^vir = [X]` (`VirtualClass.lci_formula`, and `OverField.lci_formula` over an infinite field with the purity of the dimension function of `X` as the only extra hypothesis). `VirtualFundamentalClass/LciFormulaPure.lean` discharges that hypothesis for a regular sequence in a polynomial ring: quasi-regular generators give a basis of `I/I²` (`QuasiregularGenerators.cotangentBasis`), the virtual dimension is `card σ - c` (`virtualDimension_lciHom_eq`), minimal primes over a regular sequence have height equal to its length (`height_eq_length_of_isWeaklyRegular`), hence `Spec (R⧸I)` is pure of dimension `card σ - c` (`dimensionFunction_eq_of_isMax`), and `OverField.lci_formula_polynomial` states `[X]^vir = [X]` for a regular sequence in `k[x_1,…,x_n]` over an infinite field with no further hypothesis. The smooth case needs "smooth ⇒ locally quasi-regular" (issue #64); the locally free obstruction-bundle formula needs the top Chern class (issue #46); the external product formula and the zero locus of a section of a vector bundle are not treated. |
| 8: functoriality | `VirtualFundamentalClass/Functoriality.lean`, `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackBaseChange.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeCone.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeConeIdeal.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeInvariance.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeObstruction.lean` |   genuine cartesian-square shapes remain active; the chosen equivalence in a cartesian square is required to classify its displayed cone, and isomorphisms of squares satisfy the comparison-face pasting equation; cotangent-triangle compatibility is absent, and records carrying virtual-pullback, product, and graph formulas as fields are inactive; the general theorems remain to be constructed, but the affine-model base change along a smooth projection is now proved (Round 16.) `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackBaseChange.lean` establishes the three base-change compatibilities of the flat pullback along a trivialised affine vector bundle, at the level of cycles, of dimension-graded cycles and of rational Chow groups. `chowPullbackBundle_tower` descends the cycle-level `VectorBundle.pullbackBundle_tower` to Chow groups for a tower `R → R' → A` of trivialised bundles; the degree bookkeeping `i + r₂ + r₁ = i + r` is an explicit hypothesis and the induced change of the Chow group's type is handled by the new transport equivalences `cyclesDegreeCongr` and `chowDegreeCongr`, whose compatibility with the quotient map is `chowDegreeCongr_quotientMap`. For a closed immersion `Spec (A ⧸ K) ⟶ Spec A` and a trivial rank-`τ` bundle, `pullbackBundle_map_baseImm`, `flatPullbackBundle_properPushforward` and `chowPullbackBundle_closedImmersionPushforward` prove the base-change square `π^* ∘ ι_* = ι'_* ∘ π'^*` on cycles, graded cycles and Chow groups, the key input being the ideal computation `comap_map_map_algebraMap` identifying the restricted bundle with the preimage of the closed subscheme on the points which carry a flat pullback. Finally `polyTrivialization` constructs the trivialisation of the base-changed bundle `Spec (MvPolynomial τ A) ⟶ Spec (MvPolynomial τ R)` induced by `e : A ≃ₐ[R] MvPolynomial ι R`, `pullbackBundle_polyBaseChange` proves that the two composites `Spec R → Spec (MvPolynomial τ A)` induce the same pullback of cycles, and `zeroSectionGysin'_chowPullbackBundle` deduces that the zero-section Gysin isomorphism commutes with polynomial base change. All proofs are unconditional apart from the homogeneity and injectivity hypotheses `hhom`, `hinj` which are the inputs of `VectorBundle.zeroSectionGysin'` itself; nothing in the file is a `sorry` or a new axiom. `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeCone.lean` sets up the base change of an affine obstruction datum along the projection `Spec B × 𝔸^τ → Spec B` in the polynomial model of `Cones/NormalConeBaseChange.lean`: with `R = k[x_σ]`, `A' = k[y_τ]`, `R' = A'[x_σ]`, `I ⊆ R` and `I' = I·R'`, the file constructs the algebra structure `BaseChangeCone.algebraBaseExt` making `B' = R'/I'` a `B = R/I`-algebra and proves `BaseChangeCone.baseAlgEquiv : B' ≃ₐ[B] B[y_τ]`, whence `BaseChangeCone.free_baseExt` and `BaseChangeCone.flat_baseExt`. It then builds the two natural comparison maps `BaseChangeCone.cotangentComparison : I/I² →ₗ[B] I'/I'²` (from `Ideal.mapCotangent`) and `BaseChangeCone.kaehlerComparison : B ⊗_R Ω[R⁄k] →ₗ[B] B' ⊗_{R'} Ω[R'⁄A']` (from `KaehlerDifferential.map`), proves the naturality square `BaseChangeCone.kaehlerComparison_conormalMap` for the conormal maps, and assembles `BaseChangeCone.baseChangeHom φ : Hom (E.baseChange B') (conormalComplex A' R' I')` for every `φ : Hom E (conormalComplex k R I)`. On the cone side it identifies the coordinate ring of the bundle `E₁` after base change, `BaseChangeCone.bundleRingEquiv : Sym_{B'}(B' ⊗_B E⁻¹) ≃ₐ[B'] B' ⊗_B Sym_B(E⁻¹)` (an instance of `GradedCone.baseChangeEquiv`), and its trivialised form `BaseChangeCone.polyBundleRingEquiv`, a ring isomorphism onto `MvPolynomial τ (ResolvedCone.bundleRing φ)` computed on generators by `polyBundleRingEquiv_ι_one_tmul`. What is still missing is the comparison of the resolved-cone *ideals*: that needs the base change `gr_{I'}(R') ≃ B' ⊗_B gr_I(R)` of the associated graded ring (only `NormalConeBaseChange.grBaseChangeEquiv : R' ⊗_R gr_I(R) ≃ gr_{I'}(R')` is available), the resulting identification of `ResolvedCone.productRing` and `ResolvedCone.productMap`, and the flat-base-change computation of the kernel; consequently `ringEquiv`, `ringEquiv_mk` and the transfer of `PicardCriteria.IsObstructionTheory` along `baseChangeHom` are not proved. `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeConeIdeal.lean` completes the comparison begun in `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeCone.lean` between the resolved cone of an affine obstruction datum `φ` over `Spec B` and the resolved cone of its base change `baseChangeHom τ I φ` over `Spec B × 𝔸^τ`. It first trivialises the associated graded ring of the extended ideal, `BaseChangeCone.grPolyEquiv : gr_{I'}(R') ≃+* (gr_I R)[y_τ]` and its `B`-algebra form `BaseChangeCone.grPolyTensorEquiv`, by combining `NormalConeBaseChange.grBaseChangeEquiv` with the variable swap `MvPolynomial.commAlgEquiv` and `MvPolynomial.algebraTensorAlgEquiv`; this route avoids any tensor product over `B` of rings that are only `B'`-algebras. From it, `BaseChangeCone.productRingEquiv` identifies `gr_{I'}(R') ⊗_{B'} Sym_{B'}(B' ⊗_B E⁰)` with the polynomial ring `(gr_I(R) ⊗_B Sym_B(E⁰))[y_τ]`, and `BaseChangeCone.productRingEquiv_productMap` proves the commuting square with the two product maps, checked on the generators of the symmetric algebra using the naturality `BaseChangeCone.grMap_conormalToAssociatedGraded` of `I/I² → gr_I(R)` under base change. Combining the square with `MvPolynomial.ker_map` yields `BaseChangeCone.ideal_map_polyBundleRingEquiv` and `BaseChangeCone.ideal_map_bundleRingEquiv`: the ideal of the base-changed resolved cone is the extension of the ideal of `C(E)`; no separate flat-base-change kernel lemma is needed, as the flatness is already absorbed in `grBaseChangeEquiv`. Hence `BaseChangeCone.ringEquiv : ResolvedCone.ring (baseChangeHom τ I φ) ≃+* MvPolynomial τ (ResolvedCone.ring φ)` with `BaseChangeCone.ringEquiv_mk` and `BaseChangeCone.ringEquiv_comp_mk`, the ring-level commuting square of the closed immersions `C(E)' ↪ E₁'` and `C(E) ↪ E₁` in the form required by `IntersectionTheory/BundlePullbackBaseChange.lean`. Still missing from the base-change package is the transfer of `PicardCriteria.IsObstructionTheory` along `baseChangeHom` (F2a's third open item), and the scheme-level/Chow-level assembly of the square with the intersection-theoretic lemmas of F2b. `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeInvariance.lean` assembles Layer 8 in the affine model: the virtual class is compatible with the base change along the smooth projection `X × 𝔸^τ → X` (Behrend–Fantechi, Proposition 7.2). With the notation of `BaseChangeCone.lean`, it first upgrades `BaseChangeCone.polyBundleRingEquiv` to an isomorphism of `B`-algebras `BaseChangeInvariance.polyBundleAlgEquiv : Sym_{B'}(B' ⊗_B E⁻¹) ≃ₐ[B] (Sym_B E⁻¹)[y_τ]`, whence the isomorphisms of schemes `bundleSpaceIsoPoly : E₁' ≅ E₁ × 𝔸^τ` and `coneSchemeIsoPoly : C(E) × 𝔸^τ ≅ C(E)'`, and constructs the trivialisation `trivializationBaseChange` of `E₁'` over `X'` with the same index type as a given trivialisation `e` of `E₁` over `X`, together with `totalTrivializationBaseChange`, its trivialisation as a bundle over `X`. `bundleImm_comp_specPolyBundle` is the scheme-level form of `BaseChangeCone.ringEquiv_comp_mk`, the commuting square of the two closed immersions; combined with `VirtualClass.map_fundamentalCycle_of_isIso` and F2b's `PullbackBaseChange.pullbackBundle_map_baseImm_fundamentalCycle` (whose hypothesis is discharged by `VirtualClass.pullbackBundle_fundamentalCycle`) it gives the cycle-level theorem `flatPullbackOpen_flatPullbackBundle_resolvedConeCycleAt`: the flat pullback of the resolved-cone cycle `[C(E)]` along the trivial bundle `E₁ × 𝔸^τ → E₁` is, through `bundleSpaceIsoPoly`, the resolved-cone cycle `[C(E)']` of the base-changed datum, in every degree. On the other side `pullbackBundle_baseChange_square` proves, from `VectorBundle.pullbackBundle_tower` applied to the two towers `B → B' → Sym_{B'}(B' ⊗_B E⁻¹)` and `B → Sym_B E⁻¹ → (Sym_B E⁻¹)[y_τ]` and from `AlgebraicCycle.pullbackBundle_algEquiv`, that the two ways of pulling a cycle of `X` back to `E₁'` agree; `chowPullbackBundle_baseChange_square` descends this through rational equivalence. The main theorem `virtualClassAt_baseChangeHom` then follows by applying the injective `chowPullbackBundle` of the bundle `E₁' → X'`, and `relativeVirtualClassAt_baseChangeHom` restates it in the canonical degree as the relative virtual class of `ψ` in relative dimension `Nat.card τ` (`DegreeOneSummand.relativeVirtualClassAt`), the virtual dimension being unchanged (`virtualDimension_baseChangeHom`). What is still missing from the base-change package is the transfer of `PicardCriteria.IsObstructionTheory` along `baseChangeHom` (F2a's open item, untouched here) and any transport of the rational-equivalence systems across the *propositional* equality `virtualDimension ψ = virtualDimension φ`. (Round 17.) `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeObstruction.lean` transfers the Behrend–Fantechi condition `PicardCriteria.IsObstructionTheory` along the two base changes used by the globalisation. On the abstract side it proves that composing with a quasi-isomorphism *reflects* the property (`isObstructionTheory_of_comp_quasiIso`, `isObstructionTheory_of_quasiIso_comp`) and that a faithfully flat base change reflects it as well (`isObstructionTheory_of_baseChange`, `isObstructionTheory_baseChange_iff`), by combining `PicardCriteria.isObstructionTheory_iff_exact_cone` with `Module.FaithfullyFlat.lTensor_exact_iff_exact`; the forward direction is the already unconditional `PicardCriteria.IsObstructionTheory.baseChange`. For the flat formally étale base change `R → R'` of `VirtualFundamentalClass/LocalisationCone.lean` the file packages `LocalisationCone.cotangentComparison` and `LocalisationCone.kaehlerComparison` into a chain map `BaseChangeObstruction.LocalisationCone.comparisonHom` and shows it is a quasi-isomorphism, so that `LocalisationCone.baseChangeHom I R' φ` is (definitionally) `comparisonHom ∘ φ.baseChange B'`; this gives `LocalisationCone.isObstructionTheory_baseChangeHom` and, when `R'` is faithfully flat over `R`, the equivalence `LocalisationCone.isObstructionTheory_baseChangeHom_iff` — which closes the corresponding open item of round 17's task D. For the polynomial base change `Spec B × 𝔸^τ → Spec B` of round 16 the file additionally proves the two bijectivity statements that `VirtualFundamentalClass/BaseChangeCone.lean` had left open, `Poly.bijective_baseChangeZero_id` (through the flat base change `Poly.cotangentEquiv : B' ⊗_B I/I² ≃ I'/I'²` built from `AffineNormalCone.quotTensorIdealEquiv` and `AffineNormalCone.idealTensorEquiv`) and `Poly.bijective_baseChangeOne_id` (both sides are free `B'`-modules on `KaehlerDifferential.mvPolynomialBasis`), whence `Poly.isObstructionTheory_baseChangeHom` and, for `B` nontrivial, `Poly.isObstructionTheory_baseChangeHom_iff`. Nothing here is conditional on an unproved hypothesis, and nothing here is at scheme level: all statements are about two-term complexes of modules over one ring. |
| 9: relative theory | `IntrinsicNormalCone/Relative.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/Relative*.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/RelativeAbsolute.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/RelativeAbsoluteObstruction.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/RelativeVirtualClassBaseChange.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/RelativeHyperplaneObstruction.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/RelativeHyperplaneReindex.lean`, `VirtualFundamentalClass/RelativeAbsoluteTriangle.lean` |  the relative diagonal used in the relative-DM predicate is constructed as the bilimit lift of the identity cone, but the former relative cone, resolved class, base-change, independence, and formula packages are inactive; the general relative theory remains to be built, but the affine-model comparison of relative and absolute obstruction theories over a smooth polynomial base is now proved (Round 16.) `VirtualFundamentalClass/RelativeAbsolute.lean` formalises Behrend–Fantechi's passage from an obstruction theory relative to a smooth base `Y = 𝔸^τ` to an absolute one, in the affine polynomial model `R = MvPolynomial (σ ⊕ τ) k`, `X = Spec (R ⧸ I)`. The concrete relative conormal complex `relConormalComplex I = [I/I² → (σ → R/I)]` is built from the coordinate functionals `sigmaPart I` of the tangent basis of `Cones/NormalConeAction.lean`, whose values on conormal classes are the partial derivatives (`sigmaPart_conormalMap_toCotangent`, `tauPart_conormalMap_toCotangent`), and a tangent vector is recovered from its two coordinate blocks (`sigmaLift_sigmaPart_add_tauLift_tauPart`). A relative datum `φ : E ⟶ relConormalComplex I` is turned into the absolute datum `absHom φ : absComplex φ ⟶ conormalComplex k R I` with `(absComplex φ).degreeOne = E.degreeOne × (τ → R/I)`. The main theorem `ideal_absHom` proves `ResolvedCone.ideal (absHom φ) = ideal' φ`: the resolved cone of the absolute datum is cut out by the *relative* datum. It is proved by factoring the coordinate-ring map as `ResolvedCone.productMap (absHom φ) = (compareMap φ).comp (relProductMap φ)` (`productMap_absHom`) through the injective comparison `compareMap φ` built from the tangent coaction `act I` translated by the tautological `τ`-directions; injectivity comes from the explicit retraction `compareRetraction φ` together with the counit law `NormalConeAction.act_zero`. The consequences `resolvedConeCycleAt_absHom` and `virtualClassAt_absHom` identify the resolved-cone cycle and the degree-generic virtual class of `absHom φ` with the relative cycle `relConeCycle φ` and the relative virtual class `relativeVirtualClass φ`, and `virtualDimension_absHom` records the shift of the virtual dimension by `Nat.card τ = dim Y`.
